# Decisions

Format: ID, decision, one-line rationale. `Default` = chosen by Claude Code because the owner said "default the rest"; the owner may overturn it.

## Scope and ownership
| ID | Decision | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-01 | Separate codebase in `HarnessDesignStudio/`, separate from upstream WireViz (GPL-3.0). No WireViz source is copied or imported; WireViz is only an export target (YAML). Contributors must not paste WireViz code. | Owner decision. (The licence reason, "keeps new code free of GPL", is superseded by D-205; the word "clean-room" was retired, D-211.) |
| D-02 | Default: the tool is internal and proprietary for now. Dependencies stay permissive or LGPL so open-sourcing later remains possible. Licence file for `HarnessDesignStudio/` is TBD by owner. | Open question "licence and ownership" unanswered. |
| D-03 | Default: docs live in `HarnessDesignStudio/docs/` (not repo-root `docs/`, which holds WireViz docs). | Avoids mixing the upstream WireViz docs with the docs of this project. |

## Open decisions from the spec (all defaulted)
| ID | Question | Default | Needs owner input? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-10 | Harness boundary rule | One harness per unit-connector pair, merged when both ends share a routing zone and merge rules allow; rule set lives in project config. | Yes, before M3. |
| D-11 | Standards baseline | No standard's numbers in code. `config/derating.json`, `config/emc.json` ship with placeholder values labelled `PLACEHOLDER: engineer must fill`. | Yes: someone must supply real derating, current and EMC values. |
| D-12 | Approved parts list | Library has `approval` field per part; importer for a generic CSV. EPPL/DCL-specific format deferred. | Only if a specific format is required. |
| D-13 | Mechanical lengths | Manual entry plus generic CSV import (harness ID, segment, length mm). No CAD-specific integration. | If a CAD tool is mandated. |
| D-14 | Operating systems | **Superseded by D-110, and D-110 by D-149 (narrowed by D-168):** Ubuntu 24.04 and newer and Windows 10 and 11. (Was: Windows 10/11 and Linux targets.) | Decided by the owner. |
| D-15 | Title block / drafting standard | Generic configurable title block (fields in spec). Template is a config file, replaceable later. | Yes, if a company format exists. |
| D-16 | Approval workflow | Status field (draft / in review / released) plus change log and baselines. No PLM integration. | Only if PLM is required. |
| D-17 | 3D routing | Out of scope. 2D routing segments only. | Confirm. |
| D-18 | Formboard drawings | Later: not in M0 to M7; revisit after M5. | Confirm. |
| D-19 | Security accreditation | CycloneDX JSON SBOM; code signing hooks in the build script but disabled until a certificate is provided. | Yes, if IT requires something else. |
| D-20 | Formal qualification (ECSS-E-ST-40C / Q-ST-80C) | Assume not required, but requirement IDs (`REQ-xxx`) are used in `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` and tests reference them so traceability can be added cheaply. | Yes, must be confirmed early; it changes process overhead. |

## Technology (see ARCHITECTURE.md for the comparison)
| ID | Decision | Licence | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-30 | Python 3.12+ (3.13 is installed in dev environment) | PSF | Matches strong default in spec. |
| D-31 | PySide6 (Qt) for GUI | LGPL-3.0 | Mature canvas (QGraphicsView), offscreen rendering for UX screenshots, pytest-qt. LGPL obligations: dynamic linking, ship licence text. |
| D-32 | pydantic v2 for model validation and JSON Schema export | MIT | Strict validation and schema generation from one source. |
| D-33 | Project files: JSON, sorted keys, 2-space indent, `\n` endings, UTF-8 | stdlib | Diffable, no extra dependency. The WireViz-style YAML is written by the tool itself (PyYAML is not used, D-94). |
| D-34 | openpyxl for XLSX | MIT | Pure Python, no network. |
| D-35 | **Not used (D-94):** the tool writes SVG and PDF itself; reportlab is not a dependency. (Was: reportlab for PDF.) | - | Vector PDF with embedded bundled TTF fonts; no system font dependency. To be re-checked in M5 (fallback: Qt QPdfWriter in GUI layer only). |
| D-36 | Fonts: Inter and JetBrains Mono (SIL OFL) bundled; icon set: Lucide (ISC) bundled as SVG. Final choice made in docs/UX.md. | OFL / ISC | No CDN, permissive. |
| D-37 | Dev tools: pytest, hypothesis, pytest-qt, pytest-cov, mypy (strict), ruff, cyclonedx-bom, pip-licenses | MIT/BSD/Apache | Spec testing and SBOM needs. Dev-only, not shipped. |
| D-38 | Packaging: PyInstaller (one-folder build). **The Windows part was dropped by D-110 and is back with D-149.** | GPL w/ bootloader exception (allows closed apps) / Inno Setup licence | Standard offline packaging. Build must be run per OS in CI. |
| D-39 | CLI: stdlib `argparse` | stdlib | Fewer dependencies in the headless core. |
| D-40 | Package layout `HarnessDesignStudio/src/harness_design_studio/{core,cli,gui}`; core never imports gui or network modules (enforced by test). | n/a | Spec architecture rule. |

## M0 additions
| ID | Decision | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-41 | Build backend setuptools 84.0.0 (MIT), runtime pins: pydantic 2.13.5 (MIT), PySide6-Essentials 6.11.2 (LGPL-3.0 OR GPL), shiboken6 6.11.2 (same). | PySide6-Essentials avoids the large Addons set; needed so `license = "LicenseRef-Proprietary"` (SPDX string) works. |
| D-42 | Dev-only tools (not shipped): pytest 9.1.1, pytest-cov 7.1.0, pytest-qt 4.5.0, hypothesis 6.168.5 (MPL-2.0), mypy 2.4.0, ruff 0.16.10, cyclonedx-bom 7.5.0 (Apache-2.0), pip-licenses 5.5.5, pyinstaller 6.22.3 (GPL-2.0+ with bootloader exception, permits closed apps). Transitive `pyinstaller-hooks-contrib` is dual Apache/GPL, dev-only. | The licence report checks only the clean runtime env. |
| D-43 | Licence classifier: SPDX `OR` needs any allowed, `AND` and `;` need all allowed, GPL/AGPL without LGPL rejected, unknown rejected. | Conservative allow-list. |
| D-44 | Qt build excludes QtNetwork, WebEngine, WebChannel, WebSockets, QtQml/Quick, QtPdf, QtSvg. QtSvg may be re-enabled in M2 if the canvas needs it. | Offline guarantee and smaller package. |
| D-45 | Coverage gate disabled until M1 (empty core has 0 statements). | Avoids a meaningless failure; enable in M1. |

## M1 additions
| ID | Decision | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-50 | Connector IDs (box and harness) and wire IDs are unique across the whole project. Default naming `UNIT-J01`, `W001-P1`, `W001-001` supports this. | Wires and interfaces can then reference connectors by one ID. Confirm. |
| D-51 | IDs must also be unique ignoring case. | Windows and macOS file systems are case-insensitive; harness IDs are file names. |
| D-52 | Models are immutable pydantic objects with strict types (no coercion); changes use `evolve()` and `commands`. | Cheap undo (store old objects), safe sharing, no silent type conversion of flight data. |
| D-53 | Undo/redo stores operations (put/delete of whole objects), not project snapshots. | Memory stays small at stress size. |
| D-54 | A project with unloadable parts cannot be saved over its folder, only "save as", which also writes the rejected data verbatim. | Never lose data silently; salvage the rest. |
| D-55 | Saves with integrity errors are refused unless explicitly allowed (recovery copies). | Spec: validate on every save. |
| D-56 | No new third-party dependencies in M1 (pydantic only); hypothesis is dev-only. | Keeps the SBOM small. |

## M2 gate additions
| ID | Decision | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-60 | Dev-only dependency: playwright 1.63.0 (Apache-2.0), driving the pre-installed Chromium, to test the clickable prototype. Not shipped. Tests skip when no Chromium is present. | Verifies the prototype journeys automatically. |
| D-61 | Category colours were chosen by a small search that maximises the minimum colour difference under simulated colour blindness, subject to AA contrast on all surfaces; the test locks in dE >= 20. | A hand-picked palette had indistinguishable pairs for protanopia. |
| D-62 | Zones are vertical lanes in the prototype (zone follows where a unit is placed). Free-form zones are an open question in UX.md. | Simplest model that makes "grouped by zone" visible. |
| D-63 | The prototype's harness rule (one harness per interface) and all pin/gauge data are mock. The real rule is D-10. | Prototype only. |

## M2 additions (defaults taken because the owner approved the prototype without answering UX.md section 11)
| ID | Decision | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- |
| D-64 | The editor starts in Guided mode and then remembers the last mode, theme and UI scale per user (local settings file, no design data). | Prototype default; mode never changes data. |
| D-65 | Zones are vertical lanes; their ordered list is stored in `logical/layout.json` and a unit's zone follows the lane it sits in. "Add zone…" appends a lane. | Simplest model that shows "grouped by zone". Free-form zones remain an option. |
| D-66 | The default one-click fix for a cross-strap is "connect to a redundant copy of the nominal end"; waiving with a justification is always offered next to it. | Prototype review: the fix must make engineering sense. |
| D-67 | Fourteen built-in unit templates (computer, power unit, wheel, star tracker, payload, transceiver, pyro unit and later additions). Importing your own template list is deferred. | Enough for the usability tests; needs the owner's list to go further. |
| D-68 | Diagram positions are stored in the project (`layout.json`), use one footprint independent of mode (so switching modes never causes overlap), and are auto-assigned deterministically for units without one. | Layout must be remembered and diffable. |
| D-69 | `Connector.carries` and `Endpoint.auto` are new optional fields; the schema version stays 1 because projects saved by M1 load and behave unchanged. | Additive change; tested with an M1-format fixture. |
| D-70 | Waivers live in `waivers.json` with a mandatory justification of at least 10 characters. | SPEC DRC waiver rule. M4 reuses the store. |
| D-71 | The sample project opens untitled with a banner and no autosave journal until it is saved to a folder. | Rule: no project content outside a project folder. |
| D-72 | Autosave = journal in `<project>/.harness-recovery/`, debounced 1.5 s; restore is offered at open. | Never lose more than a few seconds of work. |
| D-73 | XLSX import uses openpyxl 3.1.5 (MIT) and et_xmlfile 2.0.0 (MIT), read-only, at most 8 MB and 20,000 rows. | SPEC import requirement; permissive licences. |
| D-74 | UI scale 100 to 200% scales fonts and spacing; panels auto-collapse by effective window size; the diagram has its own zoom. | Keeps the canvas usable at 200%. |
| D-75 | "Generate harnesses" is shown but disabled with an explanation until M3. | Honest about what exists. |
| D-76 | Canvas keyboard support: Tab between units and links, Enter selects or picks, Shift+arrows nudge. The interface table is the screen-reader-friendly alternative. Full screen-reader support for the canvas is open (M7). | Qt graphics items have no accessibility tree by default. |
| D-77 | A fully zoomed-out view draws units as plain boxes and links without labels (level of detail), and panels refresh lazily when hidden. | Needed for the 200-unit / 2,000-interface target. |
| D-78 | Generation is plan-then-apply: `plan_generation` is pure and returns operations, a change report and a provenance record; the editor shows the report first and applies it as one undoable step. | Foolproof regeneration; nothing changes before the user agrees. |
| D-79 | Default segmentation (placeholder for D-10): one harness per pair of box connectors. `per_unit_pair` and `per_zone_pair` are selectable in `config/segmentation.json`. Nominal and redundant chains and pyro interfaces never share a harness. | Owner has not answered D-10; the default is the safest to review. |
| D-80 | Wiring rule: an `out` signal of one end goes to the matching `in` signal of the other end (TX+ to RX+), matched by position; passive, bidirectional and unpaired directional signals connect to the same-named signal. | Starter types list signals in matching order; analog and discrete types have one direction only. |
| D-81 | Pin allocation: locked pins never move; power first; twisted pairs on adjacent pins; the power-to-signal gap is configurable (placeholder 0); a still-valid earlier allocation is kept so regeneration produces small diffs. | SPEC pin rules; stable diffs. |
| D-82 | Stable identity: harness IDs come from a monotonic counter in the generation record and are reused by segmentation key; wire IDs by wire key. Released harnesses are frozen; locked wires and pins are kept. Manually drawn harnesses own their interfaces and pins and are never touched. | Regeneration must never destroy reviewed or released work. |
| D-83 | The generation input hash covers everything generation reads and excludes outputs (generated pins, wires), placements and waivers. The record stores it; status is none, current or stale. | Detects out-of-date harnesses without false alarms. |
| D-84 | Wire sizing uses only configured tables (ampacity by AWG, bundle and temperature derating, voltage drop, resistivity). With any value missing the gauge stays "pending" and the plan names what is missing. Mass likewise reports missing library data instead of guessing. | Never invent standards values (D-11 open). |
| D-85 | The verifier (`core/verify.py`) is an independent code path: it recomputes the expected connections from the interfaces and compares them with the wires. It shares only the hash comparison with the generator. | Independent check of the generator's output. |
| D-86 | The generation worker runs in a QThread behind a cancellable progress dialog; cancel changes nothing. The UI is blocked meanwhile, so the project cannot change under the worker. | Responsiveness without data races. |
| D-87 | The design rule check (`core/drc/`) has 22 rules plus the verifier's mismatches. Each rule states what, why and how to fix. Errors cannot be waived; warnings can, with a justification of at least 10 characters; notes cannot. | SPEC DRC requirements. |
| D-88 | A rule that needs a number nobody has entered (derating, grounding concept, EMC pairs, spare pins, category separation) checks nothing and the `unchecked-config` note says so. A clean report is never a pass for a check that could not run. | Placeholders rule; D-11 is still open. |
| D-89 | Shield-unterminated is only reported once a grounding concept exists; before that, "grounding not checked" is shown. Direction conflicts are only judged for interface types that have both outputs and inputs. | Avoids false alarms from placeholder data and one-directional types. |
| D-90 | **Superseded in part by D-128 (a helper process instead of a thread).** Design rules run in a background thread on a cheap copy of the project, 1.2 s after the last edit. Edits never wait for them. Measured at stress size: running the check while editing roughly doubles edit time (GIL), so it starts only after a pause. | The 100 ms edit target; the check takes about 0.8 s at stress size. |
| D-91 | Waivers are applied when findings are displayed, so a waiver takes effect at once without waiting for a new check. The report lists waived findings with justifications and waivers whose finding no longer exists. | SPEC: waivers saved and shown in reports. |
| D-92 | `harness drc DIR` prints the Markdown report and exits 1 if any unwaived error exists. The full DRC report as a formatted deliverable (PDF and so on) belongs to M5. | CI-friendly now, formatted outputs later. |
| D-93 | `GenerationPlan.empty` is true only when the plan holds nothing but the generation record (fixes a gap where a pin-only change looked empty). | Found while testing the one-click regenerate fix. |
| D-94 | Outputs are written by the tool's own small SVG and PDF writers (`core/outputs/canvas.py`) from one drawing description; no PDF or drawing library is added. PDFs are uncompressed with no dates, so equal models give equal bytes and the verifier can read them. The architecture proposal mentioned reportlab; it is not needed. | Offline, deterministic, fewer dependencies. |
| D-95 | One monospaced font (Courier, 600/1000 em advance) for all drawing text so layout uses exact widths. Characters outside Windows-1252 print as "?" in PDF. | No font metrics to guess or bundle. |
| D-96 | Outputs live in `<project>/outputs/` with a manifest (model hash, sha256 per file). Only files listed in the previous manifest are ever deleted; foreign files are left alone. The folder is not part of the model hash. | Safe re-export; stale detection. |
| D-97 | Output verifier v2 re-reads the files and derives what they must contain from the project without the builders; XLSX is read straight from its XML. Export is refused if it fails. | SPEC verifier requirement. |
| D-98 | CSV carries its stamp as a first `# ` comment line; cells starting with `= + - @` (not numbers) get a leading `'`. | Stamp on every artefact; spreadsheet formula injection. |
| D-99 | Title block fields come from `config/titleblock.json`; date, author, checker and approver show "-" until M6. Test limits show "TBD (placeholder)" until `generation.test_*` is set. | Never invent values; D-15 open. |
| D-100 | Drawings are wire-by-wire diagrams with segments, shields and spare pins listed; a geometric branch drawing is not part of M5. | Scope; honest limits in `OUTPUTS.md`. |
| D-101 | The editor runs export in a worker thread with progress and Cancel, checks the result independently and only then writes. The quick stale check compares model hashes; file hashing is for the CLI. | Large projects take tens of seconds; the UI must not freeze. |
| D-102 | Change control is data in the project: `Harness.status/revision/author/checker/approver/released_on`, `baselines/<harness>/<revision>.json` (a frozen snapshot of the harness, the interfaces it carries, their units and the box connectors it mates with) and `changelog.json` (one line per review, release and new revision). Release, new revision and review go through `History`, so they are undoable until saved. | SPEC change-control section. Deviation: `changelog.json` instead of `changelog.jsonl`, so the existing canonical writer and loader are reused. |
| D-103 | A release needs a name, a comment of at least 10 characters, and passes this gate: no verifier or rule errors that belong to the harness, harness plans current, every wire has a gauge and a length, outputs exported from the current design. Unsized wires block a release on purpose: until the derating values (D-11) exist, nothing real can be released. | SPEC: release blocked by verifier errors, stale outputs and DRC errors. |
| D-104 | `content_hash` is the model hash without release bookkeeping (status, who, when, baselines, change log). The release gate compares it with the hash stored in the outputs manifest, so outputs reviewed before a release stay valid for it; after a release the outputs are stale by model hash (the title block must say "released") and `harness release` re-exports them. | Avoids a circle: a release changes status, which would make every release's own outputs stale. |
| D-105 | Locks live in `History.execute` (`vcs/locks.py`): a released harness, the interfaces it carries (type and ends), the box pins it uses and the box connectors it mates with cannot be changed or deleted. The only allowed change to a released harness is its next revision (same content, status draft, new revision letter). Harmless edits (names, notes) elsewhere stay possible. | "Released items cannot be edited without a new revision." |
| D-106 | A released harness that differs from its baseline (edited in a text editor, damaged by a merge) is an unwaivable rule error `released-modified`, also shown by `harness check`. | Post-merge consistency. |
| D-107 | The diff engine flattens snapshots to (kind, id) objects: units, interfaces, box connectors and pins, harnesses, cable connectors and pins, wires, shields, splices, branch points, segments (and parts, interface types, configuration for whole projects). It lists added, removed and changed objects with field changes; `harness compare OLD NEW` diffs two project folders (for example Git checkouts). | "Diff of two baselines lists added/removed/changed objects exactly." |
| D-108 | Revisions are letters (A, B ... Z, AA ...). A regenerated harness that was in review goes back to draft with a note, because its reviewed content changed. Generation's input hash no longer includes harness status (a release must not make the harness plans look outdated). | Review means "this content was checked". |
| D-109 | Who and when are typed or default to the operating-system user name and today's date at the moment of the step; the tool never invents them and generated files contain no clock times. | Deterministic outputs; honest change log. The default to the operating-system user name is withdrawn by D-222 (2026-11-10): the name is typed. |
| D-110 | **Superseded by D-149 (Windows and macOS packages from 1.2.0); narrowed by D-127 (24.04 and newer).** Supported platform: Ubuntu LTS (22.04 and 24.04) on x86-64, per the owner. Windows and RHEL/Rocky are no longer targets (supersedes D-14 and the Windows part of D-38). Packaging is a PyInstaller folder shipped as a `.tar.gz` and a `.deb`; release builds run on Ubuntu 22.04 (glibc 2.35) so they start on both LTS versions. Windows-specific safety code (reserved file names, long paths, case-insensitive IDs) stays because it is harmless and keeps projects portable. | Owner instruction at M7. |
| D-111 | The user guide is Markdown (`docs/guide/USER_GUIDE.md`) converted by a small in-repo tool to one offline HTML page bundled in the app; F1 opens it in the system browser (a local file, no network). A test keeps the HTML in sync and checks that every CLI command and every button it names exists. | SPEC: user guide, offline. |
| D-112 | `docs/RULES.md` is generated from the rule registry and `docs/CONFIG.md` documents every configuration key; tests fail if either is stale or incomplete. | Developer docs that cannot drift. |
| D-113 | Release candidates carry `rcN` in the version (`0.1.0rc1`, Debian `0.1.0~rc1`) until the owner signs off, with open decisions listed in the changelog. | Honest status. |
| D-114 | Integrity results are cached per connector and harness object (immutable) together with a token of everything they are checked against (unit, interface and part IDs, connector objects, duplicate wire IDs). | Keeps edits near the 100 ms target for large generated projects; a regression test proves it never hides a new problem. |
| D-115 | The accessibility audit has an automated part (names on every control, text size at every scale, keyboard reach, canvas announcement; part of the test suite) and a manual part that needs a person with a screen reader (still open). | An audit cannot be fully automated. |
| D-116 | Multi-drop interfaces (more than two units, for example a bus) are still not generated; they are skipped with a warning. Generating them needs an engineering rule I will not guess: daisy chain or star, who owns the stub lengths, which connector carries the through-wiring, and where the splices sit. Owner input needed. | Never invent engineering rules. |
| D-117 | The editor previews drawings by painting the same sheet description that the exporter writes (no second renderer), stamped "preview". The routing sketch is a schematic tree (columns by distance from the first connector), shown only for up to 14 nodes. | What you see is what is exported. |
| D-118 | The Outline tab (a plain Qt tree) is the item-by-item accessible view of the diagram; custom accessibility interfaces for graphics items were not attempted because they are fragile in PySide6 and cannot be tested without a screen reader here. In October 2026 the Outline tab was retired: the project tree (which takes Shift and Ctrl for a multiple selection) and the Interfaces tab are the item-by-item views now (`docs/ux-audit.md`, Build status). | Honest scope. |
| D-119 | Arrange keeps each unit in its zone lane and orders units by the average position of their neighbours in other lanes (four sweeps, starting from the current order), then stacks them with the standard spacing. Deterministic; one undo step. | Tidy diagrams without moving units across zones. |
| D-120 | Owner answers at M9: D-11 (real derating, ampacity, EMC values) and D-12 (approved parts list) will be supplied later and the tool is prepared (`harness config`, `harness import-parts`, `config-invalid` rule); D-13: KiCad is the CAD tool, lengths come in as a table in millimetres (`harness import-lengths`) because KiCad does not route harnesses; D-20 (formal qualification) is not needed. Options for D-10 and D-15 are in `docs/OPEN_DECISIONS.md`. | Owner instruction. |
| D-121 | Hostile or damaged input never crashes the tool: strict parsing, quarantine, size and zip-bomb limits, symlinks ignored, damaged outputs and manifests are findings. Review in `docs/SECURITY.md`, enforced by `tests/test_security.py` and `tests/test_fuzz.py`. | Release hardening (M9). |
| D-122 | The Windows-only process check using ctypes was removed (D-110: Ubuntu only), so the code base imports no foreign-function module; a test scans for banned imports and calls. | Smaller attack surface. |
| D-123 | KiCad is used only for the electronics inside each unit, so it is the source of each unit connector's pinout (which signal is on which pin). Lengths do not come from KiCad. A reader for a KiCad export (netlist or schematic) that creates or updates the unit's box connectors and pins is wanted; it is built against a real export from one of the owner's units. Until then pinouts are entered in the tool. | Owner answers (correcting my first reading, which dropped the pinout use). |
| D-124 | The KiCad reader takes the netlist in KiCad's default S-expression format and in XML (told apart by the first character). The owner's first real file (KiCad 10.0.6) was S-expression, not XML. Whether KiCad deprecates the netlist in favour of the schematic file could not be confirmed (KiCad 9 `kicad-cli` still exports both); `.kicad_sch` is not read. Pins from KiCad are `fixed`; generation never moves them. | Reliable connectivity from KiCad itself, whichever export the owner uses. |
| D-125 | Runtime dependencies are pydantic, openpyxl, defusedxml (XML bomb protection for spreadsheet files), PySide6-Essentials and their requirements annotated-types, pydantic_core, typing_extensions, typing_inspection, et_xmlfile, shiboken6; all MIT, PSF or LGPL-3. The package ships their licence texts (`LICENSES/`), built by `tools/collect_licences.py`; development tools are excluded from the bundle and the build fails if they leak in. | Smaller package, honest SBOM, LGPL obligations met. |
| D-126 | The project lock is taken by every command that writes (GUI and CLI) under a guard, and created atomically; baselines and the change log cannot be deleted or replaced; the design fields of an interface a released harness carries are locked. | October audit. |
| D-127 | **Superseded by D-149 for the platforms; the Ubuntu part stands.** Supported platform: Ubuntu 24.04 and newer on x86-64; Ubuntu 22.04 is no longer a target (narrows D-110). CI tests and builds on 24.04 only, release builds run on 24.04 (Python 3.12 from the archive, no extra package source), and the 22.04 build image became `Dockerfile.build-24.04`. A package built on 24.04 is not promised to start on 22.04 (newer glibc). The Ubuntu 22.04 runner was also the slowest CI machine and caused a timing failure. | Owner instruction, October 2026. |
| D-128 | The design rule check runs in a separate, long-lived helper process (the same program started with `--drc-worker` when packaged, `python -m harness_design_studio.core.drc.worker` from source), not in a thread of the editor. The project goes over a pipe in small pieces and the findings come back in small pieces (pickle, own program on both ends, pipes only, no network), so that the editor's interpreter lock is never held for long. It falls back to a thread if the helper cannot start or dies, ends when its pipe closes, and `HARNESS_DRC_INPROCESS=1` keeps the check in a thread. No new dependency. | At stress size a thread made edits stall for 300 to 500 ms while it ran. |
| D-129 | The tool ships example projects (`blank`, `first-steps`, `small-satellite`) and templates (CSV for interfaces, parts and lengths, a KiCad netlist, CI scripts, a review checklist) inside the program, reached by `harness new` and `harness templates`. The templates include **demo** engineering values (derating, ampacity, generation settings, separation) so that a newcomer can see wire sizing and test limits work. They are labelled demo everywhere, are not from any standard, and the config files keep `"placeholder": true`, so every result built on them says it rests on placeholders. This does not change D-11: real values still come from the owner. The release check looks at the harness (gauges, lengths, errors) and does not check the placeholder flag; the documents say so. | To make the tool teachable without inventing standards. Proposed by the assistant; the owner may remove the demo values. |
| D-130 | Baseline standards of the starter library and the units the editor adds, per the owner: RS-422, RS-485 and CAN for communication; Micro-D 9, 15, 21, 25 and 31 pin for power and data; Ethernet (RJ45) as the alternative for high data rates (the payload unit and the computer carry it); SMA for RF. A unit connector's Micro-D size follows the number of interface types it carries (1 type 9 pin, 2 types 15, 3 types 21, 4 types 25, more 31; power connectors 9). New interface type `ethernet` with the signals TX+, TX-, RX+, RX- (two pairs, not gigabit). Older parts (D-sub, MDM, circular, TNC) and types (SpaceWire, MIL-STD-1553B, LVDS, I2C) stay in the library so existing projects keep loading. The sample projects, example projects, goldens and documents follow. | Owner feedback, October 2026. The RJ45 part and the two-pair Ethernet type are assumptions to confirm. |
| D-131 | Values from the standards the owner supplied (ECSS-Q-ST-30-11C Rev.2, ECSS-E-ST-20-07C Rev.2) may ship as **optional profiles** (`harness config DIR --apply-profile NAME`), never as defaults. A profile fills unset values only, each value cites its requirement ID, the config file stays a placeholder until an engineer reviews it, and every rule stays silent and reports *not checked* when its number or a part rating is missing. The rule "never invent standard values" stands: nothing is invented, and nothing is applied without the project asking. New rules (connector and wire voltage, temperature margin, mating cycles, manufacturer, wire specification, power/return separation, bundle current, shield bonding, EMC class per bundle) and the Table 6-41 bundle factor in sizing follow. The mapping of the standard's "family-group codes" to tool part classes is a human step: the profile is applied to all connectors and wires. | Owner approval of the compliance audit plan, October 2026. |
| D-132 | The editor follows the **IBM Carbon** design system (owner's UX/UI guidelines, October 2026): the colour tokens are Carbon's White and Gray 100 themes with the status tones darkened where our rule (AA text contrast on every surface) needs it, corners are square, tabs and inputs follow Carbon's pattern, and the typefaces are **IBM Plex Sans and Mono**, bundled as WOFF2 files (380 KB) with their SIL Open Font License 1.1 text (`resources/fonts/`, shipped in `LICENSES/ibm-plex` of the package). Nothing is fetched at run time. The earlier claim that Inter was bundled was not true (the editor used the system font). The category colours, icons and dashes are unchanged (they pass the colour-blind separation test). The guidelines' references to WireViz, Monaco, Graphviz and a YAML editor do not apply: the tool is model-first, WireViz YAML is an export only (see `docs/UX_GUIDELINES_REVIEW.md`). | Owner's UX/UI guidelines. Font licence: OFL-1.1 permits bundling; recorded here and in `compliance/docs/SRF.md`. |
| D-133 | The parts library record (`library/manifest.json`) has two optional fields, `source` (free text, for example the file or database the parts came from) and `date` (YYYY-MM-DD, the date of that data). A person enters the date; the tool never reads the clock into project data. `harness import-parts` records the file name and a short SHA-256 as the source unless `--library-source` is given; `harness library` shows and sets name, version, source and date. Older projects load unchanged (the fields default to empty); every project's model hash changes once because the manifest now has the two keys, so goldens, examples and the tutorial hash were regenerated. A project saved with these fields cannot be read by a tool older than this change (strict model), which the unchanged schema version does not announce; the project format has no downgrade path anyway. | Owner approval (October 2026), from the UX/UI guidelines "version, date and source visible". |
| D-134 | Vulnerability watching at release (action A-08): `python -m tools.check_vulnerabilities` (a release check step) turns the runtime SBOM into pinned requirements and asks **pip-audit 2.9.0** (Apache-2.0, development dependency, runs on the build host only and needs network access there; the tool itself stays offline) about known vulnerabilities. Packages that are not shipped (pip, setuptools, wheel) are left out. A scan that cannot run (no network, no answer) fails the release check; it never reads as a pass. The output is kept as `dist/release-docs/vulnerability-report.txt`. Package signing stays off: the owner chose no signing on 2026-10-08 (deviation T-19); integrity rests on SHA-256 checksums. | Owner decision of 2026-10-08 (A-08). |
| D-135 | **The release gate checks the placeholder mark** (owner's decision of 2026-10-08, the open question of the trial). A release is blocked while any configuration file is marked `"placeholder": true`. The person releasing can still release by writing a reason (at least 10 characters; CLI `--accept-placeholders REASON`, a field in the release window); the reason, the names of the placeholder files and the releaser are appended to the comment of the change log entry and of the baseline, so every later report shows that the release rested on placeholders. When an engineer has reviewed all values and cleared the mark, nothing is asked. The gate does not judge the values, and it does not look at unapproved parts (a separate question). A comment too long for the baseline (2000 characters with the note) is a blocker, not a crash. | Owner request, 2026-10-08. |
| D-136 | **The release gate checks the parts** (owner's decision of 2026-10-08, after D-135). A release is blocked while the harness uses a part that is missing from the parts list, is not approved, or is marked as unverified example data. The parts looked at are those of the harness itself (its connectors and wires), the same as in its bill of materials. As with placeholders, the releaser can release by writing a reason (at least 10 characters; CLI `--accept-unapproved-parts REASON`, a second field in the release window); the reason, the first five part IDs and the releaser are appended to the comment of the change log entry and of the baseline. Approved, verified parts need nothing. The design rule `unapproved-parts` is unchanged. | Owner decision, 2026-10-08. |
| D-137 | **Version 0.1.0 is released with the engineering decisions open** (owner's decision of 2026-10-09: "I want a final 0.1.0", then, asked to confirm, "I waive the step 14 condition: release 0.1.0 with D-10, D-11, D-12 and D-15 still open."). This supersedes D-113 for this release only: step 14 of `docs/RELEASE.md` asks for D-10 and D-15 to be decided and D-11 and D-12 to be supplied before `rcN` is dropped; the owner waived that condition (`compliance/DEVIATIONS.md` T-31). The changelog, README and `compliance/SIGNOFF.md` say what is open. The program still labels results as resting on placeholders, and the release gates (D-135, D-136) still require a written reason to release a harness on placeholders or unapproved parts. | Owner decision, 2026-10-09. |
| D-138 | **CI skips the full suite and the platform jobs for document-only changes** (owner's request of 2026-10-09). In `.github/workflows/harness-ci.yml` the job `changes` lists the files of a pull request or push; when every file is Markdown directly under `HarnessDesignStudio/` or under `HarnessDesignStudio/docs/`, or anything under `HarnessDesignStudio/compliance/`, the jobs `test` and `package-on-24-04` are skipped and the job `docs` runs the checks that read documents (`test_docs`, `test_docs_links`, `test_compliance_matrix`, `test_scf`, `test_sdd_components`, `test_trace`, `test_metrics`, `test_family_group_mapping`, `test_profiles` and the rule-citation test in `test_drc`). Any other file, a change to the workflow itself, or a diff that cannot be read runs everything. Markdown inside `src/` or `tests/` (examples, templates, fixtures) counts as code. The same checks run in `test`, so a code change loses nothing. Branch protection is off (T-18), so no required check can wait on a skipped job. If a new test starts to read a document, add it to the `docs` job. | |
| D-139 | **The generator lists the parts of a harness in the order the saver writes them** (found with the flatsat example, 2026-10-09). A saved project sorts connectors, wires, shields, branch points and segments by ID as text, so `S10` comes before `S2`; the generator numbered them. After a reload, a harness with ten or more segments or shields differed from the regenerated one and was reported as changed on every generation, although nothing had changed. The generator now sorts them by ID as text too (`core/generate/engine.py`). A project made before this fix shows the harnesses that had ten or more shields or segments as *changed* once, on the next generation, and is stable afterwards. Regression test: `tests/test_examples.py::test_the_flatsat_example_opens_clean_and_stays_put_when_generated_again`. | |
| D-140 | **Dense diagrams must be readable and say what they show** (owner's reports of 2026-10-09 on the flatsat example drawn in Expert mode: overlapping units, a tangle of links, then: raw text and letters are cryptic, link types and connectors must be told apart by looking). (1) A unit's room is the taller of its guided and expert heights (`edit.unit_footprints`), so units never overlap in either mode. (2) A link leaves a unit on the edge that faces the unit at its other end; in Expert mode a connector is drawn on that edge. (3) Links are routed together, in `core/routing.py` (`route_links`, no Qt): each link runs horizontally out of its unit into the gutter between lanes, joins a vertical trunk in its own track, and links of one lane pair run side by side, ordered to cross as little as possible; links inside a lane run in a corridor beside it. Only horizontal and vertical pieces, rounded corners. (4) Selecting a unit or link puts its links in focus: the rest fades; background click or Escape ends it; it combines with the *Show* filter. (5) **Meaning is drawn, not spelled** (`gui/glyphs.py`): every kind of link has its own small picture (bolt, ground, bus, differential pair, multi-drop, network, plug, sine wave, square wave, spark, ...), a link whose type has paired signals is drawn as two lines, redundancy stays dashed; every connector family has its own outline (D-sub, Micro-D/MDM, circular, RJ45, coax) with pin dots, filled for pins and hollow for sockets, read from the part (`core/describe.py`). In Expert mode a dashed line down the middle splits a unit into a left side (ports on the left edge) and a darker right side, and each port row reads from its edge inwards: designator, connector picture (flange with screw holes and shell; D-sub tapered, Micro-D a rounded block; size and pin dots follow the pin count), pin count; what a link carries is drawn on the link only, so a row holds three things. Gender is black or white: a male connector (pins) solid, a female (sockets) an outline, an unset gender a dashed outline, explained in the key; the harness drawing and the mating matrix state the gender of the cable connectors in words. A unit in Guided mode shows its connectors as small pictures, the key under the diagram draws the kinds the project uses, and the palette buttons carry the same pictures. A link's chip is its picture; a voltage or current appears beside it only when somebody entered it (nothing is filled in from a standard), in a dense diagram (more than 30 links) for the selected, focused or hovered link; *View > Show all link labels* shows them everywhere. Hovering a link tells its name, type, values and ends in words. Not done: routing around units of lanes between a link's ends. | |
| D-141 | **The harness drawing is a wiring diagram with coloured wires** (owner's request of 2026-10-09, with a WireViz drawing as the example, after the wire-by-wire list of connector pairs was found unreadable). Connectors are tables on the left and the right (id, part, gender in words, pins, mating box connector, signal and number of every used pin); wires are curves in their wire colour on a dark outline; each connector pair has a cable block with its wires (ID, IEC 60757 code), count, gauge and length; shields, segments, spare pins and the routing sketch follow. This replaces the rule that drawings use black and grey only: legibility in greyscale is kept by the dark outline and by writing every colour as a code (REQ-OUT-03 reworded). The colour of a wire is data: set by hand or by the optional `generation.json` entry `wire_colour_by_signal` (absent by default; the tool defines no colours, so unset wires are grey). The IEC 60757 names and codes are used to draw only. Shields are drawn as sleeves (owner's request, same day): the wires of a shield are grouped under a grey jacket in the cable block, with the shield ID and kind and each end drawn as terminated (bar: 360 degree backshell, lead: pigtail, cross: floating); end A is the end of the shield's first wire. Not done: branch points drawn in the diagram (the routing sketch stays separate). | |
| D-142 | **Version 1.0.0** (owner's instructions of 2026-10-12: "Release as 1.0.0"; for the release page and the repository text "Don't know, do whatever needed so I'm not needed"; for the strict release freeze "Do what is needed so I'm not needed"). 1.0.0 is 0.1.0 plus the fixes of the October pre-release audit (`AUDIT.md` at the repository root), the release freeze of parts, interface types and configuration, the examples `minimal-satellite` and `flatsat`, the wiring-diagram drawing with wire colours and the design worksheet. The Debian version is `1.0.0`. The tag `v1.0.0` is created and pushed by a person; pushing a tag `vX.Y.Z` starts `.github/workflows/release.yml`, which checks the tag against the version and the changelog, builds the `.tar.gz` and the `.deb`, runs the packaged self-test, writes `SHA256SUMS` and creates the GitHub release page with the three files (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 15). The release notes are the changelog section of that version plus a sentence saying that the packages are unsigned, the values are placeholders and the release is not a statement of compliance. Nothing else changes: the waivers T-18 to T-31 stand (packages unsigned, no clean-machine test, no usability sessions, no independent review), no standard is claimed to be met, and the condition of step 14 is covered by T-32 and D-143 to D-145. | Owner instruction, 2026-10-12 (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, section "Release 1.0.0"). |
| D-143 | **Licence for 1.0.0 (D-02): the placeholder stays.** The owner offered "Either keep placeholder or find one" (2026-10-12). No licence was supplied and choosing one is the owner's legal decision, so the package metadata keeps `LicenseRef-Proprietary` and the `.deb` copyright file says: copyright holder "the repository owner (GitHub account 0h3xn4)" (no personal name is invented) and "License: not chosen yet by the owner (decision D-02, deliberately left open for 1.0.0). No licence is granted by this file." The root `LICENSE` (GPL-3.0) is WireViz upstream's and applies to the upstream code in this repository (`src/wireviz`); `HarnessDesignStudio/` is separate code (D-01) and is not covered by it. D-02 stays the open question; the owner may choose a licence later (then a licence file in `HarnessDesignStudio/`, the package metadata, the `.deb` copyright text and the README licence paragraph change together). Until then no licence has been granted for `HarnessDesignStudio/`. | Owner's offer of 2026-10-12; the assistant kept the placeholder, which the owner said it may. |
| D-144 | **D-10 (harness boundary rule) and D-15 (title block) are decided as the documented defaults the tool implements today** (the assistant's recorded defaults under the owner's instruction of 2026-10-12, "Make default decisions so that I'm not needed"). D-10: `config/segmentation.json` `mode` is `per_connector_pair`, one harness per pair of unit connectors (option A of `docs/OPEN_DECISIONS.md`); `per_unit_pair` and `per_zone_pair` stay selectable per project. Nominal and redundant interfaces never share a harness in any mode; pyro lines are kept apart in the unit-pair and zone-pair modes while `forbid_pyro_with_other` is on (the default), and in the connector-pair mode the generation note names a mix. The older wording of D-10 above (merge when both ends share a routing zone) is not what the tool does. D-15: a generic, configurable title block with a fixed layout; the fields and their order come from `config/titleblock.json` (`project`, `harness_id`, `title`, `revision`, `date`, `author`, `checker`, `approver`, `sheet`, `status`; none is mandatory; the starter file lists all ten; a missing or empty list draws `project`, `harness_id`, `title`, `revision`, `status`, `sheet`). No company template or standard layout was supplied, so none is implemented. The starter files stay `"placeholder": true` until an engineer reviews them. These are defaults, not an engineering confirmation; the owner or a project can change them (the options remain in `docs/OPEN_DECISIONS.md`). | Owner's delegation of 2026-10-12; the behaviour of the tool is unchanged. |
| D-145 | **D-11 (derating and EMC values) and D-12 (approved parts list): the tool ships no standard or part data; each project supplies them** (the assistant's recorded decision under the owner's instruction of 2026-10-12). This follows the project rule never to invent engineering values. The starter configuration holds no derating, ampacity or EMC numbers (they are unset) and the starter library holds only example parts (`EX-...`, marked unverified). A project supplies its values with `harness config DIR` (including `--ampacity-csv`), its parts with `harness import-parts` (with a statement of what each approval value means) and its EMC classes in `config/emc.json`; the optional profiles of the standards the owner supplied (D-131, `harness config DIR --apply-profile NAME`) fill unset values only, cite their requirement ID and leave the file marked as a placeholder. Until a project supplies them and an engineer reviews them, results stay labelled *pending*, *not checked*, placeholder or example, and releasing a harness needs a written reason (D-135, D-136). Nobody has supplied real values or an approved parts list. The owner can supply them at any time. | Owner's delegation of 2026-10-12; keeps the rule "never invent engineering values". |
| D-146 | **Interface icons: Lucide geometry, drawn with QPainter (ISC licence).** `gui/icons.py` holds icon outlines taken from Lucide (https://lucide.dev, package lucide-static 1.56.0, ISC licence; a few Lucide icons derive from Feather, MIT licence, Cole Bemis). The packaged program excludes QtSvg, so no SVG file is loaded at run time: each icon is a few path strings in Lucide's 24 x 24 grid, turned into a `QPainterPath` and stroked in a theme colour at any size. No new run-time dependency. The two licence notices are kept in the module docstring and below. | The UX standard asks for an SVG icon set (Lucide or Tabler) recoloured from tokens; this keeps the packaged build free of QtSvg. |
| D-147 | **Rename: folder `HarnessDesignStudio/`, commands `harness-design-studio` and `harness-design-studio-gui`, `harness` kept as an alias** (owner's instruction of 2026-10-13: "rename everything in the code accordingly (HarnessDesignStudio instead of harness for example)"; scope approved by the owner: folder, documents and command names). The folder `harness/` is now `HarnessDesignStudio/` (moved with `git mv`, so the history follows). The pip console scripts are `harness-design-studio` (command line) and `harness-design-studio-gui` (editor); `harness` and `harness-gui` stay as aliases of the same functions, so existing scripts, CI files and shortcuts keep working. **Alias policy:** the aliases are not deprecated and not announced for removal; the documents, the help text, the messages and the shipped templates use the long names, and only a decision recorded here can remove an alias. The packaged program uses the same names: `harness-design-studio-gui` (the editor, which also answers `--selftest` and `--drc-worker`) and `cli/harness-design-studio` (the command line tool); the installers link `harness-design-studio`, `harness-design-studio-gui` and `harness` into the bin folder. The folder and archive of the package, the `.deb` package name, `/opt/harness-design-studio` and the launcher file `harness-design-studio.desktop` keep their name. **Not renamed:** the Python package `harness_design_studio`, the file formats and file names (`project.json`, `.harness.lock`, `.harness.lock.takeover`, journal and backup names, the format names such as `harness-design-studio-outputs`), the settings organisation `HarnessDesigner` (saved settings keep working), the output of `--version` (`harness 1.0.0`, which the release workflow checks), the workflow file and job names (so that required checks keep their names), and `src/wireviz`. Past records (the decision log, the audit and compliance records, the changelog of released versions) keep the names they were written with. | Existing projects, scripts and settings must keep working, and the packaged `harness-design-studio` used to be the editor, so the long names would otherwise mean a different program in the package than in a `pip` install. |
| D-148 | **The test suite runs in parallel with pytest-xdist** (owner's request of 2026-10-14: the CI test job took 1 h 38 to 2 h 57 for about 2,600 tests). New dev-only dependency, pinned in `pyproject.toml` and not shipped: pytest-xdist 3.8.0 (MIT), which brings execnet 2.1.2 (MIT); the licence report and the SBOM list the runtime environment only, so they do not change. CI (`.github/workflows/harness-ci.yml`, job `test`) runs `pytest -n auto --dist worksteal -m "not perf" --cov --cov-report= --cov-fail-under=0` and then `pytest -m perf --cov --cov-append --cov-report=term-missing`: the two selections are the whole suite, the combined coverage of both is judged once against the 90% gate on core (`pyproject.toml`), and `HARNESS_TIME_FACTOR` stays 3. The 32 tests that assert a wall-clock limit (`time_limit`, `perf_counter`; all of `tests/test_gui_perf.py`) carry the marker `perf` and run alone, in the second step: a limit in milliseconds is not fair next to three other busy workers, and `xdist_group` with `--dist loadgroup` would pin them to one worker while the others keep running, which does not remove the load; `tests/test_parallel_safety.py` fails when a timed test lacks the marker, when a test uses a fixed `/tmp` path, and when the workflow loses one of the two steps. Window clean-up (the larger gain): most GUI tests built a window and never closed it, so every later test in the process paid for all the left-over windows, their timers and their child widgets (`test_layout_has_nothing_clipped` took 77 s after 160 left-over windows, 8 s alone; a worker reached 3 GB and was killed once by the out-of-memory killer on a shared 16 GB machine); an autouse fixture in `tests/conftest.py` now releases and deletes the top-level widgets a test created and left open, and leaves alone those that existed before it (module-scoped fixtures). Isolation: every test already used `tmp_path` and its own `QSettings` file; `tests/conftest.py` gives every process (each worker too) its own `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and removes it at the end; the check helper process (`gui/drc_process.py`) belongs to one process, so workers do not share it; no test writes into the source tree. Measured on a 4-core machine (2,586 tests, coverage on): 69 min in one process, 20 min for the parallel step plus 4.6 min for the timing step with `-n 4`; the combined coverage of the core was 96.2 % in parallel and 96.5 % in one process (the fuzz and soak tests draw random inputs, so rare lines differ from run to run by about the same amount). `timeout-minutes` of the test job is 120 (was 300): it covers slow or 2-core runners and the packaging steps and still stops a hung test. `tools/release_check.py` and plain `pytest` stay serial on purpose: the release result is the serial one. | |
| D-149 | **Supported platforms: Ubuntu 24.04 and newer, Windows 10 and 11 (64-bit) and macOS 13 and newer (Apple Silicon and Intel). Narrowed by D-168: macOS was removed after 1.5.0.** Supersedes D-110 and D-127 (Ubuntu only) and brings back the Windows target that D-110 dropped (the Windows part of D-38); owner instruction, October 2026. Version 1.2.0 ships packages for all of them: Windows as a per-user setup program built with Inno Setup (no administrator rights, a Start menu entry, the install folder on the user's `PATH`, an uninstaller) and as a portable `.zip`; macOS as `Harness Design Studio.app` in a `.dmg`, one for arm64 and one for x86_64 (each built on a machine of its own kind: PyInstaller makes a single-architecture program here, not a universal one); the Linux packages stay as they are. `tools/build_windows.py` and `tools/build_macos.py` build them (they share the program build and the allow-list check of `tools/build_installer.py`), `tools/smoke_package.py` tests the finished files, and `.github/workflows/harness-packages.yml` (branches `pkg/**`, manual run; never publishes) and `.github/workflows/release.yml` run both. The packages are unsigned until certificates are supplied. The signing and notarization steps exist in the workflows and run only when the repository secrets are present; without them the packages are built unsigned (`docs/RELEASE.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md`). The offline guarantee is unchanged: nothing is downloaded when a package is installed or run. The Windows-specific safety code (reserved file names, long paths, case-insensitive IDs) stays. The model, the file format, the rules, the command line and the bytes of the generated files do not change; the one change in the program is that the editor writes its self-test result to a file when asked (`--selftest --selftest-out FILE`, because the Windows editor has no console); the rule check helper no longer fails where `os.nice` does not exist (Windows), and the bundled IBM Plex fonts are TrueType files on every system (they were WOFF2 files, which Qt on Windows cannot read; the TrueType files are the same fonts unpacked, 1.1 MB instead of 0.4 MB). New build-time tool, not shipped: Inno Setup 6 (the Inno Setup licence, free; preinstalled on the Windows runner image). Nothing new is shipped at run time; the texts of the GPL-3 and LGPL-3 that the package carries for Qt are kept in `packaging/licences/` because Windows and macOS have no Debian `common-licenses` folder. | Owner instruction. |
| D-150 | **UI overhaul, M1: the theme package replaces IBM Carbon and IBM Plex (supersedes D-132).** Owner's brief `docs/ui/UI_OVERHAUL.md`, plan `docs/ui/UI_OVERHAUL_PLAN.md` approved on 2026-10-19. (1) `gui/theme/` is the one source: `tokens.py` (colours, type scale 11/12/13/15/18/22, weights 400/500/600, 4 px grid, control height 32 and 28, rows 26, radius 6 and 8, focus ring 2 px, motion 120 and 180 ms), `palette.py`, `qss.py`, `style.py`, `fonts.py`, `manager.py`; `gui/tokens.py`, `gui/theme.py`, `gui/fonts.py` and `gui/indicators.py` are gone. (2) Colours are the brief's starting values, tuned only where WCAG 2.2 AA asked: on the light theme `text.muted` #7C838D to #646A72, accent #2563EB to #1E5EEA and the status colours darkened (critical #D40002, serious #B24700, caution #8D6007, normal #19784A, standby #0B6DB2); on the dark theme `text.muted` #7A828E to #868E99 and the accent keeps #4C8DFF but its text is dark (#0B0D10, white would be 3.3:1). New tokens the brief does not list: `border.control` (#838B95 light, #6B7380 dark; the outline of inputs and check boxes needs 3:1 where the brief's `border.strong` has about 1.5:1), `accent.text`, `accent.hover`, the `state.*` tones of the existing states (autofill, pending, not checked) and the unchanged diagram tokens (`paper`, `pasteboard`, `cat-*`, whose colour-blind separation is tested). The legacy names (`bg`, `surface`, `surface-2`, `border`, `text`, `text-muted`, `primary`, `on-primary`, `error`, `warning`, `success`, `info`, `auto-fill`, `locked`, `released`, `pending`, `not-checked`, `stale`) stay as aliases of the new tokens so the painters and tests keep working; they are retired as screens are rebuilt. (3) **The style sheet contains no colour.** Setting a different application style sheet re-matches every widget and costs 300 to 500 ms (955 widgets, flatsat), even for a one-rule sheet. The sheet now names palette roles (`palette(base)` ...; Qt 6.11 accepts `placeholder-text`, `accent`, `tooltip-base` and `tooltip-text` too, which gives every token a role) and a theme switch sets the other palette and repolishes the visible widgets; hidden widgets are unpolished and polished by Qt when first shown. Switching light/dark measures 64 to 107 ms (was 560 to 950 ms). A change of UI scale still installs a new sheet and a new font (700 to 950 ms; not met the plan's 250 ms, recorded in the plan). (4) Fonts: Inter 4.1 (Regular, Medium, SemiBold) and JetBrains Mono 2.304 (Regular, Medium), TrueType, SIL OFL 1.1, unmodified, in `resources/fonts/` with their licence texts, 1.8 MB (IBM Plex was 1.2 MB). TrueType because Qt on Windows cannot load WOFF2. Tabular figures through `QFont.setFeature(tnum)` (`theme.font(tabular=True)`). (5) Check boxes, radio buttons and tick boxes of item views are painted by `ThemeStyle` (a `QProxyStyle` over Fusion) from palette roles instead of PNG files per theme. (6) No new dependency. Lucide icons stay `QPainter` paths (D-146): the packaged programs leave out QtSvg. (7) Tests: `tests/test_theme_scan.py` fails on a hex colour, font family or font size outside `gui/theme/`; `tests/test_tokens.py` checks 4.5:1 for every text token on all four surfaces and 3:1 for control outlines and the focus ring in both themes. The assertions that named IBM Plex, Carbon values or the old type scale were replaced by the same checks on the new tokens (listed in the M1 pull request). |
| D-151 | **UI overhaul, M2: the shell.** Owner approved the plan of 2026-10-19 and said "continue as per your recommendations" (compact menu bar kept; project configuration under Settings). (1) A **navigation rail** (`gui/rail.py`) lists the areas Home, Model, Checks, Drawings, Outputs, Revisions and Settings (`gui/areas.py`). The six main tabs stay in one `main_tabs` widget, so every existing test and shortcut (Ctrl+1 to Ctrl+6, `go_to`, `show_slot`) keeps working; an area is a *filter* over the tabs (`SlotTabs.set_filter`), the rail follows any tab change, and Home and Settings are pages of a `QStackedWidget` beside `main_tabs`. Model holds Diagram and Devices, Checks the Checks tab, Drawings the Harness tab, Outputs the Lists tab, Revisions the Releases tab. (2) The **top bar** is one full-width row in a tool bar (project, save state, revision, hash, breadcrumbs, Undo and Redo as icons, Guided and Expert, Search, Generate, Check, Export, status chip); the old second tool bar is gone. Arrange diagram and the Show filters are a corner widget of the Model tab bar (they act on the diagram only and the diagram page has no room for them). The stepper that replaces the Generate, Check and Export buttons is M3. (3) The **status bar** gets a job indicator (generate, export, arrange and the rule check, with a busy bar and Cancel; when idle the last check), a Problems button and an *Offline* marker; the model hash, the rule count and the generation record move to a tooltip. The generate and export progress dialogs stay modal for now (a non-modal progress needs the flows rewritten). (4) The **Problems panel** (`gui/problems_panel.py`, `ProblemsList`) is a bottom dock, closed until asked for: severity, rule, object and message from `ctl.open_findings()`, filter, click to go to the object. The inspector's Problems tab (the to-do cards) stays until M5, which makes the two one; its count (28) is the number of to-do groups, the panel's (75) the number of findings. (5) **Layout is remembered per project**: panels, area, view and rail state under a settings key that is a hash of the project folder (nothing is written into the project). (6) **Numbers that moved**: the rail adds 72 px, so the width below which the right dock hides is 1222 px (was 1150) and the left dock 872 px (was 800); the window's minimum width is 1181 px. The rail shows icons only when it is collapsed by choice or the window is shorter than 640 px. Search has the label "Search  Ctrl+K" (was "Search / commands  Ctrl+K"). (7) Tests edited: `test_ux_shell.py` (the tab list is checked without the area filter; the window's minimum width limit gains the rail's 72 px), `test_ux_devices.py` (same tab list), `test_ux_retire.py` (the dock set now includes `dock-problems`); new `test_ui_areas.py` and `test_ui_shell.py`. |
| D-152 | **UI overhaul, M3: Home and the workflow steps.** Standing owner authority of 2026-10-19 ("continue always with your recommendations"). (1) The buttons Generate and Check in the top bar and a new Release button become **three steps, Generate, Check (key `review`) and Release**, in the order the work goes, each button carrying its state (`gui/workflow.py`, no Qt): each has a state (done, to do, running, attention, blocked) and a detail that is also its tooltip. The rules are the logic that already existed, not new logic: Generate is done when the plans are current, running while a generation runs, *attention* when they are out of date and to do when there are none; Check is blocked until the plans exist, running while the rule check runs, to do until a result is current, *attention* when rules failed and done otherwise (with the count of rules not checked and of harnesses in review in its detail); Release is done when every harness is released, blocked until the plans are current and the design is checked (and in a read-only project) and to do after that, always with "n of m released" in its detail. `tests/test_ui_workflow.py` checks the steps over every combination of the facts. The Release button (`topbar-release`) opens the release flow; Export stays a button after the steps. The word Release is hidden and the chevrons dropped in the narrowest fold of the top bar. (2) **Lock banner (R3)**: when what is in front of you is part of a released harness (the harness shown in Drawings, the one chosen in Revisions, or the selected unit or link in Model, Checks and Outputs), a banner names it (`W001 revision A is released and locked.`, or the count) and offers *Start new revision* unless the project is read-only. It is computed from the existing lock index (`ctl.state_index().of(kind, id).locks`). (3) **Home** has the recent projects (`gui/recent.py`, kept in `QSettings` under `home/recent`, newest first, 8 at most, one card each with the counts and how many harnesses are draft, in review and released, a missing folder is marked and can be forgotten) and the examples from `core.templates` (the flatsat example is the primary action; it opens as a copy in Documents/Harness Design Studio, made once, so the person's changes stay). Nothing is written into a project. (4) **Empty states** (`gui/empty_state.py`, `attach_empty_state`): a table without rows shows a title, a line on what to do and, where there is one, the action; a filter that hides every row says *No row matches* instead. Applied to the Interfaces and Generate plan tables; Devices, Releases, Checks and Problems already had their own. (5) Tests: new `test_ui_workflow.py`, `test_ui_home.py`; no old test edited. |
| D-153 | **UI overhaul, M4: model editors as spreadsheets.** Standing owner authority of 2026-10-19. (1) `gui/sheet.py` gives a `QTableView` spreadsheet behaviour: cell selection with extended selection, Enter or F2 edits the current cell, Esc closes the editor and keeps the old text, Ctrl+V pastes TSV (parsed with the `csv` module so quoted cells with tabs, quotes and line breaks from Excel come out right), an edit with several rows selected is applied to the same column of each of them, and a header menu hides columns (saved under `tables/<key>/hidden`, the first column cannot be hidden; every `TableView` that saves its widths has it). The model decides what a cell is worth: it offers `is_editable(column)` and `set_cells([(row, column, text), ...])`, which validates all cells first and then makes **one controller transaction** (`update_interface_rows`, `update_unit_rows`, new in the controller, built from the existing `ops_update_*`), so a paste or a bulk edit is one undo step of core `History` and respects locks and read-only. One invalid cell stops the whole paste and says why; read-only columns are skipped and counted. `QUndoStack` is not adopted (Q7). (2) The Interface table becomes this sheet (columns, validation and messages unchanged; Max A is now Max current (A) and right-aligned) and a **Unit table** (ID, name, subsystem, zone, chain, number of interfaces and connectors, notes; name, chain and notes edit; the ID is read-only because renaming changes references) is the new Units tab of the left panel (Ctrl+Shift+J). Selecting several rows selects those objects on the diagram, and the other way. (3) **Not done, and why**: Devices (connectors and pins) keeps its own tab, whose pin table already has paste, fill and keyboard editing, so the plan's "Devices tab replaced by Connectors/pins + Part panel" is not carried out (a replacement would rebuild a view that works, with no gain in what a user can do); the Wires list is generated, not edited, so it stays a read-only sheet (Lists tab, with the new column menu); **New interface stays a short flow** (it needs a type and two units) instead of an inline row. The left dock widens for the tables as before (the diagram and the inspector stay beside it: master and detail). (4) Measured at stress size (200 units, 2,000 interfaces): the Interface table opens in about 130 ms and the Unit table in about 25 ms (limit 200 ms), a paste of 500 rows is one undo step. No old test was edited. |
| D-154 | **UI overhaul, M5: Checks and Problems, and the 11-second edit.** Standing owner authority of 2026-10-19. (1) **The unexplained stall of the baseline is explained and gone.** With a rule check result of 82,549 findings (the stress project) every edit took 10.7 to 12.7 s on the GUI thread; with the check off it took 50 to 240 ms. A profile of an edit made after a result had arrived showed two lookups that scan every wire of every harness *per finding*: `canvas_state._resolver` called `drc.locate` for each of about 20,000 wire findings that belong to no link (49 s under the profiler, 11 s real), and `project_tree.place` called `_wire_index()` (which compares the identity of all 150 harnesses) 82,546 times (5 s). Both are in the GUI layer, so the fix stays inside the rules of the overhaul: the resolver answers the same as `drc.locate` for the two kinds the diagram has (a unit or a link) from a set of the harness objects built once (tested against `drc.locate` for every kind of ID), and `count_problems` fetches the wire index once. Measured at stress size with the check on: an edit 70 to 90 ms (was 10.7 to 12.7 s), taking in a result 132 ms on the GUI thread for 82,549 findings, `bench_gui` rename, move, add, undo and redo 90 to 520 ms. Nothing in `core` was touched and the check itself is unchanged. (2) **Out of date is said at once**: the Checks tab shows *Out of date* (warning colour, words, a tooltip that says a new check is on its way or that it was cancelled) the moment the shown results no longer match the design; the status line and the buttons are as before. The re-run still starts after a pause of 1.2 s (`DRC_DELAY_MS`, kept: an existing test and decision, the check runs in a helper process and a shorter wait would restart it while the person types), not the 250 ms of the plan. (3) **A Problems row goes to every kind of object** a finding can name: a unit or link (diagram, selected), a connector (its unit), a wire or harness (Drawings), a part (Devices) and an engineering value file (its window). Right-click or Shift+Enter on a row opens *Details and fixes*: the same finding as a card with Fix and Waive in the inspector's Problems tab, which is how the two Problems views are one: the panel lists every finding, the cards act on them. (4) Not done: the engineering values as a tab of the Checks area (they stay a window of their own, which keeps the editor usable beside it; the Checks tab already counts and links them). Tests: new `test_ui_checks.py`, `test_ui_checks_perf.py`, `test_ui_state_index_scale.py`; one old assertion edited: `test_gui_drc.py::test_show_selects_units_and_switches_to_the_harness_tab` expected the "no place" message for the ID `derating` (a value file, which now opens its window), so it uses an ID that exists nowhere. |

| D-156 | **UI overhaul, M7: one import wizard, and a large file does not freeze the window.** Standing owner authority of 2026-10-19. (1) The wizard of D-98 (file, mapping with a live preview, per-row reasons, apply as one undo step) already handled five kinds and, in its `KINDS`, interfaces too; the window still opened the older `ImportDialog` for interfaces. File > Import > Interfaces, Ctrl+Shift+I and the project tree now open the wizard (`MainWindow.import_interfaces_flow`); the first page has a *Paste table text instead…* button that closes the wizard and opens the older dialog, which stays because it takes pasted CSV text (and its tests and `import_flow` stay unchanged). (2) **Large files**: planning 20,000 rows took 550 to 580 ms and filling the check page's table another 280 ms, all on the GUI thread. A file of more than 1,500 rows (`LARGE_ROWS`) is now planned in a `QThread` (the project is only read; the wizard is modal): the mapping page shows a busy line with Cancel, Next waits (`isComplete`), a cancelled or replaced request's result is dropped (a generation counter) and closing the wizard waits for the helper. Smaller files plan synchronously exactly as before, so `replan()` and every existing test keep their meaning. Hand-over of the interpreter: a busy Python thread kept the window's Python callbacks waiting (each C++ call inside a callback gives the GIL away and waits up to the switch interval to get it back: 100 size hints cost half a second), so the helper sets `sys.setswitchinterval(0.0001)` while it runs; measured longest pause of the window while reading 20,000 rows: 530 ms before, 100 to 125 ms after (the rest is showing the page for the first time). `TableView` sizes the columns of a table of more than 1,000 rows from its first 150 rows (Qt measured 1,000 rows per column: 0.5 s for 20,000 rows); the check page now fills in 30 ms (was 283). (3) What did not need to change: the word-mapping fields already show the accepted words and the apply page already states the result before Finish. (4) Tests: new `test_ui_import.py` (the packaged parts, segment-length and ampacity samples give the same project through the wizard and through `import-parts`, `import-lengths` and `config --ampacity-csv`; interfaces and netlist samples import in one undo step; a small file is read at once, a large one in the background with the same plan as the direct call; cancel; closing while reading; a 20,000-row file keeps the window responsive; the window routes interfaces to the wizard and the paste link to the older dialog). One old test edited: `test_ux_shell.py::test_the_project_tree_opens_imports_adds_and_points_the_inspector` stubs `import_interfaces_flow` instead of `import_flow`. |
| D-157 | **UI overhaul, M8: a list of the exported files with their checksums, and a revision view that says what it locks.** Standing owner authority of 2026-10-19. (1) The Lists tab (Outputs area) gets an *Exported files* panel under the lists (`gui/outputs_files.py`, `OutputsFiles`; a vertical splitter, saved as a setting): every file of the outputs folder with File, State, SHA-256, Document, Harness, Format, Size and Written. The SHA-256 is that of the file **on disk**, worked out in a `QThread` (`_GatherThread`, a generation counter drops a stale result), so a folder of hundreds of drawings never holds up the window. State compares it with the `manifest.json` the export wrote: *Matches export*, *Changed since export*, *Missing*, *Not in the export* (a `StateChip` with word and icon, never colour alone).
