Hooks `opener` and `ask_folder` let tests drive it. The export dialog (and the old result list in it) is unchanged. (2) **Revisions**: the Differences table tints a row with `diff.added`, `diff.removed` or `diff.changed` (`DictTableModel` returns a `BackgroundRole` brush for a row with a `tint`; the change word stays in the cell), and the Releases tab says what the shown harness's release locks ("H1 revision B is released and locks 18 wires, 9 interfaces, ...", from `core.vcs.lockview.locked_objects`, no new core code). (3) Why no more: the timeline, the diff by kind with counts, the release, revise and review dialogs with their lock trees and the change-control buttons already did what the plan lists; M8 adds what was missing. (4) Acceptance as measured: the hashes in the panel equal `hashlib` of the files and `manifest.json` (`test_gather_is_the_same_as_hashing_the_folder_by_hand`); the 567 files an export writes are byte-identical to 1.2.0 (`tools/output_hashes.py` against `OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json`, test unchanged); the diff rows agree with `core.vcs.diff` change records for every kind (`test_the_diff_agrees_with_the_change_records_of_core_vcs`). New `test_ui_outputs.py` (12 tests). One existing test edited: `test_security.py::test_the_code_never_replaces_the_process_or_opens_a_browser` also allows `QDesktopServices` in `gui/outputs_files.py` (Open and Reveal for files the export wrote, with `QUrl.fromLocalFile`, as the export dialog does; the same check otherwise). |
| D-158 | **UI overhaul, M9: Settings.** Standing owner authority of 2026-10-19. (1) The Settings area becomes a section list and a section (`gui/settings_page.py`; `SettingsPage` moved out of `pages.py`): **Appearance** (theme: follow the system, light or dark; interface size 100, 125, 150 or 200 %; *Reduce motion*), **Editing** (mode, *Snap units to the grid*, *Show every link label*), **Project** (the button to the engineering values, as before), **Keyboard shortcuts** (a filterable table of every key of the window, read from the actions, plus the table and diagram keys), **About** (version, Python, Qt and PySide6 versions, where the settings file is, the licence note of the program) and **Licences** (Inter and JetBrains Mono, SIL OFL 1.1, from the bundled files; Lucide and Feather, ISC and MIT; Qt for Python, LGPL 3.0, with where its text ships). (2) Every control has a twin in a menu and on Ctrl+K and is only a view of it: the page calls the same action, the window stores the value and applies it at once, and `sync` follows whatever changed it. Persisted keys: `ui/theme`, `ui/scale`, `ui/mode`, `ui/snap` (as before) and the new `ui/labels` and `ui/reduce_motion`; tests start a second window on the same settings file and compare. (3) **Reduce motion** has real effect: `ThemeManager.set_reduce_motion` makes `motion()` return 0 and turns off Qt's own fade and slide of menus, combo lists and tooltips (`QApplication.setEffectEnabled`), putting each back as it was when the setting is turned off; it is a new View menu action (`act-reduce-motion`). The program has no animations of its own. Qt has no reduce-motion hint, so it does not follow the operating system's setting (default off). (4) **About** is no longer a message box: Help > About opens this section. The licence text of Lucide and Feather moved from `tools/collect_licences.py` into `gui/licences.py` (one source; the build tool imports it). (5) **Not changed:** the scale choices stay 100 to 200 % (the plan said 90 to 125 % plus the existing steps if kept); shortcuts are shown and not editable (no rebinding was asked for); the docks stay in Home and Settings (the user's layout). (6) **UI scale change is still about 1.1 s** (target 250 ms: not met): measured with cProfile, 1.06 s of it is Qt's `QApplication.setStyleSheet` re-reading the sheet for about 1,250 widgets (25 tables); freezing updates and header signals gave 0.8 s. A sheet without scale needs `em` units for every size and would change how every control is laid out, so it is left; the pointer shows a wait cursor during the change. (7) Tests: new `test_ui_settings.py` (11 tests: sections and names, theme/scale/reduce motion and mode/snap/labels applied and kept across a second window, the page follows menus and Ctrl+K, the Qt effects switched off and restored, the shortcut list and its filter and empty state, About, the licence texts, one source for the Lucide text). No existing test was edited. |
| D-159 | **UI overhaul, final milestone: checks, two faults found by them, release 1.3.0.** Standing owner authority of 2026-10-19. (1) **Checks over everything** (`tests/test_ui_final.py`): every area, the tabs, the six sections of Settings and the Problems panel are opened in both themes with a Qt message handler (no warning but the offscreen platform's `propagateSizeHints`), every control has an accessible name, every icon-only button a tooltip and a name, the Tab key (a real focus walk from the first stop) visits at least three controls in every area and comes back to where it started, and the focus can be seen on a combo box, a check box, a push button and a line edit (the picture of the control changes when it takes the focus) in both themes. (2) **A keyboard trap, found by the walk and fixed:** a `SlotTabs` with one visible tab hides its tab bar, but a `QTabWidget` is a Tab stop that hands the focus to its tab bar, so Tab from the project panel's tab bar chose the (visible) tab widget of the right panel, could not give it the focus and stayed where it was, in every area. `SlotTabs._sync_bar` now makes the widget a Tab stop only while its bar is shown. (3) **The left panel did not return to its width** after Interfaces then Units then Project (the Units tab of M4 took the widened width for the original); fixed, with a test. (4) `tools/ui_screenshots.py` runs each theme and part in a process of its own (two themes in one process crashed Qt's offscreen platform inside `setStyleSheet`); `docs/ui/screenshots/after/` is the complete set (103 pictures, both themes) and `docs/ui/BEFORE_AFTER.md` compares it with the 1.2.0 set. (5) **Version 1.3.0 and the output proof.** The version is raised in `pyproject.toml` and `__init__.py`, the example projects, the golden fixtures and the documents that quote it are regenerated, `docs/releases/1.3.0.md` and the changelog section are written (`tools.release_notes check v1.3.0` passes). A version change cannot leave generated files byte-identical (they name the tool version, and a generated project's model hash includes the version in its generation record), so `tools/output_hashes.py` compares like with like: each example is given the version of 1.2.0 (project and generation record) and the current version string is replaced by it in every file before hashing (same length, so PDF offsets stay valid). **567 of 567 files identical.** (6) Performance numbers are in `PERF_BASELINE.md`; the interface-size change (1.0 to 1.1 s, target 250 ms) is the one target not met. (7) Not done by the assistant, as it needs a person: tagging and publishing 1.3.0 (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 15), the owner's sign-off (step 14), a screen-reader pass, the clean-machine install test and signing (all waived or open as before). No existing test was edited in this milestone except the version-bearing goldens and `tests/test_output_hashes.py` (its docstring). |
| D-160 | **Version 1.4.0: the feedback of the AIT engineers (plan `docs/ait/AIT_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`).** Standing owner authority of 2026-10-19 and the owner's instruction "Continue as per your recommendations" (2026-10-20). Ten requirements were mapped to the code of 1.3.0; five milestones, each a pull request: M1 edit a wire, M2 wires a person draws (several wires on one pin, twisting), M3 pin function, labels and soldering, M4 who changed what, M5 reuse (a device library, an InvenTree file import). The rule "UI only" of the 1.3.0 overhaul does not apply to this work; the other design rules do. **File format rule:** a field added after 1.3.0 is optional and is not written while it has its default value (`SparseEntity`, `OMIT_WHEN_DEFAULT`, `core/model/base.py`), so a project that does not use a new feature keeps byte-identical files, the same model hash and the same outputs; new output columns exist only when used, as the EMC class column does. Schema version stays 1. A project that uses a new field is not read completely by 1.3.0 (an older tool quarantines fields it does not know and says so). Checked by `tests/test_wires.py` and `tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json`. |
| D-161 | **1.4.0 M1: edit a wire.** The gauge, type (a wire part of the library), colour and length of one wire can be set; setting any of them locks the wire (`Wire.locked`), which generation already honours; clearing one of them is refused (a person gives the wire back with *Reset to generated*, which unlocks it, and the next Generate sets the values from the design). Two new optional wire fields, `label` (text that the wire labels print instead of the signal name) and `note` (a remark; a column in the wire list), are always kept by generation and do not lock; a wire with a label or note that generation cannot carry over (its interface is gone) is named in the findings, as a locked wire is. The plans are in `core/wires.py` (`plan_wire_edit`, `plan_wire_reset`), run through `History.execute` (one undo step, `check_locks` refuses a released harness). The Wire list gets the columns Label and Note only when some wire has one; the independent verifier checks them and the label text. The form is the *Edit wire* tab beside *Why* in the Drawings area (`gui/wire_editor.py`): fields a person did not touch change nothing, several selected wires take one edit, mistakes are said inline. No value is invented: colours are those of IEC 60757 (or the person's text), types those of the project's library. |
| D-162 | **1.4.0 M2: wires that a person draws, several wires on one pin, twisting groups.** `Wire.manual` and `ShieldGroup.manual` (optional, written only when true). A manual wire joins two pins of the cable connectors of one harness, has no interface, is never matched by generation (`_wire_keys` leaves it out) and is appended unchanged while its connectors and pins exist; its ID is reserved so generation never reuses it; a manual wire or group that cannot be kept (the harness has nothing left to carry) is named in the findings ("that you drew", "that you made"). Its ends can be moved and it can be deleted (`plan_wire_edit` with `from_connector` ..., `plan_delete_wires`); the pins of a generated wire follow the interface and the pin assignment of the devices and are changed there. **Several wires on one pin** are allowed; the new design rule `pin-shared` (warning, waivable; 35 rules) names the pin, and `verify_project` reports *pin_shared* as a warning instead of the error *pin_reused* when a manual wire is among the users (two generated wires on a pin stay an error). The DRC report says `Rules run: 35`; `tools/output_hashes.py` gives that number as 34 so the proof against 1.2.0 still compares every other byte (D-160). **Groups:** `plan_group_wires` makes a twisted pair or shielded pair (2 wires), quad (4), coax (1) or overall shield (2 or more) from wires of one harness that are in no group; the shield ends come from the generation settings (floating while they are placeholders); `plan_ungroup_all` takes apart only manual groups; deleting a wire drops a manual group that falls below its size. Generation reserves the IDs of manual groups, appends them while all their wires exist and none is in a generated group, and says when it cannot. The form is in `gui/wire_editor.py` (New wire, Ends, Delete wire, Group, Ungroup). |
| D-163 | **1.4.0 M3: pin function, connector and harness labels, termination in the lists.** `Pin.function`, `Connector.label` and `Harness.label` (optional, written only when set). *Function* is free text and means nothing to the tool: generation copies it from a box pin to the cable pin it creates; it is an editable column of the Devices tab, a column of pasted blocks and of the pinout import (a table with both a Signal and a Function column stores both; a table with only a Function column keeps reading it as the signal, as before; "Function description" is no longer an unstored heading). Labels: a box connector's on its Devices card (`ops_update_connector(label=)`), the harness's and a cable connector's in the *Labels* section of the Edit wire tab (`plan_set_labels`); generation keeps them. `labels.csv` prints `<label> (<ID> mates <box>)` for a connector (a cable end without a label takes the one of its box connector), and a row of the kind `harness` for a labelled harness. The pinout tables (`pinouts.csv`, `box_pinouts.csv`) get `Function` and then `Termination` columns only when some pin has a function or a termination other than `unspecified`; `crimp`/`solder` was already a pin field and cable pins already copied it. What soldering needs beyond this (a note on the wire list, a rule, a process step) is for the AIT engineers to say. The independent verifier checks every new column and text. |
| D-164 | **1.4.0 M4: who made the last change, read without starting Git.** The Revisions area names the last commit of the Git repository around the project folder (author name, date, first line of the message, short id, branch). `core/vcs/gitinfo.py` reads the repository's own files, read-only: `HEAD`, the branch file or `packed-refs`, then the commit object, loose (zlib) or in a pack (`.idx` version 2, `.pack`, with the delta forms). **The `git` program is not started**, so the "no `subprocess`" rule of `docs/SECURITY.md` keeps its single exception (D-128), no hook, filter or `core.*` setting of a repository can run, nothing needs Git to be installed, and the packaged programs behave like the source. Limits: every read is capped (1 MiB per object, a delta chain of 64), a damaged, shallow or unknown store gives "no line", never an error; only the author's name is kept, not the e-mail address. It is the last commit of the whole repository, not of the project alone, and uncommitted changes are not shown (the tooltip says both). Revisions of the change log (review, release, new revision) stay the record of release steps; the commit line is context, not a signature. Nothing is written to a project file or an output, so every output stays byte-identical. |
| D-165 | **1.4.0 M5: reuse of devices across projects, and parts lists from InvenTree.** *Device library file*: a JSON file `harness-device-library`, version 1 (`core/device_library.py`), holds devices (the unit, its box connectors with every pin: signal, function, contact size, termination, fixed) and the parts and interface types they name, so a device is complete on its own. A file is added to (a device with the same unit ID is replaced) and written sorted by ID so a library kept in Git shows only the change; the old file is kept as `.bak`. What belongs to one project does not travel: the zone, and what generation allocated on a pin (`interface_id`, the signal given for it, `locked`); a signal that is fixed, or one a person set by hand, does. Importing only adds: a taken unit ID gets the next free one (`OBC1` becomes `OBC1-2`), parts and interface types the project has stay as the project has them (and the plan names those that differ), parts that come with the file keep the approval status the file says and are reported when that is not `approved`; one device with a problem is a row with a reason and the others are still added. Reading is capped (8 MB, 500 devices, 64 connectors per device); a newer file version is refused, not guessed. Where: Devices tab buttons *Save to library…* and *Add from library…* (one undo step), `export-devices` and `import-devices` (`--list`, `--dry-run`). *InvenTree*: there is no connection to a server (the tool still opens no network). The parts import reads the export file of the parts program: `.csv`, `.tsv`, `.json` (a list of flat records, or a paged answer with one list) and `.xlsx`; the column `IPN` is the part ID (its `ID` is a database number), `Name` or `Description` the description, `MPN` the part number; a category path such as `Connectors/D-Sub` is read as `connector` (the first step that is one of the six categories, or its plural), any other category is refused as before. The approval status is never guessed: you say what the values mean, as for every parts list. |
| D-166 | **1.5.0: a harness made by hand (the AIT engineers: create a project completely from scratch).** A project could be started empty (`new`, the example *blank*) and could have units, devices down to each pin and interfaces, but a harness only came from Generate, so a project with no interface had nowhere to put a connector or draw a wire. `core/manual_harness.py` adds the plans `plan_new_harness` (an empty draft, `generated=False`, the next free ID of the naming pattern, a name that is not used by another harness), `plan_add_connector` (a cable connector from a library connector part with pins `1` to its pin count or the names given; or, with `mates_with`, the other half of a unit's connector: mating part from the library else the same part, opposite gender, keying and pins copied, one per unit connector in a harness; roles cable, inline, feedthrough or test), `plan_set_pin` (function, termination, contact size) and `plan_delete_connector` (refused while a wire uses it). Only a harness a person made takes a connector: Generate rebuilds the connectors of its own harnesses from the design and would drop one added by hand, so the plans refuse that harness and say why; a released harness is refused as everywhere. Generate never touches a harness with `generated=False`, its connectors or its wires (tested: a plan after the harness is made is empty for it). Nothing is invented: no gauge, colour or length is chosen for a person. No new field, so no file format change and no change of any output of an existing project. Where: Harness tab (*New harness…* and the *Connectors* tab, `gui/connector_editor.py`), Edit > New harness… (Ctrl+Shift+H). A command line equivalent is not built: the plans are plain functions of the core and a script can call them. |
| D-167 | **1.5.0: the packages work without hassle (Linux and Windows).** The owner asked that a user can just run the installer, with no separate Python and no PATH choice. Findings: the six packages are PyInstaller builds and already carry Python, Qt and the Visual C++ runtime; the Windows setup asks nothing but the folder and puts its folders on the user PATH by itself, and removes them on uninstall (D-149); the Linux self-test passes with no Python reachable. What was not covered: (1) the CI smoke tests ran the packages in the runner's own environment, which has a Python, so they could not show that a package does not lean on it: `tools/smoke_package.py` now runs the programs with `clean_env()` (no `PYTHON*`, no virtual or conda environment, no PATH folder of the runner's with a Python; the system folders such as `/usr/bin` stay, which the first release run of 1.5.0 showed: macOS keeps a stub Python there and the launcher script of the app needs `dirname`) and the self-test has the step *runs on its own Python* (a frozen program fails it when anything on `sys.path` is outside its own folder, which for a macOS app is the whole `.app`); (2) on Linux a missing graphics or window-system library ended in a Python traceback, or in nothing when started from the menu. Measured on the real package: with `libxcb-cursor`, `libxcb-icccm`, `-image`, `-keysyms`, `-randr`, `-render-util`, `-shape`, `-xinerama`, `libxkbcommon`, `libxkbcommon-x11`, `libfontconfig` and `libdbus` hidden from the loader the editor still starts, so the package really needs only `libGL.so.1`, `libEGL.so.1` and `libxcb.so.1` from the machine (graphics driver and desktop; they cannot be bundled: OpenGL must match the driver). `gui/startup_help.py` (no Qt, no subprocess) checks those three with the loader, notices a missing desktop session, parses an `ImportError` that names a library, and words the fix; `packaging/gui_entry.py` runs it before Qt is imported (not for `--selftest` or an explicit `QT_QPA_PLATFORM`), shows the message in the terminal and, when there is none, in a window of zenity, kdialog or xmessage; `--check-system` runs only the check (exit 3 when something is missing); `install.sh` runs it after installing and warns, without undoing the install. The `.deb` keeps its longer dependency list (apt installs the extras at no cost to the user). Not done, and not doable in code: signing, so Windows SmartScreen and macOS Gatekeeper still ask once (needs the owner's certificate, `docs/RELEASE.md`). |
| D-168 | **macOS support removed (after 1.5.0).** Owner instruction, 2026-10-21: "please remove macOS completely and entirely, nobody needs it". Supported platforms are Ubuntu 24.04 and newer and Windows 10 and 11 (64-bit); this narrows D-149. Removed: the macOS build, test and release jobs of the workflows (`harness-platforms.yml`, `harness-packages.yml`, `release.yml`: the release now builds and publishes four packages, the `.deb`, the `.tar.gz`, the Windows setup program and the `.zip`, and one `SHA256SUMS`), `tools/build_macos.py`, `packaging/macos/`, the macOS mode of `tools/smoke_package.py`, the `.icns` of `tools/make_icons.py`, the macOS parts of the package check, the release notes and the checksum tool, the macOS tests, and the macOS steps and statements of the documents; the repository secrets `MACOS_*` are not read any more. Not changed: the file format, the model, the rules, the command line, every generated file, the Linux and Windows packages. What stays as history, because it is true: the releases 1.2.0 to 1.5.0 on GitHub still carry their two macOS disk images (they are not maintained, tested or supported any more; deleting them is the owner's decision), and the changelog, the release notes, the publication records and the checksum lists of those releases still name them. `release.yml` in `notes_only` mode lists a `.dmg` of an old release under *Other files*. The `macos-latest` test job is gone from the pull request checks; if the repository requires it as a status check, remove it in the repository settings (Branches). | Owner instruction. |
| D-169 | **Version 1.7.0: a build workbook instead of the PDF plans as the main output (plan `docs/ait/TEST_USER_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`).** The test users of 1.6.0 did not like the harness plan PDFs; they asked for "a clearly structured, visually understandable and actionable spreadsheet" that can be used "to produce production quality harnesses", and said the tool felt overwhelming (D-171). The owner answered four questions on 2026-10-21: the workbook is the main output and the PDF drawings stay available but are off unless asked for; the build sheet must carry strip length per wire end, contact or terminal part and crimp tool, sleeves, heat shrink and label position, and a test sheet with measured values and sign-off; the order is the workbook first and the simple workspace after it (planned for 1.8.0 on 2026-10-21; on 2026-10-22 the owner decided that both ship as one release, 1.7.0, D-171). **First step (built):** a new file per harness, `harnesses/<ID>/<ID>_build.xlsx` (`core/outputs/buildsheet.py`), written from the model that exists today: *Start here* (ready to build, what is open: wires without length, gauge, colour or part are TO FIX, pins in use without a termination and parts not approved are NOTE; the author, checker and approver; a sign-off block), *Cut list* (cut order: thick before thin, then colour, then length; the colour as a coloured cell with its name and IEC 60757 code; empty values shown as *to fill in*), *Connectors* (one block per connector, pin by pin, with the steps to tick), *Parts*, *Labels*, *Tests* (empty columns for the measured value, result, initials and date) and *About*. A normal openpyxl workbook with styles, frozen header rows, filters, widths, A4 landscape print set-up with the stamp in the footer and conditional formats; **no formulas** (every value is computed by the program, and text that looks like a formula is stored as text); deterministic (`exports.normalise_xlsx`, the zip rewritten with fixed time stamps). The independent verifier reads it with the spreadsheet library and checks the sheets, every wire of the cut list (values and order), every pin of every connector, the parts, labels and tests against the same rows as the CSV tables, that the result columns are still empty, and the counts of *Start here* (`core/outputs/verify.py`, mutation tests in `tests/test_build_workbook.py`). **The drawings are optional:** `build_outputs(..., drawings=False)` leaves out `drawing_A3.pdf`, `drawing_A4.pdf` and `drawing_A3_s<n>.svg` of the harnesses (the system diagrams stay); `harness-design-studio export DIR --drawings` and the check box *Include the PDF drawings* of the export window turn them on; the command line writes none by default, the editor starts with the box checked only when the previous export in the folder had them, and a release writes the outputs again with the same set (`drawings_exported`). The library default of `build_outputs` stays every file, so the output proof and the goldens still cover the drawings; a set with the drawings of all harnesses or of none is valid, of only some is reported (`out_missing`). **Proof that nothing else changed:** `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` still compares every file of the four examples with release 1.2.0; the one thing it lets through is the new file kind by name (`ADDED_AFTER_BASE`). The golden digests (`tests/fixtures/outputs`) are regenerated: additions only. Not built yet (milestone 2 of 1.7.0): the model fields for strip length, contact and tool, and accessories (D-170). Not changed: the file format, the model, the rules, every other output file. **Status of 2026-10-22:** built and released in 1.7.0 (tag `v1.7.0`, published 2026-10-22); the model fields named above as not built yet were built under D-170, in the same release. |
| D-170 | **Version 1.7.0: the build data a technician needs (plan `docs/ait/TEST_USER_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, milestone 2).** The owner asked (2026-10-21) that the build sheet carry the strip length per wire end, the contact or terminal part and the crimp tool, and sleeves, heat shrink and label position. New optional fields, all left out of the files while empty (`SparseEntity`), so a project that does not use them keeps byte-identical files, hashes and outputs (`tools/output_hashes.py`: identical): `Wire.strip_a_mm` and `Wire.strip_b_mm` (the From and the To end, millimetres, 0 to 100,000 as a guard against a typing slip, not a design limit); `Pin.contact_part` (a library part of category *contact*: the contact the cable side uses on that pin); `Part.tool` (the crimp tool or its setting, free text); `Harness.accessories`, a list of `Accessory` (`id`, `kind` sleeving, heat_shrink, label, backshell or other, `part_id`, `target` = a connector or a wire of the harness or none for the harness, `position` in words, `length_mm` of one piece, `quantity` 1 to 9999, `note`). **The contact is set where termination and function are set:** on the unit's pin (Devices tab), copied to the cable connector's pin by Generate; on a harness made by hand on the cable connector's own pin. **Rules:** an accessory whose target is not a connector or a wire of its harness is an integrity error (`dangling_accessory`, and a duplicate ID is `duplicate_id`); accessories are children of a harness for change control (`vcs/diff.py`, `vcs/lockview.py`): a released harness is locked with them; generation keeps a person's strip lengths of a wire it carries over (like label and note), the accessories whose target still exists, and reports what it cannot keep; nothing is computed from any of these values (the tool does not check that a contact fits a pin or that a strip length is sensible) and no default is invented. **Where they show:** the tables only when used (wire list *Strip A (mm)*, *Strip B (mm)*; pinouts *Contact*; a per-harness `accessories.csv`; the BOM counts contacts and accessory parts); the build workbook always (an empty value is shown as *to fill in*; a sheet *Accessories*; two NOTE lines on *Start here*). Entered in the editor (Edit wire tab, Devices tab, Connectors tab, an Accessories tab) and by `harness-design-studio import-build-data DIR FILE` (a table read by its column headings, with templates). The file format stays at schema version 1; a project that uses a new field cannot be read completely by 1.6.0 (it sets the fields aside and says so). **Status of 2026-10-22:** built and released in 1.7.0 (tag `v1.7.0`, published 2026-10-22). |
| D-171 | **Version 1.7.0: a simple workspace, built in stages (plan `docs/ait/TEST_USER_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`).** The test users of 1.6.0 said the tool felt overwhelming: too much on the screen, too much information, and they did not know what to do. The owner first planned it for 1.8.0; on 2026-10-22 the owner decided that it ships with the workbook and the build data as one release, **version 1.7.0**, and that the number 1.8.0 will not be used for this work. The owner decided on 2026-10-21 that the **simple workspace is the default** and that the full editor stays as it is (*Full*), and that the work is done in stages that the testers check one by one. **Stage S1 (built): one next step.** A new rail area **Next step** (first in the rail) shows one page, *What to do next*: a title in plain words, one sentence, **one** primary button, the six steps of the work (Add your devices, Connect them, Generate the harnesses, Check the design, Get the build sheets, Review and release) each marked with an icon and a word (*done*, *next*, *to do*; *not possible* for a read-only project), and the link **Show everything**. The step is chosen by a pure function of the facts of the project (`gui/next_step.py`, no Qt, tested for every rule and its order: a running job, a read-only project, no device, no connection, harnesses to generate or out of date (a project whose harnesses were all made by hand skips this), not checked, failed checks, files not up to date, harnesses not released, everything released); each button calls a flow that already exists (`gui/next_page.py` only shows and calls back). **The setting** *Workspace: Simple or Full* (`ui/workspace`; the View menu and the Settings page) decides where a project opens: Simple opens on **Next step**, Full where it opened before. A settings file with no stored value at all (a first run) gets Simple; a settings file that already holds values gets Full, so people who use the editor keep what they have and can switch in the View menu; the choice is stored at once. Changing the setting does not move the open page, and the Next step page can be opened in both. **Nothing written changes:** no model field, file, rule, command-line option or output; `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` stays *identical*. **Stage S2 (built): fewer things on screen.** In the Simple workspace the project tree with the Interfaces tab, the inspector and the Problems panel start closed, the minimap and the legend are off, the status bar keeps only the save state, the job indicator, *Offline* and the project path (the selection text, the counts, the *Problems* button, the pending-values link and the zoom value are hidden), and the diagram tool row shows *Select*, *Connect*, *Add device* and one **More** menu that holds every other tool (copy, delete, zoom, fit, arrange and the *Show only* filters). The rail shows Next step, Model, Checks, Drawings, Outputs, Revisions and Settings; Home is hidden (Next step does its job) but stays in the Go menu, on Ctrl+7 and in the palette. One switch, **Show panels** (top bar) or **Show side panels** (View menu, first entry), opens and closes the tree and the inspector together. Nothing is removed: every hidden thing keeps its View-menu action, shortcut and palette entry, and selecting a problem opens the inspector. The layout memory is per workspace (`ui/simple/state`, `ui/simple/minimap`, `ui/simple/legend`, `layout/<hash>/simple/...`); the Full workspace keeps its old keys and looks and behaves exactly as before, so nobody who already uses the editor loses a layout. **Not built yet:** S3 (plain words in place of jargon, the tour included) is **discarded**: the owner decided on 2026-10-22 that it is not built and not planned. S4 (a blank project asks for one thing at a time; it remains open and would go in a later version), and the measurement with the testers; the plan says that nothing claims easier use before that result exists. **Status of 2026-10-22:** stages S1 and S2 were built and released in 1.7.0 (tag `v1.7.0`, published 2026-10-22); S3 stays discarded; S4 and the measurement with the testers were not built and 1.7.0 claims no easier use. |
| D-172 | **The workshop feedback of October 2026 (plan `docs/ait/WORKSHOP_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`).** A workshop with a systems engineer, an AIT engineer and an electrical engineer gave 23 points (new devices all named DEV; the word *unit*; the Problems notification; export; RTN; editing connectors and harnesses; connectors visible in the diagram; connections limited by signal type; the person's pinout disregarded; interfaces that the user should not have to include; a library in its own window and shared; redundancy by number; an overcrowded window; two user modes). The owner decided on 2026-10-25, with the plan's recommended options: (1) **the person's pinout is the source of truth**: a link joins two connectors pin by pin with no signal type, interfaces are derived from the pinouts, labelled pins on both devices give a pairing the person confirms, Generate keeps the pinout, a review mode only checks (this reverses the interface-first design of D-10 and D-166 for projects that choose it and is built as optional fields); (2) the order of work is M1 quick fixes, M2 the connection model, M3 connector and harness editing, M4 the library window with sharing and redundancy by number, M5 the roles; (3) the **two roles replace the Simple/Full workspace** of D-171; (4) the library is shared as **a folder or repository that anyone points the tool at**, the tool stays offline (D-165, `tests/test_security.py`). Not yet answered by the owner: what "kladow" and "Sam's tool" are, and how the export failure was reached. Status of 2026-10-25: milestone M1 built (device names, the word device, problem notices, export, RTN); M2 to M5 not built. Follow-up of 2026-10-26: M2 built, see D-173; M3 built, see D-174. M4 built, see D-175; M5 built, see D-176. |
| D-173 | **Pinout first: connections between connectors** (milestone M2 of D-172, design in `docs/ait/WORKSHOP_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, "M2 design" and "M2 as built"). Follows D-172 decision 1 (the person's pinout is the source of truth) and reverses the interface-first design of D-10 and D-166 only for projects that choose it. (1) A *link* (shown as a *connection*) joins a connector of one device to a connector of another, pin by pin (`Link`: id, name, a_connector, b_connector, pairs of pins, harness_id, note; file `logical/links.json`, written only while a link exists), with no signal type; a pin has an optional `role` (power, return, ground, signal, shield, spare; empty means derived from the signal name). What a link carries is derived from the pins when shown and never stored. (2) The program **proposes the pairs by signal name** (equal names and the two ends of one line, TX with RX, never by position; output against output is not paired) and the person sees and edits them before they are saved. (3) The review (*Check my connections*, four new design rules `link-unpaired`, `link-direction`, `link-signal-mismatch`, `link-power-return`, 39 rules, and the integrity codes `dangling_link`, `link_pin_missing`, `link_pin_reused`, `link_same_device`) changes nothing. (4) **The program never creates a harness on its own**: the button *Create harness* shows what it will add and makes a harness the person owns (Generate leaves it alone; length, gauge and colour stay pending). (5) Generate keeps the person's pinout; the interface-first way stays, as *Connect with an interface type*, for projects and examples that use it. (6) A link of a released harness is locked with it. (7) Every new field is optional and left out of the files while empty, so existing projects keep byte-identical files and outputs (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` stays *identical*). Status: built after 1.7.0 on the branch `feat/m2-links` (core 20f1f4d, screens 83ac9ec, documents in the milestone's last commit), not released; follows up D-172, whose status line ("M2 to M5 not built") is now: M1 and M2 built, M3 to M5 not built. Requirements REQ-GUI-11, REQ-GUI-12, REQ-DRC-05, REQ-IO-02. |
| D-174 | **Harness and connector editing** (milestone M3 of D-172, plan `docs/ait/WORKSHOP_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, "M3 as built"). The workshop said: "should be able to edit the connector", "more editing of the harness itself (like twisting the cables)", "more harness features (like add length, gauge)" and "see the connector also on the harness itself, when in diagram view". Decisions: (1) **A cable connector can be edited** (part, gender, keying, name; the signal, role, function, termination, contact size and contact of its pins; add, rename, delete a pin) in the Connectors tab; planners in the new `core/connector_edit.py`, one undo step, a released harness refuses with the reason. (2) **On a harness that Generate made the edit locks against regeneration the same way a wire edit does**: a new sparse field `Connector.locked` (written only when true) marks the connector as the person's, and `generate/engine.py` keeps its part, gender, keying and pin details (signal, role, function, termination, contact size) while the pin IDs, their number and the contact of each pin keep following the device (the contact is set on the device's pin, as since D-170, and is refused here with that reason); the name of a generated connector follows its ID and is refused. The simplest consistent option was chosen over refusing the edit: the whole connector is the unit of the lock (not each pin), so a locked connector no longer follows later changes of the function or termination of the device pins until it is given back (`plan_reset_connector`, *Give back to Generate*); a connector that Generate cannot keep is named in `locked_data_lost`. (3) **A group's kind, wires and shield ends can be changed** for a group a person made (`plan_regroup`, `plan_set_group_ends`; *Change group…*); a group of the interface type is refused because it follows the type. (4) **Bulk edit**: *Edit all wires…* (`plan_bulk_wire_edit`: gauge, type, colour, length on all wires, the selected wires or one signal; optionally only where a value is empty) is `plan_wire_edit` for many wires in one `Put`, so the wires are locked exactly like an edited wire; label, note and ends stay single-wire edits. (5) **Connectors on the connections**: a small box at each end of a connection line (the connector name and how many of its pins the connection pairs) in Expert mode or with the new View setting *Show connectors on connections* (`ui/connection_ends`, a view of a checkable action like the others, off by default), the tooltip names the path through the harness made from it, and *Show the harness* (Edit > Selected connection, the project tree and the right-click menu of the line, which was empty until now) opens the Harness tab on it. (6) **Unchanged**: every new field is optional and left out of the files while unused, so no file or output of an existing project changes (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` stays *identical*, no golden changed). Not built: a command-line equivalent, renaming the pins of a generated connector, a per-pin lock. Status: built after 1.7.0 on the branch `feat/m3-harness-editing`, not released; follows up D-172 (status: M1, M2 and M3 built, M4 and M5 not built). Requirements REQ-HARN-02, REQ-WIRE-07, REQ-WIRE-08, REQ-GUI-13. |
| D-175 | **The library (workshop M4)** (milestone M4 of D-172, plan `docs/ait/WORKSHOP_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, "M4 as built"). The workshop said: "subsystems should be in a library, not visible in a blank project", "instead of nominal chain redundant chain rather give number of those devices", "need a separate library interface, basically opens a new window", "library should be accessible to everyone (e.g. via a shared drive)". Decisions: (1) **The library is a folder** that a person points the tool at (`File > Library folder…` and Settings > Project; the setting `library/folder`, per person, on this computer). The folder holds the device library files of D-165 (unchanged) and the new **subsystem library files**; the tool reads plain files and never uses a network: a shared library is a shared drive or a Git checkout, and keeping it current is the job of the drive or of Git (`tests/test_security.py` still forbids network code and `subprocess`). Nothing is written into the folder except on *Save to library*; a subsystem file is never replaced (choose another name, or delete the file); a device already in a device file is replaced as it was in D-165. A missing or unreadable folder, and any damaged file in it, is said in words (the other files are still listed). Files are looked for in the folder and two levels of folders below it; hidden folders and links are not followed. (2) **A subsystem is a set of devices with the links between them**: one JSON file, `harness-subsystem-library` version 1 (name, description, notes, devices as in the device file, parts, interface types, connections as in `logical/links.json` written with the connector IDs of the file; `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md`). `core/subsystem_library.py` writes (`export_subsystem`: a connection with an end outside the chosen devices is left out) and adds (`plan_subsystem_import`: devices through `plan_device_import`, so a taken ID gets the next free one; `Unit.subsystem` of each device becomes the name; connections through `plan_add_link` on the new connectors; places the devices in one lane; one undo step; all or nothing; never overwrites). `Unit.subsystem` stays free text and no field was added to the model. (3) **Blank projects hold no subsystem**: the `blank` example has no device (it never had; a test pins it) and a new project asks for subsystems only through the library. *general* stays the default subsystem name of a device that is added by hand. (4) **The library window** (`gui/library_window.py`) is a window of its own, not modal, opened by **File > Library…** (Ctrl+Shift+O, because Ctrl+Shift+L is Arrange) and the Home page: search and filter, preview (pins, or devices and connections), *Add to project* and *Save selection to library…*; it shares the controller, so an addition is one ordinary undo step; it keeps its own size and place under `library/` and does not touch the editor's layout. The Devices tab buttons *Save to library…* and *Add from library…* are unchanged (one file, one dialog). The folder is read in a helper thread. (5) **Redundancy is a number of devices** (`core/copies.py`): *Number of these devices* in the Add device window, the Devices tab (with *Add copies*) and the library window. The number is not stored; it is counted from the devices that exist. The second device is `ID-R`, which is exactly what *Create redundant copy* has made since 1.0 (`edit.ops_redundant_copy`, now with a `suffix`), so a project and its outputs are the same whichever way it was made; the third and later are `ID-3`, `ID-4` ..., on the redundant chain, with the same connectors and the links mirrored where the other end has a free connector (the others are named). The simplest plain option was chosen over `ID-2` because `-2` is what the library import and Duplicate use for a clash. The number only grows (delete a copy to have fewer; the tool never deletes by a number), at most 10. The old way (*Create redundant copy*, the Chain field, *Fix* of the cross-strap warning) stays and is the same as setting the number to 2. (6) **Unchanged**: no field of the model, no file and no output of a project that does not use the library or a number changes (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` stays *identical*, no golden changed). Not built: a library index or a lock for two people writing to one folder at once (the drive or Git decides), versions of a subsystem, a number from the command line, per-device quantity inside a subsystem, replacing a subsystem file from the window. Status: built after 1.7.0 on the branch `feat/m4-library`, not released; follows up D-172 (status then: M1 to M4 built, M5 not built; M5 is D-176). Requirements REQ-LIB-05, REQ-LIB-06, REQ-EDIT-03. |
| D-176 | **Roles replace Simple/Full (workshop M5)** (milestone M5 of D-172, plan `docs/ait/WORKSHOP_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, "M5 as built"). The workshop said: "basically the tool should have two user modes, one for systems engineers and the other for electrical/AIT engineers" and "the tool generally is just too overcrowded". The owner decided on 2026-10-25 (D-172, decision 3) that **the two roles replace the Simple/Full workspace** of D-171. Decisions: (1) **Two roles**, **Systems engineer** (thinks in devices, the connections between devices, subsystems, the library, the checks and the Next step page; no pinout, wire or build detail first) and **Electrical / AIT engineer** (connectors, pinouts, harnesses, wires, build sheets and tests; sees everything). The word *Simple*, *Full* and *workspace* are gone from the screen, the menus, Settings, the Home and Next step pages, the tour and the documents. (2) **What each role shows.** The Systems engineer role is the Simple workspace of stage S2 (side panels closed, minimap, legend and status counts off, tools tucked under *More*, problem notices off, a project opens on Next step) and its rail lists Next step, Model, Checks, Revisions and Settings: **Home, Drawings and Outputs are left out** (`areas.SYSTEMS_HIDDEN`; Simple left out only Home); the Connections group of the tree and the library stay (the Next step page has the buttons *Library…* and *Show everything* for this role). The Electrical / AIT engineer role is the Full workspace: every area, panels open, notices on, a project opens where it did. Nothing a role leaves out is gone: it is in a menu with its key, in the command palette, in the buttons of the Next step page (**Go to Releases**, **Open the Outputs**), and the rail lists the area that is open. A role is a view, never a permission: it locks nothing, is never written into a project (a test compares the project files after a switch) and the command line has no role. (3) **Where it is set.** *View > Role* (a submenu with two exclusive checkable actions `act-role-systems` and `act-role-electrical`) and a *Role* row of the Settings page (`settings-role`, a view of the same actions) switch **at once**, with no restart, as the workspace switch did; the workspace switch had no shortcut, so none was kept. The setting is `ui/role` (`systems` or `electrical`). (4) **Stored layout is kept.** The per-role layout memory reuses the keys of the workspaces (`role_key`: the Electrical / AIT engineer keeps the plain keys that Full had, the Systems engineer the `ui/simple/...` and `layout/<hash>/simple/...` keys of Simple), so nobody loses a layout; the internal name `simple` stays in those keys and nowhere else. (5) **Existing users.** A settings file that holds values and no role becomes **Electrical / AIT engineer** without a dialog; one that held `ui/workspace` = `simple` (a choice of 1.7.0) becomes Systems engineer, because that was their choice (the old key is read once and left alone). (6) **The first start** (a settings file with nothing stored) shows a small modal window *Who are you working as?* with two big buttons, each with one plain sentence, and *You can change this later in View > Role.*, before the tour; closing it without choosing means Electrical / AIT engineer. Tests and CI set a role through the hook `roles.FIRST_RUN_ROLE` (the successor of `FIRST_RUN_WORKSPACE`; `tests/conftest.py` sets `electrical`), and then the window never asks; a window built with `first_run=False` (the screenshot and benchmark tools) never asks either. (7) **The tour** got a last step that points at the rail and says that what it lists depends on the role. (8) **Rename.** The code says role, systems and electrical (`gui/roles.py`, `gui/role_dialog.py`, `MainWindow.set_role`, `_apply_role`, `ask_role_flow`); `tests/test_ui_simple_workspace.py` became `tests/test_ui_role_layout.py`; the picture files `simple-workspace.png` and `full-workspace.png` became `systems-engineer.png` and `electrical-engineer.png`; the user page `use-the-simple-workspace.md` became `choose-your-role.md` (no pointer page was kept: every link was updated). (9) **Unchanged:** no model field, file, rule, command-line option or output (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` stays *identical*). Not built: S4 (a blank project that asks one thing at a time), a role in the command line, roles that restrict anything, filtering the tabs of the side panels by role, and a measurement with people. Status: built after 1.7.0 on the branch `feat/m5-roles` (from `feat/m4-library`), not released; closes the five milestones of D-172. Requirements REQ-GUI-14, REQ-GUI-15; revises REQ-GUI-07 and REQ-GUI-09. |
| D-177 | **The audit of 1.8.0 and the 1.8.1 trust fixes** (2026-10-27). Six independent read-only auditors (core and file format, generation/rules/outputs, command line/security/packaging, editor, tests and code quality, product and documentation) audited master after 1.8.0; the report is `docs/AUDIT_1.8.0.md`. Verdict 3.6 of 5, no Critical finding. Decisions taken for the fix release 1.8.1: (1) **fixes only**: no new feature, no file-format change, no new design rule (39 stay); the files and outputs of a project that does not meet one of the cases are unchanged and `output_hashes` against 1.2.0 still prints *identical*. (2) **The new integrity checks are warnings**, not errors (`box_connector_mated_twice`, `box_pin_wired_twice`, `link_harness_not_mated`, `link_harness_shared`), because an integrity error blocks saving and rolls an edit back; the one error is `link_wire_mismatch` in the verifier (a harness made from a link that no longer carries the link's pairs). (3) **"Not checked", not "pass"** when a rule has nothing to evaluate, by notes of the existing `unchecked-config` kind. (4) **The build workbook** says `yes (values rest on placeholder configuration: ...)`: the only written text that changes for placeholder projects (the test goldens of the workbook cells changed; the 1.2.0 proof leaves the workbook out). (5) **The library never replaces a different device without asking**; a part's approval is never lowered; `export-devices` needs `--replace`. (6) **A segment length of zero stays accepted** (pinned by `tests/test_limits.py`); two rows that give one segment different lengths are refused. (7) **pytest-timeout 2.4.0 (MIT)** is a development tool (dev extra, `compliance/docs/SRF.md`). Not done, for the owner to decide: a marker that makes an older tool open a project with links read-only (A-3, a file-format question), gating the release workflow on a green CI run and a vulnerability scan (C-2, C-3, cannot be tested locally), the larger items of the report (buses, variants, import of existing wire lists, command-line coverage of the 1.8.0 features, a complete Systems-engineer path). |

| D-187 | **Waivers carry `finding_hash` (release 1.11, first workstream; owner: "continue as per your recommendations", 2026-10-29, D-186).** Audit finding B-13: a waiver was bound to the finding ID (`<rule>.<object>`) only, so a finding that changed later (another value, another name in its text) stayed waived. `Waiver.finding_hash` (sparse, 12 hex characters) is the first 12 characters of the SHA-256 of the rule and the finding's own statement (`Hit.title`); the explanation (`why`, `how`) is not part of it, so rewording a rule's help text does not reopen waivers. `checks.with_waiver` applies a waiver only if it has no hash or the hash matches; otherwise the finding is open with `waiver_changed` set and the report lists it. The logical findings and the design rules use the one function. No waiver in the shipped examples, so no written file of the examples changes. The Problems panel card of such a finding names the earlier reason and says that the waiver no longer applies, and the *Waiver* column of the Checks panel reads "Waiver no longer fits" (owner, 2026-10-29: as recommended). |
| D-188 | **Pair-part counts (release 1.11, second workstream; owner, 2026-10-29: (a), a pair part is bought per metre of whole cable).** Audit finding B-05: the BOM, the mass and the cut list counted every conductor of a pair part, so a twisted pair showed 2× and a quad 4× the cable. `Part.conductors` (whole number 1 to 64, default 1, sparse) says how many conductors one metre of a wire part carries; each wire that names the part is one conductor of it, so the BOM adds `length / conductors` per wire and the mass uses `mass_per_m_g * length / conductors` (`mass_per_m_g` is the mass of one metre of the part). The independent output check counts the same way. The starter part `EX-WIRE-TWISTED-SHIELDED` has `conductors` 2, so `twisted_shielded_pair` and `twinax` count once and a `quad` (mapped to the same part) counts two pairs; the parts import reads a *Conductors* column for wire parts. Not changed: the per-wire cut list and the wire list stay one row per conductor (a technician strips and crimps each conductor), and the tool does not guess `conductors` for a project's own parts: a project that has not set it counts as before. Output change: the shipped examples' BOM, mass table and `export.json` change; every file of every example also carries a new model hash because the parts are part of the model (checked: with hashes masked, 420 text files compared, only these differ). The baseline is renewed on purpose (D-183). |

| D-191 | **The transparency work: the owner's decisions of 2026-11-02, and step 0.** The owner asked for a tool that is transparent, traceable and customisable for experienced systems, electrical and AIT engineers (14 requirements: an Explain view, an assumptions panel, externalised configuration, selectable models, units everywhere, workflow visibility, flagged overrides, change history, provenance, open data, a scriptable core, explainable warnings, extensibility, visible data links). Phase 0 was an audit (`docs/transparency_audit.md` and its appendices, pull request 141; read-only). On 2026-11-02 the owner answered "all as per your recommendations" to the ten decisions of its section 12: (1) **a timestamp and the operator go in a separate run record outside the file hashes and the baselines**, so the same design still gives byte-identical files; (2) **the configuration stays JSON, with a description, unit and source for every key** (no YAML, no new dependency); (3) **an error can carry a recorded deviation** (reason, author, date, approval, expiry) that keeps it visible on every output, for the electrical rules at least; (4) **today's defaults stay as they are and are made visible and selectable**; a change of a default that moves a number needs the owner's approval case by case and is recorded in `docs/changes.md`; (5) **extension is declarative first (rules and tables as data), Python entry points second, both opt-in with a trust prompt**; (6) the Electrical and AIT roles may become two, and capturing as-built data, serial and lot numbers and measured results is in scope; (7) a schema-version bump with a migration is accepted where a step needs it, and a project that does not use a feature stays byte-identical; (8) one release per step or two; (9) standards are cited by ID and title, never with the standard's text; (10) the order of the plan in section 11 of the audit stands. Ground rules for every step: regression tests that capture the numbers first (`tests/test_numbers_snapshot.py`, `tools/numbers_snapshot.py`), no number changes unless a defect is found and the owner agrees, every moved result recorded in `docs/changes.md`, the default screen stays clean, and every model, rule and assumption is documented for engineers in `docs/models/`. **Step 0 (WP0)** is this change: the numbers snapshot, `docs/changes.md`, `validate` runs the check of the configuration values (defect D1) and the bundle table is cited as the standard only when it is (D4). Not decided yet and asked again when its step comes: whether AWG is shown with mm² (D2) and how `import-lengths` shows the converted metres (D8). |

| D-194 | **Version 1.12.0** (2026-11-04; decisions D-191 to D-193; there is no row D-192 in this file). The transparency release: the whole of the transparency work (stages S1 to S10 and the work before them; `docs/transparency_plan.md`, `docs/transparency_final_audit.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` section 1.12.0): explanations of every number and rule, the settings and assumptions page, selectable models (`config/model.json`), overrides with a reason, the change journal and recorded deviations, open data and the run record, import records and units on import, the scripting interface and `--json`, extensions (data first, Python plug-ins behind enable and trust), the workflow shown, as-built capture for AIT and the three roles; the cross-section in mm² beside every AWG gauge on screen (D2) and the echo of converted values on import (D8). The version number, the changelog section, the release page text `docs/releases/1.12.0.md`, the regenerated example projects, fixtures and golden outputs (the version stamp and the model hash that follows from it) and the status lines are prepared in a pull request; the owner has not signed it off and nothing is tagged by the agent. Published on 2026-11-04 as the tag `v1.12.0` on commit `c8ecf3a` (release workflow run 41802552651; `compliance/SIGNOFF.md`). **A minor release (1.12.0) and not 1.11.2 or 2.0.0 because the file format is additive:** `schema_version` stays 1 and there is no migration; the new files are optional (`config/model.json`, `imports/records.json`, `journal.json`, `asbuilt/records.json` and the folder `extensions/`), a project that does not use them is written as before, and none of them is part of the model hash. | D-200 | **The programme towards 2.0.0: the owner's decisions of 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-06** (plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md`; nothing of it is built yet). The owner chose three themes: a cable-first, free-form harness workflow, team and process features, and a rebuilt user experience. **Model decisions (2026-11-05):** (1) the project file format may change with an automatic migration (`schema_version` 2, taken once, as 2.0.0, with a safety net: backup, dry-run, `MIGRATION.json`, self-check, rollback; the model hash does not include `schema_version`); (2) a loose cable end is a saved object (a connector with the new role `loose`, in the model hash); (3) when a harness drawn by hand and the interfaces disagree, whichever came first is the source of truth (`Harness.origin` is `interfaces`, `link` or `drawn`, and Generate never touches a harness whose origin is not `interfaces`). **Answers of 2026-11-06, all as the plan recommends:** (a) sign-off strictness: the default is *Recorded* (sign-offs exist, nothing is enforced), *Enforced* (three different people for the three roles) is a project setting, and the person is authenticated by the Git host (code owners, protected branch, signed commits), not by a key inside the tool; (b) who may approve: a role stays a view and never a permission, the tool gets no user accounts, and "may approve" is a named list in a project setting (`config/naming.json`, like `release_distinct_roles`); (c) parts: the library folder is the team's working copy, InvenTree stays the master where there is one, and the two exchange by file and a separate script; the tool gets no live network connection, so the offline guarantee holds; (d) the installer-signing certificate: an internal certificate through the owner's IT if there is one, otherwise a cloud signing service whose eligibility is confirmed first; the licence and signing items (D-02, T-19, postponed by D-186 until the tool is given to anyone outside) are to be reopened in writing before the signed-installer milestone, and until then the packages stay unsigned; (e) a harness with an unresolved loose end cannot be released, with a blocker `loose_end_unresolved` that can be waived with a recorded reason; (f) the screen position of a loose end is not in the model hash (it goes to `diagram/marks.json`, like a label), so dragging one never makes outputs stale; its existence and connection are in the hash. The remaining recommendations of section 5.2 of the plan stand until the owner says otherwise: 2.0.0 is tagged only when the loose ends, the origin, format 2 and the journal layout are done and the owner's own projects have run through release candidates; one small release for each milestone; the default mass model is left alone and splices go into the opt-in `full_bom` model only. The first step is the migration safety net, which changes no format. No compliance claim is made. |
| D-201 | **Loose cable ends in the model: M4a of the programme towards 2.0.0** (spike `docs/ait/SPIKE_M4_LOOSE_END.md`, plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` 3.2; the owner said *continue as per your recommendations*, 2026-11-08). **Spike findings, in short:** a loose end that is an ordinary connector (role `loose`, part `TBD-CONNECTOR`, no `mates_with`) is carried by every part of the tool with no change to the readers of the wires; a stored `Harness.origin` changes the model hash, the content hash and `system/export.json` and the migration safety net (D-200, M1) refuses it; the first cable made the generated plans look stale (the part list is in their input hash) and the parts waiver silently covered `TBD-CONNECTOR`; every output wording exists twice (the generator and `core/outputs/verify.py`); a `Link` joins two device connectors only, so a half-dragged cable is a harness, never a link. **Decisions taken (1 to 8):** (1) **the origin is derived, not stored**: `interfaces` when `Harness.generated`, else `link` when a `Link.harness_id` names it, else `drawn` (`core/origin.py`); no field, so every existing hash and the v1 to v2 step are untouched, and a connection that names a harness Generate makes is the integrity warning `origin_mismatch` (a warning and not an error, so that a project that already has such a link is not blocked); (2) the wording of the spike: part column `TBD-CONNECTOR (loose end)`, Mates with `open end`, label `W009-P2 open end`, BOM one line `(open end)` with approval *to fill in* and the number of ends, DRC *N cable end(s) have no connector chosen yet (loose end)*, build workbook *to fill in* (the words in the written outputs are M4b); (3) **unplug** resets the part to `TBD-CONNECTOR` and the role to `loose` (pins and wires stay), and the unplug of a harness made from a connection is refused with a plain reason; (4) giving a loose end a real part resolves it (role `cable`), and so does plugging it into a port; (5) a loose end blocks the **release** (blocker `loose_end_unresolved`, waivable with a recorded reason, `--accept-loose-ends`, kept in the change log and the baseline), not *submit for review*, and `--accept-unapproved-parts` does not cover it; (6) plugging by number onto a port pin of another name is refused with the pins named, never mismatched silently; (7) a loose end is a design-rule **warning** under the existing rule `part-unapproved` (no new rule, 39 rules); (8) the format number moves in a separate final step by the lead: M4a wires the real v1 to v2 step (the number only) and proves it with the safety net and `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1. The position of an end is the mark `pos:<connector id>` in `diagram/marks.json` (outside the hash). **Built in M4a:** the model (role `loose`, the part `TBD-CONNECTOR` added on first use, left out of the generation input hash and of the part pickers), the plans in `core/manual_harness.py` (cable from a port, plug, unplug, resolve, add a loose end), three integrity errors, the release blocker and flag, the ninth readiness check *Ends not decided* (generator and verifier), the DRC wording, the empty migration step, and the tests `tests/test_loose_end*.py`. **Not built:** the words of a loose end in the pinout and connector tables, labels, BOM, mass, test tables, as-built sheet, drawing and the Connectors sheet of the build workbook (M4b); the canvas, the project tree, the inspector, the release dialog field for the reason and the connector editor (M4c); the version bump. No compliance claim is made. |
| D-202 | **Version 1.17.0** (2026-11-09). The safety-net release: the first two steps of the programme towards 2.0 (D-200, plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md`), made before any new file format exists (`CHANGELOG.md` section 1.17.0). **M1, the migration safety net (pull request 193):** the model hash and the content hash no longer depend on `schema_version` (a test holds the hashes of 16 projects made before the rule; upgrading to a later format changes no hash, no 12-character stamp and no baseline); `harness-design-studio migrate DIR --dry-run` loads the project, upgrades it in memory and writes nothing (files that would change, objects by kind before and after, model hash before and after, a full export before and after, anything lost; exit 0 only if nothing would be lost); `migrate` copies every original file to `.migration-backup-v<N>/`, writes the record `MIGRATION.json` there (from and to format, tool version, SHA-256 of every file before and after, model hash, counts), **checks itself** after the write (model hash, findings of `validate` and `check`, baselines of released harnesses, a byte-identical export) and **puts every file back from the backup**, and says so, if anything differs or the write fails; a run killed half-way is put right by the next one; the editor asks once when it opens a project in an older format (*Upgrade*, *Open read-only*, *Cancel*) and no longer upgrades it at the first save without asking. A migration generates nothing and makes no ID; a project with a journal gets one entry, `migrated from format N`. **M2, a real-platform test lane and a click budget (pull request 194; tests and CI only):** a separate GitHub check, *harness-design-studio real platform*, runs the layout audit, the sweep over every dialog and window at the interface sizes (100 to 200 %) and font sizes (+2 and +4 pt) and the mouse tests of connecting two devices on Qt's real `xcb` platform (a virtual X display at 100 % and 125 %) and `windows` platform (the Windows runner, whose screen the job first raises to 1920 x 1080); a test holds the quick path (New project, Add device twice, Connect, Export diagram) to a click budget of 11 clicks and 5 dialogs. **A minor release (1.17.0) because of new commands and options, not a patch release; the file format stays 1 and there is no migration for a format-1 project, no new field, no new file, no calculation and no written output changes** (the design rules are still 39): the version number, the changelog section, the release page text `docs/releases/1.17.0.md`, the regenerated example projects, fixtures and golden outputs (the version stamp and the model hash that follows from it, because the model hash includes `generation.generator_version`) and the status lines were prepared in a pull request (branch `release/1.17.0-prep`). `1.10.0`, `1.16.0` and `1.17.0` have the same string length, so the stored baseline of the written files keeps `BASE_VERSION` 1.10.0 and is not written again (D-183); `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` print *identical*. The packages stay unsigned (D-186); no compliance claim is made. |
| D-203 | **Offline and security evidence kit** (after 1.17.0; colleagues of the owner asked for evidence of the offline nature, of data security and of *no secret telemetry* that does not rest on our word). **(1) The offline audit of the built package** (`tools/offline_audit.py`, REQ-OFFLINE-02, REQ-OFFLINE-03): a whole workflow on the `.tar.gz` or `.deb` that users download, once in a network namespace without network (`unshare -rn`, `bwrap --unshare-net` or `sudo unshare -n`, whichever works; the probe inside is in the report) and once under `strace -f` with the normal network; every socket family, connect target, program started, file written, library mapped as code, network configuration file read and network-capable file of the package is judged against `tools/offline_audit_allow.json`, a reviewed allow-list with one reason per entry; canary words planted in the project are searched for in every write, file, output and trace. JSON and Markdown report; exit 1 on a finding. CI: the workflow `harness-offline-audit.yml` (not a required check) on every change of `src`, `packaging` or `tools`, and a step of `release.yml` that attaches the report to the release. The Windows counterpart (`tools/offline_audit_windows.py`, REQ-OFFLINE-06) uses firewall rules and the Windows audit events, since Windows has no strace and no network namespace. **(2) The packages lose the network-capable files they never load** (REQ-OFFLINE-04): Qt's VNC platform plug-in (a VNC server) and TUIO touch plug-in (a UDP listener), the Qt network library that only those two need, the Kerberos libraries that only it needs, Python's TLS module with OpenSSL's TLS library, and the multiprocessing start-up hook of PyInstaller (it imported Python's socket module at every start of the packaged program; nothing of the program uses multiprocessing). They are deleted after PyInstaller (`strip_network_capable`) and `check_package` refuses a package that still has one; a file that stays and needs a removed library stops the build. Linux package of this checkout: 8 files, 3.75 MB, removed; the program folder went from 234.6 MB to 230.4 MB and the `.tar.gz` from 92.8 MB to 90.8 MB; the self-test passes. Kept with a reason on the allow-list: Python's socket module (the standard library must be able to import it) and OpenSSL's crypto library where Python's hashlib uses it. **(3) Python plug-ins can be switched off on a computer** (REQ-OFFLINE-05, `core/extensions/policy.py`): the machine-wide policy file `/etc/harness-design-studio/policy.json` or `%ProgramData%\HarnessDesignStudio\policy.json` (`{"python_plugins": "off"}`; fails closed: a file that does not say exactly `"allowed"` switches them off), or the environment variable `HARNESS_DESIGN_STUDIO_NO_PLUGINS`. The path of the file is fixed so that no user setting can move it; the switch can only take away. The default is unchanged. **Found by the audit, not changed here (owner to decide):** the recent-projects list of the editor's settings file keeps each project's name; openpyxl writes the worksheets of a build workbook to temporary files of the system's temporary folder while it writes the workbook. Both are on the allow-list with that reason and in `docs/SECURITY.md`. No written output, model hash or golden changes. The guide for colleagues: `docs/VERIFY_OFFLINE.md`. | Evidence that others can rerun is stronger than a test of our own source; removing code the program never runs makes the claim smaller to check; an IT department needs a switch it controls, not one each user can change. |
| D-204 | **Loose cable ends in the editor: M4c of the programme towards 2.0.0** (D-200, D-201; plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` 3.2; REQ-GUI-28). **How it relates to connecting by dragging (REQ-GUI-21):** a cable dragged from a port and let go **on a port or a device** still makes a **connection** (`Link`) exactly as before (the window *Link connectors*, or at once in Quick mode); let go **on empty ground** it is no longer a draft of the window but a **saved harness drawn by hand** whose far end is loose (`core.manual_harness.plan_cable_from_port`, origin `drawn`, one undo step, the place of the end in the mark `pos:<connector id>` outside the model hash). A half-dragged cable is a harness and never a link (the spike's finding), so the two gestures make two different things on purpose: a connection says which devices talk; a cable drawn by hand is a physical harness from the start. Esc while a cable is in hand still saves nothing. The end of a **connection** picked up and let go on empty ground stays a draft of the window (a connection always joins two connectors); it is dropped by Esc, a click on empty ground and a save, as before. **On the diagram** a saved loose end is drawn on a line out of the port its harness plugs into (the first plugged connector of the harness, by ID) as a plug with a dashed outline (the written drawing of M4b draws it dashed too) and the words *open end*; a click picks it, a drag moves it (only the mark changes) or plugs it into a port (`plan_plug_loose_end`; on a device as a whole the connector whose pin names fit, `core/cable_ends.plug_port`); the further plugged ends of a harness drawn by hand are lines between ports; a harness with no plugged end has no place on the diagram and is reached from the tree and the menu; a harness made from a connection is drawn by its connection's line, plus its loose ends. **Elsewhere:** the project tree lists loose ends under their harness; the inspector shows the origin of every harness and the fields of a cable end (name, part, plug, unplug); *Edit > Cable ends* and the right-click menus are the way without dragging; the release window asks for the reason of `--accept-loose-ends`; the problem card and the Next step page word the loose end as a choice, never as a part to approve (`part_approval.approval_blocker` refuses the stand-in part). **One core change found by the journey unplug then re-plug:** an end that was plugged and unplugged has every pin of the port, the spare ones too, and the plug refused it (*Pin 5 ... has no partner*); `plan_plug_loose_end` now puts a pin the proposal leaves over on the free port pin of its own number when the names do not differ (a pin of another name is still refused). **Not changed:** `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1; a project without a loose end keeps byte-identical files and outputs (`tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` print *identical*); no new file, field or rule. **Limits:** nobody has looked at it on a real display (offscreen tests and the real-platform mouse helpers only); the keyboard way chooses from lists, there is no keyboard way to move an end on the diagram; a re-plug of the plugged far end of a drawn cable by dragging is not built (unplug, then plug). No compliance claim is made. |
| D-205 | **Licence of `HarnessDesignStudio/`: GPL-3.0-only (the owner's decision L-1 of 2026-11-09). This closes D-02 and the licence part of D-143.** The code, tests, tools, packaging and documents in `HarnessDesignStudio/` are licensed under the GNU General Public Licence, version 3 only (SPDX `GPL-3.0-only`). The package metadata says `license = "GPL-3.0-only"`; every source file has an `SPDX-License-Identifier` header; `HarnessDesignStudio/LICENSE` holds the full text so that the folder is complete on its own; the root `LICENSE` (WireViz's GPL-3.0) is unchanged; the About dialog shows the licence text; every package holds the licence file. The placeholder `LicenseRef-Proprietary` and the sentence "no licence is granted" are removed. The rationale of D-02 (dependencies that allow closed internal use) and of D-01 ("keeps new code free of GPL") no longer holds; the dependency allow-list now follows GPL-3.0-only compatibility. | Owner decision L-1: one licence for the whole repository; compatible with the GPL dependencies and with the WireViz code in the fork; sections 15 and 16 of the GPL-3.0 give the warranty disclaimer and the limit of liability. Whether the 20 releases already public (1.0.0 to 1.17.0) are changed or superseded is not decided here (`compliance/legal/owner-checklist.md` B1). |
| D-206 | **The project is and stays free and non-commercial (L-2).** No prices, paid support, donations, sponsorship, advertisements or services; no `FUNDING.yml`, no sponsor links, no company name. A change of this decision reopens D-205 first. | Owner decision L-2. Keeps the project outside the EU Product Liability Directive (EU) 2024/2853 and the Cyber Resilience Act (EU) 2024/2847, which exclude free and open-source software supplied outside a commercial activity, and avoids consumer-law and business-imprint duties. Whether that holds is a lawyer question (`compliance/legal/lawyer-questions.md` Q-4, Q-7). |
| D-207 | **The app and the website collect no personal data and have no telemetry (L-3).** The app makes no network connection, has no update check and no crash upload; the website sets no cookie, stores nothing in the browser and loads nothing from another host. | Owner decision L-3. Supplying software in exchange for personal data can count as commercial activity; it also avoids privacy-law duties. The no-network test (`tests/test_offline.py`) keeps this true. |
| D-208 | **Contributions are accepted only under GPL-3.0-only with a Developer Certificate of Origin sign-off (L-4).** `CONTRIBUTING.md` says so; a CI check fails a pull request whose commits lack the `Signed-off-by` line. | Owner decision L-4. Keeps the origin of every contribution documented and the owner's rights to the owner's own code clear. |
| D-209 | **The website is hosted on GitHub Pages at the default `github.io` address; no custom domain (L-5).** | Owner decision L-5. No cost, no registration data, no risk of a lapsed domain being taken over. Nothing is deployed before the owner's explicit OK. |
| D-210 | **One text, used everywhere (rule 6 of the legal brief of 2026-11-09).** Every legal text (disclaimer, intended use, project status, "not independently reviewed", AI authorship, copyright scope, licence, relation to WireViz, non-affiliation, trademark note, app privacy, output notice) is written once in `compliance/legal/texts.md`. `python -m tools.gen_legal_texts` writes `src/harness_design_studio/core/legal_texts.py` from it. The READMEs, the app, the written outputs, the release notes and the website copy the text word for word. `tests/test_legal_texts.py` fails when a copy differs from the source or when a place that must carry a text does not. A second check (`tests/test_promise_words.py`) fails on promise words in user-facing text, with an allow-list. | Owner brief, rule 6 and rule 7. One place to change a text, and a test to catch drift. |
