| D-213 | **Third-party components, SBOM, source offer and signing of the packages.** (1) `tools/third_party.py` makes the inventory of every package **from the built package** (the Python packages named in its `LICENSES/README.txt` and `.dist-info` folders, the Python runtime, Qt and its modules, ICU, each shared library of the operating system found with `dpkg -S` and its Debian package version, fonts, icons, the PyInstaller bootloader; for Windows the DLLs by a table), writes the SBOM (CycloneDX), the inventory (Markdown and JSON) and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` (name, version, licence, copyright notice and licence text of each component; a source offer valid for at least three years; how to replace the libraries), and fails on a component with an unknown licence or one that is not on the allow-list. The build (`tools/build_installer.py`, `tools/build_windows.py`) puts `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` into every package; CI (`harness-ci.yml`) and the release workflow compare the inventory with the committed one in `compliance/legal/` (a library that is new, or gone, is a failure). (2) The licence allow-list follows D-205: permissive, LGPL, MPL-2.0 and GPL-3.0 are allowed; GPL-2.0-only, AGPL, unknown and mixed texts are rejected (the policy before D-205 was "permissive or LGPL only"). The licence of each Ubuntu library is in a reviewed table in the tool; a library that is not in it has no known licence. (3) `tools/fetch_sources.py` fetches the corresponding source of the Qt for Python, Qt (qtbase, qtsvg, qtwayland) and Ubuntu libraries in the versions of the package (Qt: from download.qt.io, checked against the sums Qt publishes; Ubuntu: `apt-get source`); the release workflow attaches it to the release page, where the notices file says it is (*amended by D-232:* from 2.0.1 the source is in the bundle `harness-design-studio-<version>-release-bundle.tar` on the release page, folder `sources/`, and the notices file says so). (4) Every release is signed without a key (Sigstore, `cosign sign-blob`, the OIDC identity of the workflow) and has build provenance attestations; `SHA256SUMS`, the SBOMs, the inventory and the notices are attached (*amended by D-232:* from 2.0.1 the SBOMs, the inventories, the notices, the offline-audit report and the signatures of the packages and the SBOMs are in that bundle, and the page carries the four packages, `SHA256SUMS`, `SHA256SUMS.sigstore.json` and the bundle). The release pages say "signed" from `SIGNED_FROM` (1.17.1) on; the releases up to 1.17.0 stay unsigned and say so. A Windows code-signing certificate stays optional (D-19). **Untested until the first release:** the workflow steps for the sources, signing and attestation could not be run before a release; the owner decides when to make one. **Open and not decided here:** pruning the Ubuntu libraries the package does not use (about 43 come only from an optional GTK theme plug-in and from `libQt6Network`), the Microsoft and Mesa redistribution terms of the Windows packages, and the notices of the code inside Qt (`compliance/legal/owner-checklist.md`, `lawyer-questions.md` Q-11). | The legal brief of 2026-11-09, steps 2.5 and 2.9; findings TPC-1 to TPC-19 and SEC-6 of the audit. |

A lock with another id, including any lock file written before this decision, counts as held by another computer (on Linux, unless its boot id is this computer's) and keeps the existing way out (delete the lock file; the editor offers to open read-only). Files of this version also say `"host": "not recorded"`, so an older version leaves them alone. The run record describes Windows with `platform.win32_ver()`, because `platform.platform()` asks for the computer name there; the program now imports no network module on any system, and the Windows allowance of `socket` in `tools/offline_audit_allow.json` is removed. | D-225 | **Version 1.17.1 is the full release of what the release candidate 1.17.1rc2 tested** (D-218 (7): a release candidate first, then the full release). It is the first full release with signed packages. The program is the same as in 1.17.1rc2, which was published on 2026-11-11 as a pre-release (tag `v1.17.1rc2` on `7fae805`); since then only the source step of the release workflow changed: a download is tried again after a network error (pull request 215, merged on 2026-11-11 as `0cdfa63`; it is part of 1.17.1, while the run of 1.17.1rc2 did not have it). Its changelog section says in short what changed since 1.17.0 and points to the sections 1.17.1rc2 and 1.17.1rc1 for the full text; `docs/releases/1.17.1.md` covers everything since 1.17.0, and its notes say that it supersedes the releases 1.0.0 to 1.17.0 (D-218 (4), option (b)). The 1.17.1rc1 and 1.17.1rc2 sections of the changelog and their release page texts stay as history. `1.17.1` is shorter than `1.17.1rc2`; the stored hashes are written again with it and `BASE_VERSION` in `tools/output_hashes.py` is `1.17.1` (`docs/changes.md`, 2026-11-11). The third-party inventory and the SBOMs of `1.17.1` are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published 1.17.1rc2 packages (their SHA-256 sums checked against the `SHA256SUMS` of that release), which carry the network stripping of #204; they differ from the 1.17.1rc2 files only by the version. The release workflow compares them with the packages it builds. **Published on 2026-11-11** as a full release and the latest release, the lightweight tag `v1.17.1` (no tag object) on `cb72a3c` (the merge of #217), created by the owner at 23:06:18 UTC, most likely by publishing the release on the GitHub website; release workflow run 43117202544, first attempt: every job passed, with the keyless Sigstore signing and the build provenance attestation; the offline-audit report on the page says PASS. A text fault of the Windows install step of the page was fixed by pull request 219 (the template) and the `notes_only` run 43125894334 (the page, 2026-11-12). The three faults of the screen that the end-to-end test of 1.17.1 found are fixed in #218, after the tag. Not done: the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision), the Windows packages on a real computer, a look at the program on a real display, the owner's sign-off, the release compliance record (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 1.17.1*). | The release workflow ran to the end for 1.17.1rc2: the packages were signed and attested and the sources were attached (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 1.17.1rc2*). A full release needs a new version, because the tag `v1.17.1rc2` exists (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 1). The tag `v1.17.1` is pushed only after this change is merged and CI is green on the merge commit (step 15). The owner asked for the full release on 2026-11-11; that is the release decision. The signature and the attestation of 1.17.1rc2 were not checked from outside the workflow; the owner decided on 2026-11-11 to skip that check, so it is not an action for 1.17.1. Step 14 says to remove `rcN` only after the owner's sign-off: the owner has not signed this release off as a compliance record, as for every 1.x release, and its notes claim no sign-off. |
| D-226 | **Format 2: the project file format moves from 1 to 2** (M4 of the programme towards 2.0.0; D-200, D-201, D-202, D-204; plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` 2.3 to 2.5 and 3.2; REQ-MIGRATE-05; not in a release yet, the program version stays 1.17.1). **What format 2 is:** format 1 with the number 2 (`SCHEMA_VERSION` 2). It is the format that may hold a loose cable end, a connector of a harness with the role `loose` and the library part `TBD-CONNECTOR` (D-201), whose place on the diagram is the mark `pos:<connector id>` in `diagram/marks.json`, outside the model hash. The origin of a harness (`interfaces`, `link`, `drawn`) stays worked out and is not stored (D-201 (1)): the plan's sparse field `Harness.origin` (2.4) is not written, because a stored value changes the model hash, the content hash and `system/export.json` of a project with a hand-made harness, and the safety net refuses that. The step `_v1_to_v2` of `core/io/migrate.py` writes the number and nothing else; it runs the parts of the tuple `V1_TO_V2_PARTS` (now one, `_v2_number`). **Format 2 is not frozen until 2.0.0 is tagged.** The owner decided on 2026-11-11 that the mergeable journal (M7a, `journal.jsonl`) is part of 2.0.0; it follows in its own pull request before 2.0.0rc1 and goes into format 2 as one more part of the step from 1 to 2, not as a format 3. A project that a build of `master` wrote in format 2 before that part exists still has `journal.json`; M7a decides whether its part recognises the old layout by the files (so that it also runs for such a project) or not (no release writes format 2 before 2.0.0rc1). After the tag, only sparse fields. **How a format 1 project is upgraded:** as M1 built it (D-202). The editor asks once when it opens one (*This project is in format 1. Upgrade? A copy of the original stays in .migration-backup-v1. Older tools can no longer edit it.*; *Upgrade*, *Open read-only*, *Cancel*; the words of 1.17.0 are kept); `migrate DIR --dry-run` shows the change (only `project.json`, the model hash equal, the export identical) and writes nothing; `migrate DIR` keeps the originals in `.migration-backup-v1/` with `MIGRATION.json`, checks itself and puts everything back on a difference; a project with a journal gets one entry `migrated from format 1` (kind `edit`, which every reader accepts). **Chosen, can be undone:** (a) a command that would save a project of an older format into its own folder (`generate`, the imports, `override`, `release` and the others; `core/io/saver.py` `save_project`) no longer upgrades it on the way with a backup but without the record, the check and the journal entry, as M1 had left it for format 0 (D-202, *known limits*): it refuses with *This project is in format 1 ... Upgrade it first: `harness-design-studio migrate DIR`* and writes nothing (exit 2). The reason: from now on every existing project is in an older format, and that path also skipped the journal rows of the change itself (the saver journals nothing while `migrated_from` is set). `validate`, `check`, `drc` and `export` still read a format 1 project in memory, and a save to a new folder (*Save as*) writes format 2; only `core.io.upgrade` and the old `migrate_project` pass `upgrade=True`. (b) The fixture `m1_project` (written by 0.0.1) stays in format 1, so that a project written by an old tool, with fields left out, is still read through the step; the new fixture `tests/fixtures/v1_project` is a format 1 project written by the code of 1.17.1 (the `mini3` fixture with a cable drawn from `OBC-J01` whose far end `W002-P2` is loose, and the mark `pos:W002-P2`), and its model hash as 1.17.1 computed it is held by `tests/test_format2.py` and `tests/fixtures/migration/hashes.json`. **Written outputs:** no written file holds the project format number; it is only in `project.json`, which no output copies (the `format_version` 1 of `manifest.json`, `system/export.json`, `system/provenance.json` and the run record is the version of those files, not of the project). The model hash reads the number as 1 (D-202), so the 12-character stamp is unchanged. Checked: a full export with drawings and a run record and `export-data` of the four packaged examples and of `v1_project` are byte-identical in format 1 and in format 2 (the run record apart from its time); `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` print *identical*; no baseline was renewed. The one file the tool writes that changes is the JSON Schema of `project.json` from `harness-design-studio schema`, whose `default` of `schema_version` is now 2 (it describes the format; no stored copy). **Fixtures and examples:** the five shipped example projects (`tools.build_examples`) and the fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device` (`harness-design-studio migrate`, then the backup folder and the `.bak` files removed) are format 2; in each only `schema_version` in `project.json` changed. **What a 1.x tool does with a format 2 project** (run with the code of the tags `v1.17.0` and of `master` at 1.17.1 on a copy of `mini3` marked format 2): it opens it read-only with the warning *This project was saved by a newer version of the tool (format 2; this tool understands up to 1). It is opened read-only.*, refuses to save, and `generate`, `export`, `migrate` and `migrate --dry-run` stop with exit 2 and *this project is read-only (saved by a newer tool version)*; `validate`, `check` and `drc` read it. 1.17.0 also sets aside a harness that holds a loose end (a role it does not know; read-only all the same); 1.17.1 reads it. This is the safe path the plan asks for. **Not done:** the version number (2.0.0rc1 is a later change); M7a; the plan's sparse `Harness.origin` (see above); nobody looked at the question on a real display (offscreen tests only). No compliance claim is made. |

| D-228 | **The journal that merges: `journal.jsonl` in format 2** (M7a of the programme towards 2.0.0; D-191, D-202, D-226; plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` 2.4, row *Journal layout*, and 3.2 *M7a*; not in a release yet, the program version stays 1.17.1). The owner decided on 2026-11-11 that M7a is part of 2.0.0 and goes into format 2 before 2.0.0rc1, as one more part of the step from 1 to 2 (D-226), and told us to work to 2.0.0 on our own recommendations. **The file:** the change journal is `journal.jsonl`, one entry per line, each line one canonical JSON object (sorted keys, no spaces, UTF-8, `\n`; `canonical.dumps_line`); a save writes the lines in the order of the file and only ever adds lines at the end. **The chain:** a new entry has `parent`, the chain value of the entry before it on its own line of work (`0000000000000000` for the first), and `chain` = the first 16 hexadecimal characters of SHA-256 of the parent's chain value and of the entry without `chain` (the function of D-191). So the entries that two Git branches added are both valid after Git kept both sides' lines. The claim "no gaps in `seq`" became "every entry's parent exists". Entries written before M7a (*entries of format 1*) keep their `seq`, have no `parent` and keep their chain values; their parent is the entry numbered one less, as before. **Reading** (`core/model/journal.py`): a line that is there twice (same id, same content) is read once, two different entries with one id are damage; the order does not depend on the order of the lines: entries of format 1 first, by number, then every other entry after its parent, and among those that may come next the earliest `when`, then author, then id; the number shown (`seq`) is the place in that order. A kind this tool does not know (a short lower-case word) is read and kept, shown as `other` in the command line and the editor, and given as written in `--json` and the scripting interface. Tamper evidence holds for each line of work: a changed entry no longer fits its chain value, a removed one leaves an entry whose parent is missing (or a `merge` entry that names a missing entry), an entry of format 1 with a gap; the loader sets the journal aside (`journal_damaged`), as before. Cutting off the last entries of a line of work is not detectable, as before. **Git:** `.gitattributes` gets `journal.jsonl merge=union` (Git's own driver, no set-up per clone; `core/mergefix.py` `GITATTRIBUTES`). Tested with real `git` (`tests/test_journal_merge.py`): two branches that both add entries merge without a conflict and the journal reads; both generate: only the record conflicts. **Not verified:** whether the GitHub web *Merge* button honours `merge=union` (the manual says so). **`merge-fix`** adds one entry of the new kind `merge` (object `journal`, field `heads`, `new` = `{"heads": [ids]}`, following the last head) when the journal has more than one head, also when no file is in conflict (result `journal_merged`); when the journal is in conflict (no `merge=union` line), it keeps both sides' lines (`take_both_sides`) before that; it still refuses when any other file is in conflict. **The migration:** the second part of `V1_TO_V2_PARTS`, `_v2_journal_lines`: `journal.json` becomes `journal.jsonl` with every entry as it was (number, chain value, no parent); the existing upgrade path appends `migrated from format 1` (kind `edit`, now with a parent); the backup holds `journal.json`, the record the hashes of both files, the dry run lists both; after the check has passed, `.gitattributes` gets the line (`add_journal_union`). **Written outputs:** none. The journal stays outside every hash and output (`layout.OUTSIDE_THE_HASH_FILES` holds both names). Checked: `tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print *identical*, the output goldens and `tests/fixtures/migration/hashes.json` are unchanged; the example projects changed only in `.gitattributes`. **Chosen, can be undone:** (a) `seq` is not stored on a new entry, so `merge-fix` renumbers nothing: the number is the place on load, and a merge can renumber later entries (rewriting lines would make Git keep both copies at the next union). (b) The id of an entry is its chain value; there is no separate id field. (c) The order puts entries of format 1 first and a parent before its child, then `when`, author, id (the plan's order refined, so that a wrong clock never shows a child before its parent and the lines of one save keep their order). (d) A format 2 project that a build of `master` wrote before M7a (with `journal.json`) is recognised by the files: it is read as it is (`journal_old_layout`, info) and the next save writes `journal.jsonl` and keeps the old file as `journal.json.bak`, not through the upgrade path, because the format number is already current and the file is outside every hash; with both files, the entries of both are read. (e) `.gitattributes`: written when there is none, replaced when it is the text of 1.x word for word, otherwise two lines are added at its end, a link is left alone; done by the saver when the journal file is new, and by the upgrade only after its check (so a rollback never has to undo it). (f) A line with both `seq` and `parent` is damage. (g) The JSON shape of `journal --json` and `api.get_journal` is unchanged (no `parent`). `migrate --json` gets `gitattributes`. **Not done:** M7b (two branches that each create `W005` or `L7`; a merge-doctor in `core/mergefix.py`); `changelog.json` of two releases on two branches still conflicts; the GitHub web merge is not tried; nobody looked at the editor's history window on a real display (offscreen test only). No compliance claim is made. |

| D-231 | **IDs that two Git branches both made: `merge-fix` renames the other branch's harness or link** (M7b of the programme towards 2.0; plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` 3.2, *ID collisions, read but not tried*; D-164, D-226, D-228, D-230; REQ-MIGRATE-07; planned for the patch release 2.0.1, not in a release, the program version stays 2.0.0; prepared for 2.0.1 by D-236 and published in it on 2026-11-16). **What happens without it** (tried with real `git` 2.x on a project made by `new --template first-steps` and generated, so `W001` and `W002` exist; Bob, on his branch, merges Ann's branch; `tests/test_merge_ids.py`): (a) each adds an interface (different ones, other IDs) and presses Generate: both get `W003`. Git stops with `CONFLICT (add/add)` on `physical/harnesses/W003.json` (the index has stages 2 and 3, no 1) and on `generated/generation.json` (binary, ours kept); the connector and interface files merge. `validate` exits 1 with `merge_conflict ... [physical/harnesses/W003.json]` and counts two harnesses (W003 is not read); `merge-fix` of 2.0.0 refused (*these files of the project are in conflict, and merge-fix only resolves generated/generation.json and journal.jsonl*) and changed nothing; the editor opens the project in recovery mode, its list of problems names the file, and W003 is missing. When both let the tool choose the interface ID, both new interfaces are `IF-003` as well: kept in the files of two subsystems (`logical/interfaces/power.json`, `avionics.json`) they merge **without a conflict**, and the loader sets one aside (`quarantined: ... duplicate ID 'IF-003' (already defined elsewhere)`). (b) each drags a cable from a port onto empty ground (a hand-drawn harness with a loose end, M4c): both get `W003` (the first free number of the naming pattern) and the name *Cable 1*; Git stops with add/add on `physical/harnesses/W003.json` only; `validate` and `merge-fix` as in (a). (c) each adds a link: both get `L1` (the first free `L<n>`); `logical/links.json` is one file, so Git stops on it (add/add when neither had a link, else a conflict inside the list at the place of the new entry); `validate` and `merge-fix` as in (a). (d) the same harness made on both branches with the same content: Git merges the identical file without a conflict, `validate` passes, `merge-fix` says *Nothing to fix*. **The design (chosen, can be undone):** no new command (a patch release), no change of the file format, no new field. `merge-fix` (`core/merge_ids.py`, called from `core/mergefix.plan_merge_fix`) resolves, besides the record and the journal, (1) a harness file that **both branches added** (stages 2 and 3, no 1) with different content: the harness of the branch you are on (`HEAD`, ours) keeps its ID and its file; the other branch's gets the next free ID of the naming pattern from the record's `next_harness_number` on (skipping every ID of the project, both sides' and the IDs that baselines, the change log and the as-built records name), and so do the IDs inside it that start with the old ID followed by a character that is not a letter or digit (`W003-P1` to `W004-P1`; its wires, shields, splices, branch points, segments and accessories, and every reference inside the harness to them); an ID inside it that does not start with the harness ID and is also in ours is refused. Ours is kept, not chosen by date or size, because Git's own `--ours` is the branch you merge into and the rule needs no judgement; (2) `logical/links.json`, `waivers.json` and `diagram/marks.json` in conflict, merged entry by entry against the version both started from (stage 1, read from Git's object store); a link that both added with different content: the other branch's gets the next free `L<n>`; an entry that both changed in different ways is refused (a real conflict, not a new ID); (3) every entry of the other branch that names a renamed ID is changed: `Link.harness_id`, the marks `pos:<connector>` (the place of a loose end), `link:`, `end:` and `bend:` of a renamed link, and a waiver or deviation (`object_id`, and its ID made again by `checks.finding_id`). Which branch an entry comes from is the side of the conflict when it is in one, and otherwise whether the commit of `HEAD` holds it as it is (ours) or not (the other branch's), so a link that merged without a conflict is still told apart. A waiver keeps its `finding_hash`; its finding names the new ID, so it no longer applies until it is given again, and `merge-fix` says so (so nothing becomes waived by a rename). The same harness on both sides (the JSON equal) is kept once with no rename. The journal: one entry of kind `edit` (object `project`, field `ids`, `new` = `{"renamed": {old: new}}`, every reader accepts it, `core/journal.rename_rows`) after the `merge` entry of D-228; journal entries written before the merge keep the old IDs (history is not rewritten). `merge-fix` prints every rename (`old -> new`, the IDs inside a harness too), what else now names the new ID, and notes (two harnesses with the same name: the name is a person's and is not changed). `--dry-run` (new) prints the same plan and writes nothing; `--json` gets the keys `dry_run` and `renamed` at the end (a document had six keys; a reader of the old keys is not affected). A failure after writing (the project does not load, the journal, `generate`) puts every file back and removes the new harness file. **Refused, writing nothing:** a harness with a release history on either branch (status not `draft`, or a baseline, a change-log entry or an as-built record naming the ID): released IDs never change, and renaming the other branch's draft when ours is released would also need a judgement, so it is left to a person; a link that a baseline holds; two harnesses that Generate made for the same group on both branches (a rename would leave two harnesses for one group; a person keeps one); a harness file changed on both branches (stage 1 present); a project folder outside a Git repository the tool can read, or a file with markers that the index does not list (then it cannot tell which side added what). **Reading Git:** `core/vcs/gitobjects.py` (new) and `gitindex.conflict_stages` read the index stages, a blob and the file in the commit of `HEAD` (tree objects) from the repository's own files, loose or packed, with the size limits of D-164 (64 MiB per file, as the loader); `gitinfo._read_object` takes the object type and a limit (a commit as before). The `git` program is still never started. **Prevention (not done, an option):** IDs could be made less likely to collide without a format change, for example by numbering a new harness from the largest number any branch could have used, or by a branch-specific suffix; each changes the IDs a person sees and the naming patterns, so nothing is changed now. **Interface IDs** (`IF-003` on both branches, case (a) when the tool chooses them) are not renamed: they are spread over the files of the subsystems and named by wires, pins, harnesses and the generation record; `merge-fix` finds the project damaged and puts every file back, and the manual says how to resolve it by hand. **Written outputs:** none change; a project without such a merge is read and written as before. Checked: `tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print *identical*. After a fix in case (a), `validate` passes, `generate` prints `0 added, 0 changed, 4 unchanged`, and `export` and `verify --outputs` pass (`tests/test_merge_ids.py`). The test of A-23 in `tests/test_merge_fix.py`, which held that `merge-fix` leaves two links `L1` to the people, now holds the rename. **Not done:** interface, device and connector IDs made on two branches; a second release of the same ID on two branches (`baselines/`, `changelog.json`); the editor does not show the renames (command line only); the GitHub web *Merge* button. No compliance claim is made. | The plan lists M7b as the small answer to ID collisions after M7a (3.2); the owner told us to continue on our own recommendations, and this is the safer of the ways the plan names: one side keeps its ID, the other is renamed only where the tool can tell the sides apart from Git's own records, and a release history is never renamed. |
| D-232 | **The release page carries only what users need: the four packages, `SHA256SUMS`, its signature and one release bundle** (amends D-213; first in release 2.0.1). Up to 2.0.0 the release workflow attached every file on its own: the page of 2.0.0 holds 221 files (the four packages, `SHA256SUMS`, seven `.sigstore.json` signatures, two SBOMs, four inventory files, two notices files, `SOURCES.txt` and 198 source files, and the offline-audit report). From 2.0.1 the page holds **seven files**: the four packages, `SHA256SUMS`, `SHA256SUMS.sigstore.json` and `harness-design-studio-<version>-release-bundle.tar`. **The bundle** (`tools/release_bundle.py`) is a plain tar (the source archives in it are compressed already) with one top folder named like the file, and in it `sources/` (`SOURCES.txt` and the files it lists, unchanged), `sbom/`, `inventory/`, `notices/`, `offline-audit/`, `signatures/` (the `.sigstore.json` of the four packages and the two SBOMs) and a `README.txt` that says what each folder holds, how to check a package with cosign (signature and package side by side) and that the build provenance attestation is checked on its own with `gh attestation verify`. It is reproducible: entries sorted by name, one time for all (`SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, else the time of the tagged commit), owner and group 0, fixed modes, GNU tar format with no pax headers (so no access or change times). **Signing, the smallest correct design:** the packages and the SBOMs are signed first and their signatures go into the bundle; then `SHA256SUMS` is written over the four packages **and the bundle** and signed. A signature cannot sit inside the file it signs, and `SHA256SUMS` cannot be inside the bundle it lists, so the one extra file on the page is `SHA256SUMS.sigstore.json`. The bundle gets no signature of its own: a valid signature of `SHA256SUMS` and its line for the bundle cover it, as they cover each package; a separate `.sigstore.json` for the bundle would be an eighth file that adds no check. The signature of each package stays available (in `signatures/`) for a user who checks the package itself with cosign. The build provenance attestation is as before (the packages and `SHA256SUMS`). **Checks before publishing:** `python -m tools.release_notes complete` fails when a package kind or, from 2.0.1, the bundle is missing from `SHA256SUMS`, and when the bundle lacks `SOURCES.txt` or a file it lists (size and SHA-256 compared), an SBOM, an inventory, a notices file, the offline-audit report, a signature of a package or an SBOM, or the README. So a run whose offline audit wrote no report now stops before the page is made; the audit is still no gate on its findings (a report that says FAIL is bundled and the release goes on). The publish step checks that it uploads exactly seven files. `harness-sources.yml` builds the bundle twice on pull requests that touch it, from the real sources with stand-ins for the files only a release makes, checks it and compares the two builds byte for byte; nothing is published. **Licence obligations:** the source offer in the notices of each package and in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` says that the source archives are in the bundle `harness-design-studio-<version>-release-bundle.tar` on the release page of this version (folder `sources/`, listed in `SOURCES.txt`). The source stays on the same page as the packages, only inside one archive; the legal texts of `compliance/legal/texts.md` do not name where the source is, so none of them changes. **Older releases are not changed:** the pages up to 2.0.0 keep their files. `tools/release_notes.py` writes the new text only from `BUNDLED_FROM` (2.0.1) on, as `SIGNED_FROM` does for signing, so the `notes_only` run writes an old page as before (the text of 1.16.0 to 2.0.0 was compared byte for byte with the text of the earlier script). The release workflow of `master` refuses a tag that has no `tools/release_bundle.py` (a tag before 2.0.1); such a page is made by the workflow of its own tag. **Publication records** (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 15, `docs/developer/release.md`) check the seven files of the page and the bundle: downloaded, its hash checked with `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS --ignore-missing`, its listing checked with `python -m tools.release_bundle check`, instead of counting 221 files. **Not done:** the change of the release workflow is first proven on the tag of 2.0.1; it could not be run before. **First real use: 2.0.1 (2026-11-16): it worked.** Release run 43903192546 on the tag `v2.0.1`, first attempt: the step that checks that the tag has the release bundle passed, `tools.release_bundle build` printed `harness-design-studio-2.0.1-release-bundle.tar: 216 files, complete`, `SHA256SUMS` lists the four packages and the bundle, `tools.release_notes complete` passed, and the page has exactly seven files (the four packages, `SHA256SUMS`, `SHA256SUMS.sigstore.json` and the bundle of 364,666,880 bytes). The bundle was downloaded for the publication record: `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` printed `OK` for it and for the four packages, and `python -m tools.release_bundle check` printed `216 files, complete`. Its listing holds what this decision says: `README.txt`, `sources/` (`SOURCES.txt` and its 198 files), `sbom/` (2), `inventory/` (4), `notices/` (2), `offline-audit/` (the report, PASS, in Markdown and JSON) and `signatures/` (6: the four packages and the two SBOMs); every entry has owner 0, the time of the tagged commit and the GNU tar format. Not checked: the signatures from outside the workflow, skipped on the owner's decision (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 2.0.1*). **Second use: 2.1.0 (2026-11-17): it worked again.** Release run 43993920392 on the tag `v2.1.0`, first attempt: `tools.release_bundle build` printed `harness-design-studio-2.1.0-release-bundle.tar: 216 files, complete`, `sha256sum -c` printed `OK` for the four packages and the bundle, and the page has exactly seven files (the bundle has 364,666,880 bytes, as for 2.0.1). The downloaded files gave the same results: `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` printed `OK` for the five files, `python -m tools.release_notes complete SHA256SUMS` passed and `python -m tools.release_bundle check` printed `216 files, complete`. | D-233 | **Several interfaces on one connector in the editor: CD-Q3 reopened, yes (owner decision of 2026-11-16).** The editor will let a device connector carry more than one interface. Today it refuses a second one (*… already carries IF-… One interface per connector.*, `core/edit.py`, `core/devices.py`), while the generator already places several interfaces on one connector (`docs/ux-custom-devices-audit.md`, appendix run 1). This supersedes the default answer to CD-Q3 recorded in `docs/ux-custom-devices-audit.md` on 2026-10-14 (*The GUI keeps refusing two interfaces on one connector*). Planned for **2.1.0**, together with the automatic pin assignment of several interfaces of one type on one connector (for example RS-422 ×n) from imported pinouts, so that the pins of each port go to one interface. Not built: nothing in the program changes with this decision. | The first demo feedback of 2026-11-16 (`docs/ux/feedback-2026-11-16.md`, participant 1: *Multi-point connections: one connector should be able to connect to many other interfaces* and *automatic linking of already predefined pin assignments*). The generator and the files already hold it; the gap is the editor's refusal, the connector choice (`connector_compat`, the Guided auto-choice) and the matching of fixed pins by the bare signal name (`core/generate/pins.py`), which does not know which pins form one port. |
| D-234 | **Reusable devices live as a folder of `NAME.device.json` files: CD-Q4 decided (owner decision of 2026-11-16).** One file per device, named after the device; the folder can be a Git repository; a project or a user points to it. Planned for **2.2.0**: pick a device (for example `PCDU00`) and its connectors and pinouts come with it. It builds on what exists: the device library file (D-165) and the library folder of devices and subsystems, a setting of the user (D-175, *File > Library folder…*), whose files are named `<ID>.json` or `devices.json` and are found by their `format` field. What 2.2.0 adds is the one-file-per-device naming, a pointer that a project can hold, and picking a device by its name. Not built: nothing in the program changes with this decision. | The first demo feedback of 2026-11-16 (`docs/ux/feedback-2026-11-16.md`, participant 2: *libraries*, and *link to our repos: if I select a device, for example PCDU00, I should see the pinouts of its connectors*). CD-Q4 of `docs/ux-custom-devices-audit.md` asked where reusable devices live and was set aside on 2026-10-14. A folder of plain files keeps the tool offline (the folder is read, never fetched) and lets Git do the sharing. |
| D-235 | **The order of the 2.x programme after the feedback of 2026-11-16 (owner decision).** 2.0.1: M7b (ID collisions on two branches, a merge-doctor; D-231) and the release bundle (D-232). Then the documentation pull request of this feedback (no change of the program). **2.1.0:** several interfaces on one connector and their automatic pin assignment (D-233). **2.2.0:** the device library of `NAME.device.json` files (D-234). **2.3.0:** branching harnesses and splices, *one connector to several connectors*. `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` gets a status line and the section *Order after the feedback of 2026-11-16*; its earlier text stays as written. | The two participants asked for the multi-interface connector, the device library and the branching harness (`docs/ux/feedback-2026-11-16.md`). The plan's new section records both names; which milestone label is kept is the owner's to say. |
| D-236 | **Version 2.0.1 is a patch release: M7b and the release bundle; no format change** (D-231, D-232, D-235; plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` section 3.6). 2.0.0 was published on 2026-11-15 as the latest full release (annotated tag `v2.0.0` on `e6a6763`, D-230). Since then come M7b (D-231, pull request 232: `merge-fix` resolves a harness or a link that two Git branches made with the same ID, keeps this branch's under the ID, gives the other branch's the next free ID with every entry of that branch that names it, journals the renames, refuses a release history, has `--dry-run`, and merges `logical/links.json`, `waivers.json` and `diagram/marks.json` entry by entry), the release page of D-232 (pull request 230: the four packages, `SHA256SUMS`, its signature and one release bundle, `tools/release_bundle.py`), the documents of the first demo feedback (pull request 231, D-233 to D-235), the publication record of 2.0.0 (pull request 228) and two GUI tests that hold the automatic design-rule check (pull request 233); all of them are on `master`. The owner set the order on 2026-11-16 (D-235): 2.0.1 is M7b and the release bundle. **A patch release, not 2.1.0:** nothing in it changes the file format, a written file (apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it) or the scripting interface; `merge-fix` gains a case it refused before and a `--dry-run`, and the release page is not part of the program. **No format change:** 2.0.1 writes format 2 as 2.0.0 does, and format 2 stays frozen (D-230); a project saved by 2.0.0 opens and saves as before, so the upgrade from 2.0.0 is to install 2.0.1 over it. **No release candidate first:** a project needs no upgrade, and 2.0.0 opens and saves a project saved by 2.0.1 (the read-only check reads the format number, which is the same), and the new parts are covered by tests (`tests/test_merge_ids.py`, `tests/test_release_bundle.py`); the release workflow with the bundle runs for the first time on the tag of 2.0.1, and it stops before publishing when a package kind or the bundle is missing or the bundle lacks a file (D-232). The changelog section `2.0.1` holds every entry that was under *Unreleased* and an intro in plain words (a patch release, no format change, how to upgrade, what is not done); `docs/releases/2.0.1.md` covers what changed since 2.0.0 for a reader of 2.0.0. `2.0.1` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` replace the stamp and say *identical*: the baseline and the snapshot are not written again and `BASE_VERSION` stays `2.0.0`, as from 1.10.0 to 1.17.0 (D-183). The example projects, the fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the output goldens and the migration hashes are written again with the repository's tools (89 files); a script compared every regenerated file with `HEAD` (the new text with `2.0.1` replaced by `2.0.0`, every run of 12 or more hexadecimal characters masked in both) and found them equal. No dependency changed since 2.0.0 (`pyproject.toml` and the packaging files are as at `e6a6763`; `tools/third_party.py` changed only the sentence of the source offer, D-232), so the third-party inventory and the SBOMs of `2.0.1` are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published 2.0.0 packages (their SHA-256 sums checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.0.0_SHA256SUMS`); they differ from the 2.0.0 files only by the version, and `tools.third_party check` passes for both packages against them. **Not done:** the release workflow with the bundle had not run yet when this was decided (it ran on the tag, see below); the editor does not show the renames of `merge-fix`; the *Merge* button of the GitHub web site with `merge=union`; the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision); a look at the program on a real display; the Windows packages on a real computer; the owner's sign-off; the release compliance record. **Published on 2026-11-16** as the latest full release, the annotated tag `v2.0.1` on `69c5aac` (the merge of #234), made by the tag workflow of D-227 (run 43903141934, its third use); release workflow run 43903192546, first attempt: every job passed, with the keyless Sigstore signing, the release bundle and the build provenance attestation; seven files on the page, the first real use of D-232; the offline-audit report in the bundle says PASS; the GitHub API names `v2.0.1` as the latest release. Before the tag, the full test suite passed on the pull request (7045 passed, 6 skipped), CI was green on Linux and Windows on the head of the pull request and on the merge commit, and an end-to-end test of the head (2.0.1 from source against the published 2.0.0 package, both directions of compatibility; M7b with real `git`; the release workflow read through) found no blocker; its findings were fixed in #234 before the merge. Not done: the editor does not show the renames of `merge-fix`, the *Merge* button with `merge=union`, the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision), the Windows packages on a real computer, a look at the program on a real display, the owner's sign-off, the release compliance record (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 2.0.1*). | The owner decided the content of 2.0.1 on 2026-11-16 (D-235) and told us to work on our own recommendations; the patch number, and no release candidate, are that recommendation. A release needs a new version, because the tag `v2.0.0` exists (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 1). The tag `v2.0.1` is made only after this change is merged and CI is green on the merge commit (step 15; by a person, or by the workflow of D-227). The owner has not signed it off (step 14). |
| D-237 | **The design of 2.1.0: several interfaces on one connector, one interface per port; a port is a naming convention on `Pin.signal`; no format change** (D-233, D-235; design `docs/ait/DESIGN_2.1_PORTS.md`). A device connector can carry several interfaces. Whether it has room for one more is decided by a dry run of Generate's own pin allocator (`core/generate/pins.py`) with every interface that ends on the connector, so the editor and Generate cannot disagree. The Guided auto-choice has two tiers: first a free connector, as today; only when none is free, a connector with a fixed pinout that has a free complete port of the type. A port is part of the existing free-text `Pin.signal`: `P<sep>t` (prefix form) or `t<sep>P` (suffix form), with t a signal name of the interface type, sep one of `_`, `.`, `-` and P not empty (`RS422_2_TX+`); the interface that uses a port is the existing `Pin.interface_id` (written by Generate, or with `locked` when a person chooses). Exact names come first: a signal name of the type is never read as a port, and Generate keeps today's exact-name path whenever it places every signal of an interface, so every project that generates at 2.0.1 generates the same at 2.1.0. A name that reads in more than one way, an incomplete port, a port with two pins for one signal and two ways of writing one port name are refused with a message, never guessed. No format change: no new field, every field keeps its meaning, and format 2 stays frozen. The owner questions Q1 (2.0.x does not read port names: its `verify` and `drc` report errors for such interfaces, its command-line `generate` saves nothing, and its editor's Generate drops their draft harnesses, which 2.1.0 makes again on the same ports under new harness numbers; the release notes say so) and Q2 (an interface on a free port of a connector that a released harness plugs into is allowed; to another far connector it gets its own harness and the warnings, until the branching harness of 2.3.0) were answered by the recommendation of the design, under the owner's standing instruction to work on our own recommendations; the owner can still choose the sparse field `Pin.port` of a format 3 instead (Q1). The work comes in six pull requests (section 7 of the design). **This pull request builds the parser only:** `core/ports.py` (`read_signal`, `ports_of`, `compose`, `port_name_problem`, `natural_key`) and `tests/test_ports.py`; `compose` writes `PORT_SIGNAL` with `_`. Nothing calls the parser yet, so nothing the user sees changes. **Pull request 2 builds** the pin assignment of a fixed pinout by ports (`core/generate/pins.py`, `engine.py`; the warning `pin_port_unknown`; the port in the explanation of a pin), the design rules `signal-unassigned` and `direction-conflict` that read port names, and the verifier's own copy of the reading rule with the error `port_mixed`; a test compares the allocator with the one of 2.0.1 (`tests/reference/pins_201.py`), and the output hashes stay identical. **Pull request 3 builds** the import: a Port column in `import-pinout` and the import wizard that writes the port into the signal name (`compose`), refused rows for a port that cannot be used, the ports of each connector in the preview and under `ports` in `--json`, and the lists of unmatched signals of the pinout and KiCad imports that read port names (`ports.is_signal_of`); the type of a port is read from the signal against the types the connector carries (all types of the project when it carries none), and a signal of no such type is imported as it is with a warning, never composed blindly. **Pull request 4 builds** the editor core: the room on a connector by a dry run of the allocator (`edit.connector_room`, through `engine.allocate_box`, the function Generate calls; cached while the project does not change), `connector_compat` that refuses a full connector and notes a second plug or a released harness, the two tiers of the Guided choice (`edit.shared_connectors`), `devices.ops_assign_pins` that refuses a pin of another interface and keeps a fixed pin's name, `devices.ops_assign_port`, the port-aware inline notes with CD-K9, the counts of `connector_choice` by free ports, the ranking in `explain`, and the assumption `A-PORTS`; the screens follow in pull request 5. **Pull request 5 builds** the editor screens: the diagram, the pointer and a click accept a connector in use that has room and say the note, the Inspector's list of an end shows a full connector as *Full* with the reason, each end on a fixed pinout with named ports shows its port with *Choose port…* (`devices.ops_assign_port`, one undo step), one *Confirm* per interface, the port side on the canvas is the first interface by ID, the Devices tab has a derived *Port* column and *Assign…* offers a port, and the prototype follows (`tests/test_ui_multi_interface.py`); the text of the setting `model.connector_choice` in `core/settings.py` is left as it is, because it is written into the `_about` comments of annotated config files. **Pull request 6 builds** the end-to-end test of section 6 of the design through the command line (`tests/test_e2e_rs422x3.py`: a blank project, `import-pinout` of OBC1 with J1 = RS-422 x3 with a Port column and with port names, three star trackers, three interfaces in Guided mode, the second and third by the second tier, then `generate`, `export`, `verify --outputs`, `drc` and `explain`; port k goes to IF-k, no verifier error, a second Generate changes no file, two exports are byte-identical, `box_connector_shared` with the default segmentation and one harness with one plug on J1 with `per_zone_pair` and the star trackers in one zone, a locked port kept, and `pin_port_unknown` on repeated bare names), a fix of `devices.ops_assign_port` (the pins of the connector that Generate gave the interface before, not locked and not in the new port, lose `interface_id` in the same undo step; before, they kept it until the next Generate), and the documents (the user manual pages *define a device* and *generate harnesses* with a worked example, the in-app guide, `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md`, `docs/models/pin-allocation.md`, the changelog). With it the six pull requests of the design are built; the version, the release notes and the status pages of 2.1.0 follow in the preparation of the release (D-238). **In a release:** 2.1.0, published on 2026-11-17 (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 2.1.0*). | The first demo feedback of 2026-11-16 (`docs/ux/feedback-2026-11-16.md`, participant 1: one connector should connect to many interfaces, and predefined pin assignments should be linked automatically; D-233). The files and the generator already hold several interfaces on one connector (`Endpoint.connector_id`); what is missing is which pins form one port. A KiCad netlist already writes the port into the net name, because net names must be unique, so a naming convention reads what imported pinouts already say. Format 2 is frozen (D-230): a field would need a format 3, which 2.0.x opens read-only. A wrong reading of old pin names would put pins into the wrong interface; refusing instead of guessing keeps that out of a harness. |
| D-238 | **Version 2.1.0 is a minor release: several interfaces on one connector and ports (D-233, D-237); no format change** (D-235; plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` section 3.6; design `docs/ait/DESIGN_2.1_PORTS.md`). 2.0.1 was published on 2026-11-16 as the latest full release (annotated tag `v2.0.1` on `69c5aac`, D-236). Since then come the six pull requests of the design of D-237: 236 (the port parser, `core/ports.py`), 237 (Generate, the design rules `signal-unassigned` and `direction-conflict` and the verifier read port names; `pin_port_unknown`, `port_mixed`), 238 (the Port column of `import-pinout` and the import wizard), 239 (the editor core: the room on a connector by a dry run of Generate's pin assignment, the two tiers of the Guided choice, `devices.ops_assign_pins` and `devices.ops_assign_port`, the inline note CD-K9, the assumption `A-PORTS`), 240 (the editor screens: the diagram, the Inspector with the port of each end and *Choose port…*, one *Confirm* per interface, the *Port* column of the Devices tab, the prototype) and 241 (the end-to-end test `tests/test_e2e_rs422x3.py`, a fix of `devices.ops_assign_port`, the documents); 236 to 240 are on `master`, and this preparation is built on 241 (before 241 took the merge of `master`). The owner set the order on 2026-11-16 (D-235): 2.1.0 is several interfaces on one connector (D-233). **A minor release, not 2.0.2:** the user sees new behaviour (a connector that carries an interface takes another one while it has room, a full connector is refused with the reason, Guided mode takes a connector with a free complete port when no connector is free, Generate gives each interface one port, the Port columns of the import and the Devices tab, *Choose port…*, a new warning, a new verifier error and a new inline note), and the code has new helpers (`core/ports.py`, `edit.connector_room`, `edit.shared_connectors`, `devices.ops_assign_port`); semantic versioning gives a minor number for new function that breaks nothing. **Nothing breaks:** no format change (a port is a naming convention on `Pin.signal`, no field is added and every field keeps its meaning, so format 2 stays frozen, D-230); the scripting interface is unchanged (`harness_design_studio.api.__all__`, the signatures and the result types are those of `tests/fixtures/api_snapshot.json`, which no pull request of 2.1.0 changed; `API_VERSION` stays 1, and `tests/test_api_stability.py` passes); the one change of a `--json` document is the added key `ports` of `import-pinout --json`, and an added key does not break a script (`docs/developer/api-stability.md`); every project that generates at 2.0.1 generates the same at 2.1.0 (the stored output hashes say *identical*, and `tests/test_ports_compat_201.py` compares the pin assignment with a copy of the one of 2.0.1). **Known limit, update the whole team (owner question Q1, answered by the recommendation of D-237):** 2.0.x does not read port names. On a project that uses them, its `verify` and `drc` report errors for those interfaces even without Generate, and its command-line `generate` stops and saves nothing; its editor's Generate drops the draft harnesses of those interfaces (a released harness stays with its pins, and a port locked with *Choose port…* keeps its pins but not its draft), and the next Generate with 2.1.0 makes the drafts again on the same ports with the same content, under new harness numbers, so their wire IDs, labels and output folders change. The changelog and `docs/releases/2.1.0.md` say so. **Until the branching harness of 2.3.0 (Q2):** interfaces from one connector to different far connectors get one harness and one plug each on that connector, with the warning `box_connector_shared`; `per_zone_pair` with the far devices in one zone gives one harness with one plug. **No release candidate first (our recommendation):** an rc protects what a tag cannot take back. 2.0.0rc1 came before the tag that froze format 2 (D-229, D-230), and 1.17.1rc2 was the first run of the signing workflow (D-218, D-224). 2.1.0 freezes nothing: no format changes, and the release workflow with the bundle ran once already for 2.0.1 and has not changed since. A fault found after the tag is fixed by 2.1.1, and projects need no step either way. Against this weigh: 2.1.0 changes the editor and the pin assignment of Generate, more than 2.0.1 did; the end-to-end test of the published 2.0.0rc1 packages found two faults (pull request 226); nobody has looked at the new screens on a real display; and once users name their pins `RS422_2_TX+`, the reading rule of the names is hard to change. What an rc would add is a test of the published packages, which the release workflow already runs (the self-test, the installs and the smoke tests of every package before it publishes), and an end-to-end test, which can run on the head of the pull request before the tag, as for 2.0.1. The new paths run only for pin names that name ports and for a connector that already carries an interface, which 2.0.1 refused; the old paths are held by the output hashes, the goldens and the comparison with 2.0.1. The minor releases 1.11.0 to 1.17.0 changed the editor and generation too and had no rc. An rc would also change the length of the version string (`2.1.0rc1`), so the stored hashes and the numbers snapshot would be written again twice, with `BASE_VERSION` moved each time. **Before the tag:** the full test suite with the `slow` tests, CI green on Linux and Windows on the head of the pull request and on the merge commit, and an end-to-end test of the head (2.1.0 from source against the published 2.0.1 package, in both directions, with the case of Q1). If the owner wants to try 2.1.0 on his own projects first, a `2.1.0rc1` can still be made instead. The changelog section `2.1.0` holds every entry that was under *Unreleased* and an intro in plain words (a minor release, what it brings, no format change, how to upgrade and why the whole team updates at once, `per_zone_pair` until 2.3.0, what is not done); `docs/releases/2.1.0.md` covers what changed since 2.0.1 for a reader of 2.0.1, with the worked example of three RS-422 links. `2.1.0` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` replace the stamp and say *identical*: the baseline and the snapshot are not written again and `BASE_VERSION` stays `2.0.0`, as for 2.0.1 (D-183, D-236). The example projects, the fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the output goldens and the migration hashes are written again with the repository's tools (89 files); a script compared every regenerated file with the head of pull request 241 (the new text with `2.1.0` replaced by `2.0.1`, every run of 12 or more hexadecimal characters masked in both) and found them equal. No dependency changed since 2.0.1 (`pyproject.toml` and the packaging files are as at `69c5aac`, and `tools/third_party.py` is unchanged), so the third-party inventory and the SBOMs of `2.1.0` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published 2.0.1 packages (their SHA-256 sums checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.0.1_SHA256SUMS`); they differ from the 2.0.1 files only by the version, and `tools.third_party check` passes for both packages against them. **Not done:** in Guided mode the message after connecting does not say that a shared connector gets a second plug (Expert mode, Generate and the design rules say it); the prototype counts the pins of the part, not the ports; the sparse field `Pin.port` of a format 3, which the owner can still choose instead of the names (Q1); the branching harness (2.3.0); the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision); a look at the program on a real display; the Windows packages on a real computer; the owner's sign-off; the release compliance record. **End-to-end test of the head, done on 2026-11-16** (head `09e6c66`, against the published 2.0.1 Linux package): the full suite with the `slow` tests 7134 passed, 6 skipped, and `pytest -m perf` 57 passed; compatibility both ways without ports passed; the Q1 case was measured (2.0.1 `verify` and `drc` report errors on a project with port names, its command-line `generate` saves nothing, its editor drops the drafts, 2.1.0 makes them again under new harness numbers) and the release notes now say exactly that; the worked example of the user manual, the new command-line functions and the editor screens (offscreen) passed. No finding blocked the tag; the documentation findings were fixed before the merge, and three notes on the code (the repeated step of `explain` for a connector, the advice of `box_connector_shared` that does not name `per_zone_pair`, the *Port* column out of view at 1440×900) are left for 2.1.1. **Published on 2026-11-17** as the latest full release, the annotated tag `v2.1.0` on `97e7d24` (the merge of #242), made by the tag workflow of D-227 (run 43993872749, its fourth use); release workflow run 43993920392, first attempt: every job passed, with the keyless Sigstore signing, the release bundle and the build provenance attestation; seven files on the page, the second use of D-232; the offline-audit report in the bundle says PASS; the GitHub API names `v2.1.0` as the latest release. Before the tag, CI was green on the merge commit (the tag workflow listed 16 other check runs: 13 passed, 3 were skipped), and the end-to-end test of the head is the one recorded above. Not done: the three notes on the code for 2.1.1, the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision), the Windows packages on a real computer, a look at the program on a real display, the owner's sign-off, the release compliance record (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 2.1.0*). | The owner decided the content of 2.1.0 on 2026-11-16 (D-233, D-235) and told us to work on our own recommendations; the minor number, and no release candidate, are that recommendation.
