| D-240 | **The design of 2.2.0: the device library is a folder of `NAME.device.json` files that a project names in `device-libraries.json`; the import record links a project device to its library file; no format change** (D-234, D-235; design `docs/ait/DESIGN_2.2_DEVICE_LIBRARY.md`). **One device per file:** a device library file named `NAME.device.json` is the device library file of D-165, `harness-device-library` version 1, unchanged, so a 2.1 tool reads a 2.2 library. It holds exactly one device, and `NAME` is its `unit.id` (case counts; two names that differ only in case are refused). Every connector is a box connector of the device with an ID `<device>-<tail>`; the file is complete on its own (every part, contact part and mating part it names, and every interface type its connectors carry); it has no `interface_id`, no `locked: true`, no `zone` and no unknown top-level key, and its values are checked with the strict models. A library folder holds such files in the folder and two levels below it; the multi-device files and subsystem files of 2.1 are still read. Across the folder, a device ID in two files is offered from neither and the message names both files, two device files of the same name are refused (the record is keyed by the file name), and a part with different data in two files is a warning that names the fields. An optional `library.json` (`harness-device-library-folder`, version 1, `name`, `description`) gives the library a name; without it the name is the folder's. The folder is data: only `*.json` is read, nothing is imported or run, the caps of D-175 stay, JSON is read strictly, and `.git` is read only through the capped readers of D-164. **How a library is found,** in this order: `--library FOLDER` on the command line; the project's `device-libraries.json` at the project root (`harness-project-libraries`, version 1, `device_libraries` with relative paths written with `/`; absolute paths and `~` refused; at most 8; no unknown key); in the editor only, the person's library folder. A device found in two libraries is refused as ambiguous, never chosen. **The link** needs no new field: a project device follows a library device `D` when the project has the import record `devices:<file>`, that file holds `D` in one of the project's libraries, and the device's ID is `D` or a copy of it (`D-R`, `D-3`); a device added under another ID is an unlinked copy. The record's `sha256` is the version of the device; the commit, the library's name, the path in the library and *not committed* go into its `notes` as fixed-form lines, which the program never parses. **The update** compares the library-owned fields (the connectors with their pins in portable form, `kind`, `mass_relevant`, `notes`) and keeps the project-owned ones (name, subsystem, zone, side, the placement, and on each pin `interface_id`, `locked` and allocated signals). It shows a diff and changes nothing until it is asked to apply; applying is all or nothing per device, a change that a released harness locks refuses the device and names the harness, and approvals and the project's parts and interface types are never replaced. **No format change:** the library file keeps its own version 1; `device-libraries.json` is not managed, not hashed and not deleted by a save, and older tools never see it; the record uses the existing kind `devices` of format 2, outside the model hash; existing projects have the same bytes, model hash and outputs. The owner questions Q1 (which project devices follow the library: the device whose ID is the library name, and its copies; the alternative is a sparse `Unit.library` field in a format 3) and Q2 (a project may hold `device-libraries.json`: relative paths, outside the project format, not undoable, ignored by older tools) were answered by the recommendation of the design, under the owner's standing instruction to work on our own recommendations. The work comes in six pull requests (section 6 of the design). **This pull request builds the folder rules only:** `core/device_file.py` (`device_file_problems`, `device_file_data`, `library_info`, `check_library_info`), the checks across the folder in `core/library_folder.py` `scan_folder` (`LibraryScan.warnings`, `name`, `description`, `holders`; `save_targets`), `save_devices` writing each device into the file that holds it, else `<ID>.device.json` (`device_library.default_file_name` gives `<ID>.device.json` for one device), the Git state of a library in `core/library_git.py` (`library_git`, `file_state`; `gitinfo.no_commit_yet`), `device-libraries.json` read and write in `core/library_pointer.py`, and the schemas `device-file.schema.json` and `device-libraries.schema.json` of `schema DIR`. Nothing calls the Git state or the pointer file yet; the library window shows the new refusals and the new file names, and *Save to library* of several devices writes one file each. Tests: `tests/test_device_folder.py`, `tests/test_library_pointer.py`, `tests/test_library_git.py`, `tests/test_schemas.py`. *Added 2026-11-17, pull request 2:* `core/library_link.py` builds the link (the libraries of a project, the states, add with the record, the diff, the update plan with its locks, impacts, copies and adopt, the save that links; requirement REQ-LIB-07); the editor's two ways of adding a device from a library now write the record through it, and a taken ID there still gets the next free one (an unlinked copy, no record) until the editor work of pull request 4. A device added under another ID gets no record, because the record would link the project's device of the library ID. `import_record.check_sources` takes `located` (record ID to file), which `library_link.located_sources` fills from the project's libraries; the command line uses it in pull request 3. Tests: `tests/test_library_link.py`. *Added 2026-11-17, pull request 3:* the command line (`cli/library.py`): `libraries` (the project's libraries with their Git state; `--add` and `--remove` write `device-libraries.json` outside undo; `--devices` with the states), `add-device`, `update-device` (no change without `--apply`; `--check` ends with 1 while a linked device differs; `--apply` all or nothing per device) and `save-device`, all through `library_link`; `imports` finds the files of `devices` records in the project's libraries (`located_sources`), and `explain DIR DEVICE` names the record and the state. Exit code 1 for a refusal that changed nothing, 2 for a name or a library that cannot be found. `import-devices` is left as it was: it reads any library file, gives a taken ID the next free one and records the file it read, which `plan_add_devices` with its rules for unlinked copies would change; the editor work of pull request 4 and the documents of pull request 5 come back to it. Tests: `tests/test_library_cli.py`. *Added 2026-11-17, pull request 4:* the editor screens (`gui/library_review.py`): the library window's *Status* column, source line and *Review changes…*; the chip on a device in the Inspector and the Devices tab; the notice when a project opens (the project's libraries and the states are read in a helper thread; it never applies anything, and there is none without a library); the review dialog (*Take the library's version* through `plan_update_device`, one undo step with the record; *Keep this project's*; *Save this project's to the library…* through `plan_save_device`); a taken ID asks (add copies, adopt, or add under another ID) where the editor gave the next free ID; *From library* in *Edit > Add device*, the diagram's *Add here*, Ctrl+K and the Quick mode *Add device*; *Settings > Project > Device libraries of this project* writes `device-libraries.json` at once, outside undo; *Save As* copies it with its paths rewritten (`library_pointer.copy_pointer`, `relative_path`, `moved_paths`). In the editor the libraries are those of `device-libraries.json`, else the person's folder; the library window still shows the person's folder only. Tests: `tests/test_ui_library_review.py`, `tests/test_ui_library_window.py`, `tests/test_library_pointer.py`. *Added 2026-11-17, pull request 5:* the end-to-end test of section 5 of the design through the command line (`tests/test_e2e_device_library.py`: save two devices into a library that is a Git repository, a blank project names it, `add-device`, an interface, `generate`, `export`, `verify --outputs`; a library change committed; *library changed*, `imports --check` and `update-device --check` end with 1, `update-device` changes no byte, `--apply` takes the change with a new record; Generate and export are stable; the same sequence in two folders gives the same project files, the times of the change journal left out; after a release a change of a used pin is refused, naming the harness). The library window now lists the project's libraries of `device-libraries.json` (through `library_link.project_libraries`, with a line that names each and its path, and a *Library* column) beside the person's folder, so a project that names its libraries shows them when the person has no folder set; a device of a project library is added and compared with the project's libraries, a device of the person's folder with that folder. `imports` names `update-device` for a changed device library file instead of *import it again*. The documents: the record of kind `devices` with its fixed-form notes in `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md`, the device library section of `docs/IMPORTS.md`, `docs/user-manual/use-the-library.md` as one page with a worked example that the end-to-end test runs, the in-app guide, the changelog. Pull request 6 is the preparation of 2.2.0. | The first demo feedback of 2026-11-16 (`docs/ux/feedback-2026-11-16.md`, participant 2: *libraries*, and *if I select a device, for example PCDU00, I should see the pinouts of its connectors*; D-234). About three quarters of it exists: the device library file (D-165), the library folder and its window (D-175), the import record with the kind `devices` (format 2) and the Git readers that never start `git` (D-164, D-231); what is missing is one file per device, a pointer a project can hold, and the link from a project device to its file. Format 2 is frozen (D-230): a field would need a format 3, which 2.0.x opens read-only, so the link uses what the files already hold. A record keyed by the file name needs unique file names, and a device in two files cannot be told apart, so both are refused rather than one chosen. |
| D-241 | **No Windows code-signing certificate: the owner answered decision D4 of the plan towards 2.0 with no, and milestone M3 is closed** (D-19, D-213, D-232; plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` section 4 *A signed installer*, section 5.2 item 4 and section 7). On 2026-11-17 the owner answered decision D4 (the certificate and the identity for signing the Windows packages) with **no**. No Windows code-signing certificate and no signing service will be obtained. **Milestone M3** (*1.18.0: signed Windows installer and a build record*) is closed and will not be built. Its other parts are already done: the keyless Sigstore signatures of every package and of `SHA256SUMS`, the build provenance attestation, the SBOMs and `SHA256SUMS`, on every release since 1.17.1 (D-213; since 2.0.1 on the release page of D-232). **The Windows packages stay without an Authenticode signature.** On the first start SmartScreen shows *Windows protected your PC*; `docs/INSTALL.md` and the release notes already tell the user to choose **More info**, then **Run anyway**, so no text for users changes. **The optional signing hooks stay in place, switched off:** `HARNESS_SIGN_PFX` in `tools/build_windows.py`, and the steps *Signing certificate* and *Check the signature* of the `windows` job in `.github/workflows/release.yml`, which run only when the repository secret `WINDOWS_CERT_PFX_BASE64` exists (`docs/RELEASE.md`). D-19 already says "code signing hooks in the build script but disabled until a certificate is provided". No code, workflow or user text changes with this decision. | The owner's answer of 2026-11-17 to D4; the owner gave no reason. What the plan noted (section 4; `docs/RELEASE.md`): a certificate or a signing service costs money and needs an identity check done by the owner, while the Sigstore signatures and the attestation already show which workflow made each file, although Windows does not know them. Removing the hooks gains nothing, and they keep the choice open. |
| D-242 | **The design of 3.0.0: the harness plan, an `.ods` file with formulas in the users' layout, one per harness; the data the model lacks comes in format 3, released as 3.0.0; revisions become versions MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH, and the word *Revision* becomes *Version*** (D-230, D-235; design `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md`). **The owner's frame of 2026-11-17:** the output is an **`.ods` file that includes formulas**, in the users' layout (the template `harness_plan_template.ods` that the owner supplied: a cover, a change register, the harness sheet with the device side and the harness side of every connector, the sums of cable lengths, `CON_…` sheets and notes); the data the model lacks goes into **format 3**, released as **3.0.0**, because the model refuses unknown fields (`extra="forbid"`) and format 2 is frozen for 2.x (D-230); and it comes **after 2.2.0 and 2.3.0**. The plan is one more written file per harness, written only when asked for (`--harness-plan`, and a check box remembered per folder; one option for all harnesses, Q13, Q27); the build workbook stays the file to build a harness from, every other output stays, and the tool never reads the plan back. Format 3 adds the fields the plan needs: the pin level, the role value `signal_ground` (never derived from a pin name, Q7), the document data, part and source links and an optional colour code by pin number; Function is `Pin.signal` and Description is `Pin.function`, no new field (Q6). **The owner's answers of 2026-11-17** to the questions of the first version (Q1 to Q17) and to the follow-up questions (Q18 to Q33): **one plan file per harness**, `harnesses/<ID>/<ID>_plan.ods`, with its own Cover, change register, harness sheet, `CON_…` sheets and Notes, because users sometimes make a quick test harness that is custom and unique (Q1; the recommended one file per project was not taken); **the change register mirrors the change log**, nothing is typed into the plan (Q2); **the users' pigtail colour code ships as an example** that a project takes on purpose, never applied by itself; it carries the mark `"example": true` until a person takes the mark off, and is to be checked against the supplier's pigtail code (Q3, Q26); **harness revisions become versions MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH** (Q9; the recommended no change was not taken): a person chooses major, minor or patch when starting a new version; an old letter becomes `N.0.0` by its place in the letter order (`A` → `1.0.0`, `B` → `2.0.0`), and any other value is given by a person (Q18); a draft carries the version it will be released as, with no suffix (Q19); notes, not blocks: at review and release a note when a minor or patch version changes the pinout at a device (Q20), and a note on a major step that names the users' rule that an incompatible harness gets a new ID (Q21); the kind can be chosen again before the review (Q22); **the word becomes *Version* everywhere in 3.0.0** (Q23; the recommended keep *Revision* was not taken): on screen, on the drawings, in the build workbook, in the CSV headings and in the other written files, not only in the plan; the keys `revision` of the project files become `version` in format 3 (Q28); `export.json`, `log --json` and the expression field write `version` and keep `revision` beside it with the same value through 3.0.x (Q29); the command `revise` becomes `new-version` and keeps its old name, and the migration option is `migrate --set-version` (Q30); `system/revision_report.md` becomes `system/version_report.md` (Q31); the screen says *New version…*, *Versions* and *v1.1.0* (Q32); new files do not show the old letter of a migrated harness (Q33); **one format 3** holds the plan's fields, the versions and the format change of 2.3.0 if it needs one (the splices, D-243), in whichever release first carries format 3, and that release is 3.0.0 (Q10, Q24); the rest as recommended (Q4 to Q8, Q11 to Q14, Q16, Q25, Q27; *Conductor* is the wire part's specification text for now, Q15; the template's conditional formats are written as in the template, Q17). **It is a design only; nothing of it is built.** The design is in `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md`: section 0 the owner's frame and the answers in short, sections 5 and 5.8 the versions and the word, section 12 the pull requests, section 14 each question with the owner's answer. | The owner's frame and answers of 2026-11-17: the users plan their harnesses in a spreadsheet of their own layout, and the tool should write it with its formulas working. The model refuses unknown fields and format 2 is frozen (D-230), so the data the plan needs is a format change; one format 3 that holds the plan's fields, the versions and the splices gives the users one upgrade instead of several. Format 3 is frozen at its tag, so the rename of the keys to *version* is done with it or not in 3.x (Q28). |
| D-243 | **The design of 2.3.0: branching harnesses fit format 2, a splice does not; 2.3.0 is branching in format 2, and splices come with format 3 in 3.0.0** (D-235, D-237, D-242; plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md` section 3.2, milestone M6; design `docs/ait/DESIGN_2.3_BRANCHING.md`). **Branching fits format 2:** a harness with several connectors, branch points and segments with lengths are in the model already (`Harness.connectors`, `BranchPoint`, `Segment`), and Generate already makes such harnesses in the modes `per_unit_pair` and `per_zone_pair`; what 2.3.0 adds is a new segmentation choice, edits of the routing tree and new outputs, none of which needs a new field or a new value of a list in a project file. **A splice does not fit format 2:** the clean form, a connector of the harness with a new role `splice`, is a new value of `Role`, and a 2.x tool sets the whole harness aside; the format 2 form, an `inline` connector named by the existing `Splice` entry, makes every 2.x tool read the project as editable and write a test table that cannot be done on the built harness (both tried, section 4 of the design). **The owner's answers of 2026-11-17 to B1 to B9, all as recommended:** 2.3.0 is a minor release with branching in format 2 (M6b, without the breakout kind); splices (M6a) go into format 3 and so ship with 3.0.0 (D-242, Q10); backshells (M6c) come later (B1). Generate joins harnesses that share a device connector only in the new opt-in mode `per_connector_group` of `segmentation.mode`; the default stays `per_connector_pair`, and a person can also draw a branching harness (B2). A splice part is a library part of a new category `splice` in format 3, with `pin_count` the number of nodes; the tool ships no splice part (D-12), only an example part in the templates, marked as an example (B3). A segment is measured from the rear of a connector to the centre of a branch point, and between branch-point centres; a wire is the sum of its path, plus Generate's service loops for a generated wire; `import-lengths` is unchanged (B4). A shield group runs between two connectors: a group whose wires end on different far connectors is refused in the editor and warned in a file (`shield_across_branch`) (B5). Existing two-plug harnesses stay as they are: nothing joins on open or on Generate; generated drafts join when a person chooses `per_connector_group`, drawn ones by *Join into one harness*, one undo step; released ones never join (B6). Every harness with a branch point gets the *Routing* sheet in its build workbook, the *Segments* sheet in `<ID>.xlsx` and `segments.csv`, which changes the files of the example `flatsat` once (B7). Generate makes no splices at first; a person places them in a drawn harness (B8). The milestone names of the plan are kept: 2.3.0 is M6b, the splices M6a, the backshells M6c, and M5 stays the drawn-first harness (B9). The work comes in seven pull requests (section 10 of the design): 1 the design page; 2 Generate's `per_connector_group`; 3 `core/routing.py`; 4 the outputs; 5 the command line; 6 the editor; 7 the end-to-end test and the documents; then the preparation of 2.3.0. The splice work (S1 to S5) follows pull request 1 of the 3.0 design (format 3). **Pull request 2 of 2.3.0 (pull request 255) is built and open, to merge after the tag `v2.2.0`.** The design is in `docs/ait/DESIGN_2.3_BRANCHING.md`: section 0 the answer in short, section 4 the format question piece by piece, section 12 the questions with the owner's answers. | D-235 placed branching harnesses and splices at 2.3.0 (`docs/ux/feedback-2026-11-16.md`: *one connector to several connectors*). Until a harness can branch, interfaces from one device connector to different far connectors get one plug each on the shared connector (D-237, Q2). Branching needs no format change, so it can ship in 2.x, and the opt-in mode keeps every project generating the same; a splice in the format 2 form would make older tools write wrong test tables, so it waits for format 3, which a 2.x tool opens read-only. |

with the version replaced and every run of 12 or more hexadecimal characters masked, every project file, every written text file and every member of every `.xlsx` was equal, and only the sizes of the `.xlsx` files in `outputs/manifest.json` differed, as their compressed bytes do; `tools.output_hashes --check` says *identical*). **Upgrade, update the whole team at the same time:** a 2.1 tool ignores `device-libraries.json` and the link, and its *Save to library* writes `<ID>.json`, so beside an `<ID>.device.json` of 2.2.0 the device is in two files and 2.2.0 offers it from neither; devices that the editor of 2.1 added from a library have no record and are adopted (`update-device --adopt`, or *Take the library's version*). **Known limits (from D-240 and the design):** only the device of the library's ID and its copies follow the library (Q1; | D-247 | **New projects make one harness that branches (`per_connector_group`) by default; owner decision of 2026-11-18, for the minor release 2.4.0** (D-243; design `docs/ait/DESIGN_2.3_BRANCHING.md` section 5.1). The owner, on 2026-11-18: "That should be the default actually, because most connectors will be custom and bespoke." The case behind it: a 21-pin Micro-D on one device carries RS-422, RS-485 and CAN to three different devices, each with its own connector; the harness must have one plug on the Micro-D and split to the three connectors. In `per_connector_pair`, the mode every new project started in until now, Generate makes three cables with three plugs on the one Micro-D (the warning `box_connector_shared`); `per_connector_group` (2.3.0) makes one harness with one plug on the Micro-D, one branch point and four segments. **What changes:** every way a person starts a new project starts in `per_connector_group`: `new FOLDER` and `new FOLDER --template blank` (the bundled blank project, built by `tools/build_examples.py` from the new `samples.start_project`), the editor's *New project* with the empty project (`EditorController.new_project`), the scripting interface's `create_project` (its default template is `blank`, so it has no default of its own) and the empty project of `tools.usability_setup`. A project in `per_connector_pair` whose device connector has the plugs of several harnesses of Generate gets, in the editor after Generate, a notice once (*… has the plugs of several harnesses (W001, W002 and W003), and a connector takes one plug. This project groups interfaces per connector pair; a new project makes one harness that branches here instead.*) with the button *Make one harness that branches*: it sets the mode through *Settings > Project* (`MainWindow.set_segmentation`, `configcheck.plan_set_choice`, one undo step) and offers *Generate again*. The answer, the button or closing the notice, is remembered per project folder in the window's settings (QSettings, `layout/<hash>/branching_notice_done`), never in the project files; the condition is `branching.branch_offer` (the fix `mode` of `plug_fix` on a device connector, in `per_connector_pair` only). The Problems panel's fix *Use per_connector_group* stays. **What does not change:** a project keeps the mode it names, and every project a tool wrote names one, so no existing project is switched. The four examples with outputs keep their mode, so their outputs stay byte for byte: `first-steps`, `minimal-satellite` and `small-satellite` stay `per_connector_pair` and `flatsat` `per_zone_pair` (each is an example of a design whose written files, numbers snapshot and goldens are fixed; `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` say *identical*). **The default of the registry stays `per_connector_pair`** (`core/settings.py`; also the in-memory `default_configs`, which the loader puts in place of a missing `config/segmentation.json`, and `samples.new_project`, on which the examples and the tests build): it is what a file that names no mode, or a mode this version does not know, is read as, and the registry's rule is that a default is the behaviour before the setting existed. Changing it would change how such a project groups: the word-for-word copy of the segmentation of 2.2 (`tests/test_segmentation_compat_220.py`, a mode that is not known on every example, fixture, sample and random project) no longer gives the same groups, which the condition set for this change (every example and fixture groups and writes the same) rules out. The setting's description, the assumption `A-SEGMENT-MODE` and *Settings > Project* (*Per connector group (a new project's choice)*) say what a new project starts in. No format change, no model field; the scripting interface and the command line are unchanged. An older tool (2.2.x and before) reads `per_connector_group` as not known and uses `per_connector_pair` (D-243). |

the design of 3.0.0 names the number (D-242: the data the model lacks goes into format 3, released as 3.0.0), as the first release that writes format 2 was 2.0.0 (D-229). **Format 3 is frozen with the tag `v3.0.0`:** `SCHEMA_VERSION` 3, the layout of the project folder and the step from 2 to 3 do not change any more; a later change of the file format is a new format with its own step (as format 2 since D-230). **Upgrade:** a format 2 project is upgraded once, by `migrate` (look first with `--dry-run`) or the editor's question, with the backup `.migration-backup-v2/`, the record and the check; every revision letter becomes the version of its place (A is `1.0.0`), a revision that is not a letter is given with `migrate --set-version`; the step changes the model hash once, so the outputs are exported once afterwards; a 2.x tool opens a format 3 project read-only, so the whole team updates at the same time, the main Git branch first (a branch merged later in format 2 is named by `revision_not_version` and rewritten by `merge-fix`). For scripts, `export.json` and `log --json` keep `revision` beside `version`, `revise` stays as the older name of `new-version` and `harness.revision` of `harness.version`, through 3.0.x; the scripting interface only adds (`API_VERSION` stays 1). **No release candidate first** (our recommendation, as for D-238, D-239 and D-244 to D-248; unlike D-229, where 2.0.0rc1 came first): the step to format 3 has its proof (section 5.6 of the design: the record of the 2.x outputs, the comparison with the list of word changes, the checks of the upgrade before it writes) and the end-to-end walk of pull request 8 from a project of 2.x through `migrate` to an export with plans and splices and `verify --outputs`; the release workflow with the bundle ran for 2.0.1 to 2.4.0 and has not changed since. A fault found after the tag is fixed by 3.0.1, within format 3. An rc would also change the length of the version string, so the stored hashes and the numbers snapshot would be written again twice. The changelog section `3.0.0` holds every entry that was under *Unreleased*, each now naming its pull request (274, 277, 278, 279, 280, 281, 282, 283, 284, 285), and an intro in the form of 2.0.0's (a major release, format 3, how to upgrade, format 3 frozen from the tag, the known limits, what is not done); `docs/releases/3.0.0.md` covers what changed since 2.x for a reader of 2.x, with *For scripts*. `3.0.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` replace the stamp and say *identical*: pull request 274 wrote the baseline and the snapshot again for format 3 with `BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0 (`docs/changes.md`, 2026-11-18), so they are not written again and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0 (unlike D-230, where `2.0.0` was shorter than `2.0.0rc1`). The example projects, the fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the output goldens and the migration hashes are written again with the repository's tools (123 files); a script compared every regenerated file with the head of pull request 285 (the new text with `3.0.0` replaced by `2.4.0`, every run of 12 or more hexadecimal characters masked in both) and found them equal. No dependency changed since 2.4.0 (`pyproject.toml` apart from the version, `tools/third_party.py` and the packaging files are as at `85df2a9`; the `.ods` writer and the check of the plans use only the standard library, `zipfile`, `xml.etree` and `decimal` among it, and the other new code imports only the program, the standard library, PySide6 and pydantic, which were there), so the third-party inventory and the SBOMs of `3.0.0` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published 2.4.0 packages, whose SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.4.0_SHA256SUMS` (the `SHA256SUMS` of the release page, downloaded again, is the same file). They differ from the 2.4.0 files only by the version, and `tools.third_party check` passes for both packages against them. The App Center data takes the release 3.0.0 from the changelog heading and `docs/releases/3.0.0.md` at build time, so it needs no edit. **Before the tag:** this preparation merged (pull requests 285 and 286 are merged), and CI green on Linux and Windows on the head of its pull request and on the merge commit; the full test suite with the `slow` tests runs there (this preparation ran the affected test files locally, not the whole suite). **Not in 3.0.0, the owner's questions:** (a) whether the Help guide (F1, which opens the local `index.html` through the system's opener, `gui/main_window.py` `guide_flow`) and the *Open* of the Export window should also stop using the system's opener; the decision of 2026-11-18 named the links of the editor; (b) the row layout of a connector (for example a Micro-D with rows 1–11 and 12–21): it is not in format 3, which is frozen at the tag, so it needs a later format; (c) `SEARCH` and `ISNUMBER` for the search boxes of the plan, outside the closed list of formulas of section 7.2 (the search boxes are not written, and *Used in Row* finds no pin inside a joined `3 + 4`). **Not done:** Generate makes no splice and a splice is placed in the editor only; the tool never reads a plan back; nobody looked at the new screens on a real display, they were tested offscreen only; the LibreOffice comparison of the plans skips in CI; M6c (backshells) and the other milestones after it; the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision); the Windows packages on a real computer; the owner's sign-off; the release compliance record. **Published on 2026-11-19** as the latest full release, the annotated tag `v3.0.0` on `711f204` (the merge of this preparation, #287), made by the tag workflow of D-227 (run 44444461651, its tenth use) after CI on `master` was green on that commit with the slow tests (the tag workflow listed 16 other check runs: 13 passed, 3 were skipped); release workflow run 44444492708, first attempt: every job passed, with the keyless Sigstore signing, the release bundle and the build provenance attestation; seven files on the page, the eighth use of D-232; the offline-audit report in the bundle says PASS; the GitHub API names `v3.0.0` as the latest release. **Format 3 is frozen from this tag.** After the tag nothing else was merged on `master` (`master` is `711f204`, the commit of the tag), and no next release is planned yet. Not done: the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision), the Windows packages on a real computer, a look at the new screens on a real display, the owner's sign-off, the release compliance record; the owner's questions (a) to (c) above stay open (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 3.0.0*). | The owner decided on 2026-11-17 that the harness plan comes with format 3 as 3.0.0 (D-242), and that one format 3 holds the splices too (Q10; D-243 puts them into 3.0.0); every pull request of section 12 of the design is built. The owner told us to work on our own recommendations, and no release candidate is that recommendation. A release needs a new version, because the tag `v2.4.0` exists (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 1). The tag `v3.0.0` is made only after this change is merged and CI is green on the merge commit (step 15; by a person, or by the workflow of D-227). The owner has not signed it off (step 14). |
| D-250 | **The owner's three answers of 2026-11-19 to the open questions of 3.0.0: (1) the Help guide and the *Open* of an exported file stop using the system's opener, in the patch release 3.0.1, and *Reveal in folder* stays for folders only; (2) the row layout of a connector comes into the project as format 4, released as 4.0.0, with a design to follow (D-251); (3) `SEARCH` and `ISNUMBER` for the search boxes of the plan, in 3.1.0** (D-242, D-249; the questions (a) to (c) of D-249). **(1) Yes: Help and Open stop using the system's opener (this change, for 3.0.1).** In 3.0.0 the editor stopped opening the links of parts and sources in a browser (the owner, 2026-11-18: "Never open browser at all"; `gui/weblinks.py` copies them to the clipboard), but three uses of `QDesktopServices.openUrl` were left: F1 handed the local `index.html` to the system, which starts a browser; *Open* of the Export window's result list and of the Outputs list *Exported files* handed an exported file to the program the system uses for it; and *Reveal in folder* handed a folder to the file manager. The owner answered YES to: "Should the Help guide (F1) and **Open** in the Export window also stop using the system's default program (the system opener, usually a browser)?" What changes: **Help > User guide** (F1) shows the bundled guide in a window of the program (`gui/guide_window.py`, `GuideWindow`, a `Dialog` that only shows something: beside the editor, not modal, moving on its own as REQ-GUI-30 asks; one window, F1 again brings it to the front; the theme's colours for links, table lines and code); a link inside the guide (its table of contents, an anchor, a page of its own folder) is followed in that window, with **Back** (Alt+Left) and **Contents** (Alt+Home); a web link that leaves the guide is copied to the clipboard through `weblinks.copy_web_link` and the window and the status bar say so; any other link (`mailto:`, `ftp:`, `javascript:`, a file outside the guide's folder) is not followed and the window says so. *Open* is removed from the Export window's result list and from the Outputs list: **Copy path** (also Enter and a double-click) puts the full path of the selected file on the clipboard (a `copier` hook that tests replace, as in `gui/weblinks.py`) and says *Path copied to the clipboard*; the picture of a selected drawing stays. The rule of the former *Open* (`may_open`: only files the manifest lists, of a kind the program writes; C-7 of the audit of 1.8.0) is dropped with the button: a path on the clipboard runs nothing, so there is nothing for it to guard, and the person opens the file where they choose. **Reveal in folder is kept, for folders only:** *Reveal in folder*, *Reveal output folder*, *Open the folder after the export*, the *Reveal* of a copy and *Show folder* after *Export diagram* hand a folder to the system's file manager. A folder is what a person needs to reach the files once the editor opens none of them, the file manager shows a folder and runs nothing that is in it, and on Linux `xdg-open` of a directory starts the file manager, not a browser. Every such use goes through one module, `gui/folders.py` (`reveal_folder`), which hands over an existing local directory and refuses anything else: a file (which the system would open with a program, or run), a path that does not exist, a web address or any URL that is not a local folder. `gui/folders.py` is the only module of the program that names `QDesktopServices` (`tests/test_security.py`, with a test that it refuses a file and a URL that is not local); `main_window.py`, `export_dialog.py` and `outputs_files.py` no longer do. The offline audit's editor step checks that F1 shows the guide and asks the system to open no URL (`packaging/gui_entry.py --audit-run`), and the allow-list no longer lists the guide's URL (`tools/offline_audit_allow.json`). No format change, no change of a written file (`tools.output_hashes --check` says *identical*), the scripting interface is unchanged. **(2) Yes: the row layout of a connector goes into the project as format 4, released as 4.0.0.** The row layout of a connector (for example a Micro-D with rows 1–11 and 12–21) is not in format 3, which is frozen since the tag `v3.0.0` (D-249), so it needs a new format: format 4, with its own upgrade, released as the major version 4.0.0. The design follows in D-251; nothing of it is built here. **(3) Yes: `SEARCH` and `ISNUMBER` for the search boxes of the plan, in 3.1.0.** The search boxes of the users' template need `SEARCH` and `ISNUMBER`, which are outside the closed list of formulas of the design of the harness plan (`docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md` section 7: `IF`, `ISNA`, `VLOOKUP`, `MATCH`, `SUMIF` and `&`, `=`, `/`, `+`). The owner allows the two, for the minor release 3.1.0: the writer's list, the independent check's list and its evaluator take them; nothing of it is built here. | The owner answered the three questions on 2026-11-19. (1) is a fix of 3.0.0's own promise that the editor opens no browser (the guide was the one place left where it did), so it is a patch release, 3.0.1: no format change, no new function, no written file changes. Reveal stays because without *Open* a person still needs a way to the files, and a folder is the least that reaches them: showing it runs none of its files and it cannot be a web address, and the one module that hands it over refuses everything else. (2) changes the file format, which is always a major release (D-230, D-249). (3) adds function and keeps the format, so it is a minor release. |
| D-251 | **The design of 4.0.0: the row layout of a connector is stored in format 4, released as 4.0.0, a major release; on the connector part, shared by every connector of that part, and on a box connector as its own layout where it differs** (D-249, D-250; design `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.0_ROW_LAYOUT.md`). **The owner's decision of 2026-11-19 (D-250):** yes, the row layout of a connector (for example a Micro-D with the rows 1–11 and 12–21) is stored in the project; format 3 is frozen since the tag `v3.0.0` (D-249), so it needs format 4 and a major release. **Format 4:** one sparse key `row_layout` on a part of category `connector` and on a box connector: `rows`, a list of rows, each a list of pin names in order along the row (1 to 64 rows, every pin at most once, at most 1000 names, on a part at most its `pin_count`, on a connector only its own pins), and `rings` (left out while false) for a circular insert. A connector's layout is its own, else its part's; a cable connector's is its part's, else that of the box connector it plugs into. **The step from 3 to 4** (`_v3_to_v4`) writes the number only; the model hash, the findings and every written output stay as at 3.0.0, so the safety net of D-202 is the whole proof and the output-hash baseline stays *identical*; a 3.0.x tool opens a format 4 project read-only (its loader's check, no change to 3.0.x); a device library file is written as version 3 only when it holds a layout. **What reads it:** a *Pin row* column in the pinout tables and in `export-pinout`, read back by `import-pinout`; the harness plan's `CON_…` sheets and its near rows row by row; the rule `power-return-adjacent`, which on a connector with a layout counts the neighbours on the face (recommended; the owner's question L1); three checks of `check`; a command `pin-rows`, the editor's *Pin rows* and the scripting interface. Each output changes only for a project with a layout. Not in it: a drawing of the face, coordinates, Generate placing pins by the face (L2), layouts on the starter parts (L3). The design asks four owner questions, L1 to L4 (L4: whether 4.0.0 drops the older names that 3.0.x kept for scripts, D-242 Q29). **It is a design only; nothing of it is built**: the program still writes format 3, and no key, file or `SCHEMA_VERSION` changes with it. *Added 2026-11-20:* its pull requests 1 to 6 and R are built and merged (#294 to #300), and 4.0.0, which writes format 4, is prepared (D-254). *Added 2026-11-20, after the publication of 4.0.0:* 4.0.0 is published as the latest full release (annotated tag `v4.0.0` on `c0e0250`; `compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 4.0.0*). The design is in `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.0_ROW_LAYOUT.md`: section 3 where the layout lives, section 4 the keys and their rules, section 5 the step and its proof, section 8 the face rule, section 14 the pull requests, section 16 the questions. | The owner answered yes on 2026-11-19 (D-250). The face of a connector is a fact of its insert, so it belongs to the part and is entered once for every connector of that part; a box connector needs its own rows where a generic part, the ICD's pin names or an imported pinout differ. Format 4 is frozen at its tag, so it holds what a later rule reads (the rows and the ring mark) now; a step that writes only the number keeps every project and output as it was until a person enters a layout. |

(1) The build workbook and the harness plan get a sheet *Production*: one block per cable connector in natural ID order, splices last; one row per pin and wire, a run of spare pins on one row; the columns Pin, Signal, Wire, Goes to, Wire part, Colour, AWG, Length, Length from, Label at this end, Shield group with its end here and at the far end, and in the workbook Done (x). No dependency changed since 4.0.1 (`pyproject.toml` apart from the version, `tools/third_party.py` and the packaging files are as at `d45da91`; the new code imports only the program, the standard library and openpyxl, which was there), so the third-party inventory and the SBOMs of `4.1.0` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published 4.0.1 packages, whose SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_4.0.1_SHA256SUMS` (the `SHA256SUMS` of the release page, downloaded again, is the same file). They differ from the 4.0.1 files only by the version, and `tools.third_party check` passes for both packages against them. The App Center data takes the release 4.1.0 from the changelog heading and `docs/releases/4.1.0.md` at build time (`tools/appstream.py`), so it needs no edit. To keep `CLAUDE.md` under its size limit, the note on the publication of 2.3.1 moved word for word to `docs/developer/CLAUDE_HISTORY.md`. **Before the tag:** pull request 319 merged, before this preparation or with it, and CI green on Linux and Windows on the head of the pull request of this preparation and on the merge commit; the full test suite with the `slow` tests runs there (this preparation ran locally the tests of the version, the release notes, the examples, the fixtures, the documents, `CLAUDE.md`, the status pages, the inventories, the App Center data, the proof of format 3 and `tests/test_e2e_410.py` and those of the production sheet, in three runs with `pytest -n 4`: 1032 passed and 1 skipped (a test that needs a user who is not root), 375 passed and 285 passed; and `-m "slow and not perf"`: 52 passed; `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` say *identical*; `ruff` and `mypy` are clean; the whole suite was not run locally). **Not done:** nobody has printed the new sheets at a bench (the scale, the rows per page and the page breaks are worked out from the column widths and an estimate of the row heights; Excel's import of the page breaks of an `.ods` was not tried); a length reference per connector end, a connector depth, the pin a shield pigtail lands on and the backshell of a shield end need a new format (milestone E5 of the plan); the known limits of 4.0.0 stand; nobody looked at the screens on a real display (tested offscreen); the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision); the Windows packages on a real computer; the owner's sign-off; the release compliance record. Also on `master` since 4.0.1 and in this release: the plan for the engineer's feedback (pull request 307, `a08358e`), the design of 4.1.0 (pull request 309, `db91b21`) and the publication record of 4.0.1 (pull request 310, `03428fc`); documents only. **Published on 2026-11-21** as the latest full release, the annotated tag `v4.1.0` on `cb43f31` (the merge of this preparation, #320), made by the tag workflow of D-227 (run 44845357892, its fifteenth use) after CI on `master` was green on that commit (16 check runs: 13 succeeded, 3 skipped; the job `linux` of the offline audit passed on its one re-run); release workflow run 44845387838 succeeded; seven files on the page, the thirteenth use of D-232; neither a draft nor a pre-release. Format 4 stays frozen. After the tag nothing else was merged on `master` (`master` is `cb43f31`, the commit of the tag). Not done: the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision), the Windows packages on a real computer, the new sheets printed at a bench, a look at the screens on a real display, the owner's sign-off, the release compliance record (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 4.1.0*). | The feedback of an electrical engineer of 2026-11-20 asked first for production-ready output (milestone E1 of the plan), and the design (D-256) adds it to the written files without a new format, which a minor release carries, as 3.1.0 carried the search boxes of the plan (D-253). The owner told us to work on our own recommendations, and no release candidate is that recommendation. A release needs a new version, because the tag `v4.0.1` exists (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 1). | D-258 | **Release 4.2.0 is a minor release with four electrical design rules (milestone E2 of `docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`); no format change** (D-254, D-256; design `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.2_ELECTRICAL_CHECKS.md`). (1) `pair-untwisted` (warning): two wires of a signal pair, from the `pair` of the interface type or, for a wire without an interface, from the types of the signals at its device pins or from the endings of `config/naming.json` `pair_suffixes` (default `+ -`, `P N`, `H L`), that no twisted pair, shielded pair or quad holds together. (2) `signal-unshielded` (warning): a wire of a harness Generate did not make whose signal every interface type that has it shields, in no shield; a generated harness stays with the verifier's `shield_missing`. (3) `level-conflict` (error, may be accepted as a deviation): a net, or a pair of a connection without a harness, that joins two device pins whose levels are a pair of `config/segregation.json` `level_pairs_to_separate`, compared as whole words; the table is empty by default and the tool ships no level word (D-11), so the rule is silent until a project fills it, and says *not checked* where pins have levels. (4) `wire-direction` (error): a net of wires drawn by hand that joins two outputs, or only inputs, by the directions the review of connections reads; a generated wire stays with `direction-conflict`, a pair of a connection with `link-direction`. Each rule notes under `unchecked-config` what it could not judge, only where the project holds the data. (5) Generate makes the overall shield of an interface type of construction `single` that shields overall (it made none, and its own verifier refused the harness). **Written files:** `Rules run: 43` in every DRC report; findings only for projects with such harnesses or levels, none of the examples; the output goldens and the proof of 3.0.0 (its step 9) are renewed for the count. **Decided on 2026-11-21 with the preparation of 4.2.0 (D-259)**, as the design proposed it (section 13), on our recommendations to the owner questions C1 to C10 (section 14); no owner answer is recorded. Built by pull requests 323 (pull request 1 of section 15, the overall shield of a `single` type, C7), 324 (2, `pair-untwisted` and `signal-unshielded` with `naming.pair_suffixes`, not registered), 325 (3, `level-conflict` and `wire-direction` with `segregation.level_pairs_to_separate`, not registered), 326 (4, the four rules registered, 43 rules, step 9 of the proof of 3.0.0 and the goldens renewed for the count) and 327 (5, the end-to-end test `tests/test_e2e_420.py` and the documents). (1) A project's review checklist is the key `review_checklist` of `config/naming.json`: a list of points, each one line of 1 to 300 characters, each once, at most 100; empty by default, so the tool ships no point (D-11); `config DIR --review-checklist FILE` takes the list items of a file (the shipped template `design-review-checklist.md` gives 26), *Settings > Project* edits it; it is not read by generation and not frozen by a release, and its list hash names it. A new config file was refused: a 4.2.x tool quarantines it and stops. (2) The build workbook gets a sheet *Review* before *About*: the release entry of the version, the checklist with a tick column, every finding of the harness with its state (one function, `vcs/release.harness_findings`, which the release gate now uses too), the findings of the whole project, the values still open (placeholders, engineering values, the open checks of *Start here* with their count and first five items, example interface types, overrides), the sources (the ICD of each device connector it plugs into, each part with its approval and link) and how a number is explained; the verifier words it again (`core/outputs/verify_review.py`, `out_review`). (3) The editor gets a page *Review* per harness, the same blocks with *Show*, *Explain*, *Copy link*, the library state of each device and tick boxes kept on the computer only. (4) The ticks reach the release comment from the person releasing (the release dialog, `release --ticked POINTS`), as one note with the list hash and the points not ticked; no gate; not from a ticked sheet read back. (5) `explain DIR FINDING`. (6) *Settings > Project* shows the label format (design of 4.1.0, section 6.5). **Written files:** every build workbook changes once; the output-hash baseline, the numbers snapshot and the goldens are written again for this reason, and the proof of 3.0.0 gets its step 10; the comment of a release changes only in a project with a checklist. **Decided on 2026-11-21 with the preparation of 4.3.0 (D-261)**, as the design proposed it (section 14), on our recommendations to the owner questions R1 to R12 (section 15); no owner answer is recorded. The changelog section `4.3.0` holds every entry that was under *Unreleased*, each now naming its pull request (331 to 336), a *Records for 4.3.0* entry and an intro in the form of 4.2.0's (a minor release, what it adds, why minor, no format change, which written files change, how to upgrade, the known limits, what is not done); `docs/releases/4.3.0.md` (pull request 336) covers what changed since 4.2.x for a reader of 4.2.x, with *For scripts*. **Written files:** every `<ID>_build.xlsx` gets the sheet *Review* and its line on *Start here*, and the comment of a release gets the note in a project with a checklist or with `--ticked`. The output-hash baseline, the numbers snapshot and the output goldens were written again on purpose by pull request 334 for the build workbooks, with its reason in the changelog, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0. `4.3.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` replace the stamp and say *identical*: this preparation does not write them again. The example projects, the fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the output goldens and the migration hashes are written again with the repository's tools (131 files, the same set as for 4.2.0); a script compared every regenerated file with `4bf2073` (pull request 336 before its move onto `master`) and with `master` at `13adc6a` (the new text with `4.3.0` replaced by `4.2.0`, every run of 12 or more hexadecimal characters masked in both) and found them equal (11 by the version alone, 120 with the hashes masked); against `master`, only the guide and the README of the templates differ beyond that, and they come from pull request 336, not from this preparation. `tools.build_guide`, `gen_rule_docs`, `gen_cli_docs`, `gen_sdd_components`, `tools.trace` and `tools.build_prototype` change nothing. No dependency changed since 4.2.0 (`pyproject.toml` apart from the version, `tools/third_party.py` and the packaging files are as at `6848510`; the new code imports only the program, PySide6 and the standard library), so the third-party inventory and the SBOMs of `4.3.0` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published 4.2.0 packages, whose SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_4.2.0_SHA256SUMS` (the `SHA256SUMS` of the release page, downloaded again, is the same file). They differ from the 4.2.0 files only by the version, and `tools.third_party check` passes for both packages against them. The App Center data takes the release 4.3.0 from the changelog heading and `docs/releases/4.3.0.md` at build time (`tools/appstream.py`), so it needs no edit. To keep `CLAUDE.md` under its size limit, the note on the publication of 3.0.1 moved word for word to `docs/developer/CLAUDE_HISTORY.md` (the note on 3.0.0 moved with the publication record of 4.2.0). **Before the tag:** pull requests 335 and 336 merged (done), this preparation moved onto `master` after them with its generators run again (done), and CI green on Linux and Windows on the head of the pull request of this preparation and on the merge commit; the full test suite with the `slow` tests runs there (this preparation ran locally the tests of the version, the release notes, the examples, the fixtures, the documents, `CLAUDE.md`, the status pages, the inventories, the App Center data, the installers, the release check and the bundle, the legal texts, the proof of format 3, `tests/test_e2e_400.py` to `tests/test_e2e_430.py`, the goldens, the Review sheet, its check, the ticks, the Review page and `explain DIR FINDING`, in two runs with `pytest -n 4`: 854 passed and 1 skipped (a test that needs a user who is not root); 1154 passed; and `-m "slow and not perf"`: 52 passed; `tools.third_party check` passes for both packages; `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` say *identical*; `tools.release_notes check v4.3.0` passes; `ruff` and `mypy` are clean; the whole suite was not run locally). **Not done:** nobody at a review has used the page or the sheet; the owner has not answered R1 to R12; the ticks are not kept in the project (a field for them needs a new format, milestone E5 of the plan); a `single` type that shields per pair still gets no shield (R11); the known limits of 4.0.0, 4.1.0 and 4.2.0 stand; nobody looked at the screens on a real display (tested offscreen); the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision); the Windows packages on a real computer; the owner's sign-off; the release compliance record. Also on `master` since 4.2.0 and in this release: the publication record of 4.2.0 (pull request 329, `97a0aac`) and the design of 4.3.0 (pull request 330, `a5a053a`); documents only. **Published on 2026-11-22** as the latest full release, the annotated tag `v4.3.0` on `20bf8f1` (the merge of this preparation, #337), made by the tag workflow of D-227 (run 45045461615, its seventeenth use) after CI on `master` was green on that commit (16 check runs: 13 succeeded, 3 skipped), but not at the first attempt: the job `test (windows-latest)` was cancelled at its time limit of 90 minutes and passed on its one re-run (pull request 338, open and not part of 4.3.0, raises the limit to 150 minutes); release workflow run 45045481035 succeeded; seven files on the page, the fifteenth use of D-232; neither a draft nor a pre-release. Prepared and merged on 2026-11-21, tagged and published on 2026-11-22 (UTC); the changelog heading keeps 2026-11-21. Format 4 stays frozen. After the tag nothing else was merged on `master` (`master` is `20bf8f1`, the commit of the tag). Not done: the signature and the attestation verified from outside the workflow (skipped on the owner's decision), the Windows packages on a real computer, the Review page or sheet used at a review, a look at the screens on a real display, the owner's sign-off, the release compliance record (`compliance/SIGNOFF.md`, *Publication of 4.3.0*). | The feedback of an electrical engineer of 2026-11-20 asked for a review view (milestone E3 of the plan, point 9), and the design (D-260) adds it without a new format, which a minor release carries, as 4.2.0 carried the electrical checks (D-259). The owner told us to work on our own recommendations, and no release candidate is that recommendation. A release needs a new version, because the tag `v4.2.0` exists (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 1). The tag `v4.3.0` is made only after this change is merged and CI is green on the merge commit (step 15; by a person, or by the workflow of D-227). The owner has not signed it off (step 14). |
